# Milestone 50: the machine and the shortcomings

## The goal

Put the system of milestones 45 through 49 on the Lenovo, in one sitting of the owner's, and fix every previously known problem, every problem recorded during those milestones, and everything the machine shows. Milestones 45 through 49 prove their work in QEMU and do not touch the machine, so this milestone is the first run on the Lenovo of the new kernel, the disk registry, the AHCI interrupt path, the per-controller USB stack, the installer and the stick. The list of shortcomings is written as the work goes: an entry the day the problem is found, and nothing is deferred past this milestone. The manuals and the audit follow it, so that what they describe is the finished system and not one with known defects; an entry made during those two reopens the technical work before the document that describes it is accepted. The milestone is complete when the Lenovo runs the installed system with every device it had before, the twenty-session load has run on it for an hour, every entry is marked fixed with its proof, and the close below has run.

## What the tree settles before this plan

* The stick behind the SuperSpeed hub stopped answering and its removal panicked the kernel once; the record leaves the reset-and-removal sequence with stale completions open (`dev-log/43-machine/notes.md:291`). A healthy stick pulled is not the same test.
* Milestone 44 needed four full runs to get one clean, and records the failures as pooled execution (`dev-log/44-krffsmount/notes.md:412`). A successful rerun does not explain the earlier failure. Diagnose the kernel, harness and scheduling before closing the entry.
* The Lenovo's disk is 488397168 sectors of 512 bytes on SATA, internal, and does not leave its bay (`dev-log/43-machine/hardware.md:43`). It has the sealed layout: a 64 MiB boot partition, the 500 MiB KRFFS fallback root, the 64 MiB recovery partition, and a fourth entry of 2 GiB with the 1 GiB ext2 root in it; the rest is unused. It has one xHCI controller and an EHCI the kernel names (`hardware.md:41`).
* Milestone 43's recovery stick, with its older kernel and its own host key, boots the machine today (`hardware.md:121`). That recovery repairs every ext2 partition it finds during its own boot (`utils/init.c:200`, the call at `:243`), has `reflash` and no `rach2-recover`, and is not changed by this milestone.
* The Lenovo's USB sockets are the right black, the left black and the left blue, with the hub on the blue one (`hardware.md:131`).
* The metal acceptance writes a file to the first USB disk it finds with a FAT or exFAT volume (`tests/system/accept/metal_accept.sh:500`, the write at `:527`). It would write to a recovery stick's image half.
* The hardware rule in `CLAUDE.md`: a write that cannot be undone by rebuilding needs the owner's approval at the time, with the device shown; before a write to the Lenovo's disk that is not a kernel through `bootctl`, a stick with recovery is booted and shown to work, every sector about to be overwritten is saved on the host and on that stick, and recovery is the saved boot default until the write is verified. That was the rule when this plan was written; the owner replaced it on 2026-09-20 with a standing authorization for the flash drive and the Lenovo's disks, and on 2026-09-24 made both a sandbox, rewritten as the work needs with no backup taken for their safety.
* Milestone 49's installer prints the device, asks for its name typed back, and prepares the image with recovery as its saved default. Its save set is the image's extent and the block ranges the grow writes into the groups it adds, computed by milestone 47's tool from the new image's superblock, the root's offset on the target and the target's size. Its phase 5 rehearses this milestone's sitting in QEMU on a disk of the Lenovo's size and layout, with the restore made through `rach2-recover` and through the older recovery's `reflash`.
* Milestone 24's boot switches skip individual device probes, milestone 47 keeps a boot option for polled AHCI, milestone 46's kernel prints its memory map at boot, and netconsole sends the kernel log as frames the host captures before SSH is up.

## Entries

An entry has: a date; the milestone that found it; its severity, with a loss of data or a panic first; how to reproduce it; the contract it breaks, with file and line; what it depends on; the test that will show it fixed; and, once done, the commit and the proof. A file and line in an entry are the tree's on the day the entry names, unless the entry says otherwise; the code has moved since, and the function or the message named beside a line is what finds it now. The two above are the first entries, numbered 1 and 2, and the list is worked in order of severity and dependency, not of the code it touches. What the machine shows in the sitting is entered the same way and worked in phase 4.

An entry is fixed when its test passes in QEMU and its review has run. A fix made after the first sitting reached the Lenovo at the second, and what phases 6 and 7 add is deployed and proved there before this milestone closes, nothing of it waiting for milestone 52, and the entry says so; a kernel fix may go on earlier through `bootctl` over SSH when the machine is on and the owner approves that write, and this is never required. The documents may be written once every entry is fixed. A fix without which the machine does not run at all calls the owner again with the same preparation as the sitting, and the report says that it did.

1. The stopped stick: reproduce the never-answering reset and the removal with completions still pending, in QEMU with a stick that stops answering, and fix what the kernel does with the stale completions. Closed in milestone 50 with what milestone 43's phase 7 built and what the rows since prove: a transfer event for a slot no longer in use is dropped before it can advance a slot handed to the next device (`rach2/sys/usb/xhci.c`, the transfer event's check of the slot), `tests/run usbdisk` pulls a stick while a reader keeps a command queued on it and requires the read to answer `EIO`, the two sticks beside it to be unharmed and the pulled one reclaimed by name, and the storage host test drives a class reset that never answers and requires the stick handed back to the core; a mutation of each was caught when it was written. QEMU cannot make a stick stop answering its class reset, so the never-answering reset is proved in the model and not in the emulator, and the machine's own record of the panic is what the sitting's capture takes if it recurs.
2. The pooled runs: find why a suite that passes alone fails in the pool, which is milestone 52's scheduling work brought forward as far as the cause requires. A further instance from milestone 45's close (2026-09-06): in the second full set, `tests/run selfbuild-ext2` failed in the pool with `the built kernel was never set aside for extraction`; its console log shows the line the harness typed, `cp build/rach2/kernel ...`, arriving at the guest's shell as `uild/rach2/kernel ...`, its first characters lost while the machine was still busy with the build. Console input dropped under load is a kernel matter, the serial receive path, and not the harness's; the suite passed alone afterwards. A third instance from milestone 46's close (2026-09-06): `musl/pwauthmusl` in `default-e1000` timed out waiting ten seconds for the session leader's report after a continue, twice in pooled sets, and passed alone and in `default` in the same sets. A fourth instance from milestone 49's close (2026-09-09): in the pool of twenty, `install` waited its 900 s for the trial boot's commit and did not see it, and `installer` and `installer-repair` each lost one step to its deadline, the install onto the second disk at 420 s a step and the restore through the menu at 900 s, while every row after the lost step passed, so the work was done late and not wrongly. The three took 1199, 1519 and 1331 s where they take 900, 826 and 544 alone; the rows hash and write a 138 MiB image at the emulator's rate, so they are the ones that go over first; run again as a set of three, they passed in 787, 815 and 559 s. Worked in milestone 50 (`e82fc0c`): the three suites' deadlines are doubled, since their work was done and only its limit was too tight for the pool, and the serial instance was tried without load, a line typed while a foreground job ran on os-real arrived whole and ran, so the type-ahead path holds and the loss belongs to the load; the pooled sets of phase 2 are where the rule is judged, and a fifth instance reopens this entry. The fifth instance came on 2026-09-10, in phase 2d: `tests/run selfbuild` alone, with nothing else on the host, failed `musl/pwauthmusl`'s stop-and-continue row on the machine-built kernel with the same line, `the session leader did not report what the continue left behind`, while `tests/run default` under ten spinning host processes had passed that row in phase 2c and the console's counters (entry 29) do not cover a pseudo-terminal. So this instance is neither the pool's load nor COM1: it is the row itself, on the tcc-built kernel or on any, and it is to be reproduced by running the probe alone in a loop on both kernels and read from the pty's side before phase 2f's set; reopened. The sixth instance came on 2026-09-10 in phase 2f's set at twenty: `install` lost the first pass's commit, the guest's build of the kernel ending as the step's 1800 s deadline did, and `installer` lost its raw-write row by one second of its 300 s; install passed alone in 778 s and installer in 1662 s, its raw-write row in 39 s. Phase 2g's set runs at twelve. The seventh instance, 2026-09-11, in phase 2g's set at twelve: `install` lost the second pass's build to the same 1800 s, and `menu` lost its closing line to a wait that ended at the line's start; `install` runs alone after the pool now (`0aee59ef`), the menu row waits for a token after its line, and the width stays twenty, since the set at twelve took 57 min against 61 and failed four suites against four. Fixed when phase 2h's rule holds: every deadline in a pooled suite scaled from the emulator's measured rate, a set in which no suite fails for a deadline it meets alone, and the false-rate mutation failing at once. The rule is built (`9507627e`, `80b9d696`), phase 2h item 2: the runner calibrates a set at its pool's width and once more at width 1, passes the factor in named variables, and every completion bound in the runners and the suites is multiplied by it, with the inventory of what is scaled and what is kept in `notes.md`; the false-rate mutation and five others each failed the runner's selftest. Whether a set now passes with no suite failing for a deadline it meets alone is item 4's two sets to show. They passed at `f52de94d` on 2026-09-11 and 2026-09-12, 89 suites each, at factors of 7.0 and 6.8 (`notes.md:109`). The owner replaced the calibration on 2026-09-15 (`notes.md:543`): `tests/run` no longer calibrates, a completion bound keeps its written seconds unless `RACH2_TIMEOUT_FACTOR` scales it, from 1 through 64 (`tests/run:765-778`), and a suite silent for `RACH2_IDLE_TIMEOUT` fails by name. This entry is closed. The calibration this entry's rule was built on was removed on 2026-09-15 at `962f09a4`, when the owner replaced the rule (`notes.md`): `tests/run` no longer calibrates and its selftest requires that it never does, a completion bound uses its written seconds unless `RACH2_TIMEOUT_FACTOR` scales it, and the suites assert on what the kernel counts. The two consecutive sets that closed phase 2h ran at `f52de94d` (`notes.md`), before that change.
3. The acceptance's stick: the metal acceptance takes the stick it writes by identity from its arguments and refuses a stick that contains a Rach2 recovery ISO; proved in QEMU with three sticks plugged, two of them recovery sticks, and by the mutation that drops the refusal. Fixed in milestone 50 (`f95bae9`): `metal_accept.sh HOST [PORT] [STICK-SERIAL]` takes the stick by the serial `/proc/disk` reports, examines every flash drive, refuses one whose table has an entry of type 17, the ISO half of a Rach2 recovery stick, and mounts the chosen stick's volume by its node and not by a label another stick could carry; the storage driver asks a stick for INQUIRY's unit serial page so a USB disk has a serial at all. `tests/run metal` plugs three sticks, two of them with the recovery entry and the same label, and requires the named one taken and both refused; with the refusal dropped the row fails.
4. The clean tree's preparation (2026-09-06, milestone 45; severity: a wrong result, no data lost): with `build/` removed, `tests/run host` stops in `prepare-all` with `No rule to make target build/utils/ping, needed by build/images/os-real.krffs`. The image rules take the products a manifest stages as ordinary prerequisites (`Makefile:435`, through `krffs/manifest_deps.sh`), and no rule makes such a file on its own: the component that builds it is an order-only prerequisite of the same rule, which make does not consult for a missing ordinary one. On a tree where the file exists the rule works, which is why `make` and then `tests/run all` passes and the runner alone does not. It breaks the runner's claim that `prepare-all` builds the product from whatever state the tree is in (`tests/system/suites.mk:9`). Depends on nothing. Fixed when `rm -rf build && tests/run host` passes, and when a mutation that removes the component from the rule's order-only list fails it again. Milestone 45's runner builds the components in a make of their own before the images (`7eb1108`), so the runner's claim holds again: on 2026-09-06 `tests/run all` from a removed `build/` built everything and ran. The rule itself is entry 5's. Fixed in milestone 50 with entry 5 (`80d5695`): `manifest_deps.sh` prints no path under `build/` that is not there yet, so the clean tree's image rule has no prerequisite without a rule; the set of 2026-09-10 ran from `rm -rf build` through its preparation, and `tests/run imagerace` holds the rebuild route entry 5's mutation covers.
5. The clean tree's `make -j` (2026-09-06, milestone 45; severity: a wrong result, no data lost): with `build/` removed, `make -j` stops in its first second with `No rule to make target build/tests, needed by build/images/test.krffs`, while `make` without `-j` builds everything, because `all` names the components before the images and a serial make meets them in that order. The cause is entry 4's: `krffs/manifest_deps.sh` prints every staged path as an ordinary prerequisite of the image rule (`Makefile:416` for the test image) whether or not the file exists, and make has no rule for a file that is not there. It breaks the Readme's claim that `make` builds everything, for the `-j` form. Depends on nothing. Fixed when `rm -rf build && make -j` passes, and when a staged program rebuilt on a built tree still restages its image, since the fix must not lose entry 4's rebuild; a mutation that touches a probe and finds the test image unchanged fails it. Seen again on 2026-09-07 in milestone 47's close, and the serial form fails now as well: `rm -rf build && make` stops at `No rule to make target build/tests, needed by build/images/test.krffs`, since the test manifest stages `build/tests` as an `exec-dir` and the directory is an ordinary prerequisite with no rule. The close was run through `rm -rf build && tests/run all`, whose `prepare-all` builds the components in a make of their own first, as milestone 46's close was. Fixed in milestone 50 (`80d5695`, on milestone 49's `72c9b00`): `manifest_deps.sh` prints the files a staged directory holds and nothing for one that is not there yet, so a clean tree's image rule has no prerequisite without a rule; `tests/run imagerace` touches a staged probe and requires the test image out of date, which holds by two routes, the manifest's files and the image's own `.d` file, and fails only with both dropped. The clean `rm -rf build && make -j` at phase 2's clean build on 2026-09-10 stopped at once on the same fault's second form, a `file` line naming `build/utils/echo` printed as a prerequisite before `utils` had built it; `manifest_deps.sh` now leaves out a `file` or `tree` path under `build/` that is not there yet, since the image rule's order-only prerequisites build it first and on a built tree it exists and is printed, so entry 4's restaging holds; `rm -rf build && make -j8` then passed in 30 s and `tests/run imagerace` after it.
6. A process always inside a system call starves the rest (2026-09-06, milestone 46; severity: the machine stops answering, no data lost): a process that writes a file on a polled disk in a loop, `loadprobe Wn` on a FAT32 or KRFFS volume of the IDE disk in QEMU, keeps the processor for as long as it runs. The timer preempts a process in user mode only, and the kernel's exit from a system call never reschedules (`rach2/sys/isr.c`, the `_irq_router` comment), so a process that returns to user mode for an instruction between two calls is preempted only when the tick lands in that instruction; a parent sleeping twenty seconds beside it never runs, and the console does not answer. The lag device hides it, since each write sleeps in a request. It breaks the scheduler Readme's claim of round-robin scheduling among the runnable. Depends on nothing; milestone 47's SATA through the request layer makes the writer sleep but does not change the rule. Fixed when a reschedule requested by the tick is taken at the system call's exit and `tests/run load` passes with its writers' ten-millisecond pause removed; a mutation that drops the exit-path reschedule fails it. Fixed in milestone 50 (`69b034d`): `sched_preempt` keeps the tick's request on a process inside a system call and `sched_take_preempt` takes it at the call's exit, with interrupts off and the trapframe whole; the load probe's writers have no pause, and `tests/run load` adds a boot on the polled disk with one writer, no sync and the console reader, which passed only with the exit-path switch and failed with it removed.
7. The raw windows' one staging buffer (2026-09-06, milestone 47; severity: a slow result, no data lost; fixed there): every raw transfer staged through one 32 KiB buffer in `rach2/sys/fs/devfs.c`, taken in turn, so two processes reading two disks through their nodes ran one after the other, each holding the buffer across its sleep on its device; with the AHCI disks on their interrupts the queue suite's fast reader waited the slow disk's whole read. Fixed in milestone 47's driver phase: a transfer stages through pages of its own from the heap and the static buffer is the fallback when the heap has none; `tests/run queue` shows it, the fast reader finishing in tens of milliseconds beside a throttled disk.
8. A partition view kept its backing's flags from when it was cut (2026-09-06, milestone 47; severity: a wrong result, no data lost; fixed there): `block_partition_init` copied `flags` and `submit` at the cut (`rach2/sys/block/block.c`), so a view cut at boot over a polled disk kept `BLOCK_FAULT_SAFE` after the disk went over to its interrupts and every transfer through the view stayed polled, holding the processor. Fixed in milestone 47's driver phase: `block_flags` reads the backing's flags when asked and a view always forwards `submit`; `tests/run queue` shows it.
9. The sparse_super rule took products for powers (2026-09-06, milestone 47; severity: a wrong result, no data lost; fixed there): `has_super` in `rach2/sys/fs/ext2.c` divided a group number by 3, 5 and 7 in turn and called the group a backup when 1 was left, so group 15, 21 and 35 were taken to hold a superblock copy that mke2fs never wrote there, and the mount's descriptor check refused every ext2 volume of sixteen groups or more, 2 GiB at 4 KiB blocks, with `refused: group descriptor`; the same rule was in `tools/inspect/setidcheck.c`. The sealed root is eight groups, which is why nothing had shown it. It broke the ext2 Readme's claim that a volume the profile allows mounts. Fixed in milestone 47's file phase, since the 5 GiB file row cannot run without it: a group is a backup when it is a power of one of the three bases alone; `tests/run large` mounts a 6 GiB volume and `tests/run grow` a 931 GiB one.
10. The disk path moved one page per request (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: a slow result, no data lost; fixed there, the cache's remainder superseded by entry 25): a sleeping device took one request of at most a page (`rach2/sys/block/request.c`, `req_run_one`), the buffer cache wrote and filled one 4 KiB block per request, each a sleep until the completion, and found a block by walking its whole list, thousands of buffers on a machine with memory, so a file written through ext2 on the AHCI disk in QEMU went at 4 MiB/s and through exFAT at 1 MiB/s, and `tests/run large` at 5 GiB took hours. It broke no stated contract; it made the full tier take hours. Fixed in milestone 47's file phase, since every later run and sitting paid for it: a request moves up to the device's `max_sectors`, 64 on AHCI, in heap pages the command table describes page by page; the cache's sync writes a run of adjacent dirty blocks in one request through a staging buffer; the cache finds a block through an index by device and block; an ext2 write marks its bitmap bytes, descriptors and pointer slots dirty and writes them at its end, the bitmaps and descriptors first, instead of writing each through per block. Measured after: ext2 8 MiB/s, exFAT 10 MiB/s on the same 1 GiB write. What remains, for a later milestone: the cache still walks its list once for the hold rule and once for the victim on every take, and a read fills one block at a time; the polled IDE channel's port I/O stays at 2 MiB/s. Closed in milestone 50: superseded by entry 25, which replaced the cache's list walks and the page-at-a-time fill; nothing of this entry remains open.
11. A disk with 4096-byte sectors is refused, not served (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: a refusal by name, no data lost): the block layer, the partition parser, the raw windows and the file systems count in 512-byte sectors, so the IDE and AHCI drivers refuse a drive whose IDENTIFY word 106 says its logical sector is not 512 bytes, by name, and publish no node; `tests/run bus` shows both refusals. Milestone 47's plan allows the restriction while it is reported and not hidden. It breaks the plan's aim that the sector size is the disk's. Depends on nothing. QEMU cannot present such a drive to either driver: `ide-hd`, which serves both the IDE channels and the AHCI ports, takes `logical_block_size=4096` only with a discard granularity that is a multiple of it, and then refuses any granularity but 512, so the row this milestone wrote was removed and the refusal is unproved in QEMU. Fixed when a machine that can present a 4096-byte-sector disk mounts and checks one, and the MBR limit for that sector size is documented; a mutation that keeps a 512-byte sector count fails it. The refusal itself is `rach2/sys/block/ata.c` and `ahci.c`, both reading IDENTIFY word 106. Kept in milestone 50 as a restriction, called one in the block Readme; `ahci_hosttest` feeds IDENTIFY words with a 4096-byte logical sector, word 106 valid with bit 12 set and words 117 and 118 giving 2048 words a sector, and requires the refusal by name, and the same words with bit 12 clear taken with the count read; with the check dropped the row fails. The ATA driver's check is the same two lines on the same word. The ATA driver's refusal has its row as well (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close): `identify_rows` in `tests/host/ata_hosttest.c` answer IDENTIFY with words a row sets, word 106 = 0x5000 with words 117 and 118 giving 2048 words refused with the record saying the logical sector is not 512 bytes, 0x4000 taken with the 48-bit count of words 100 to 103, and 0x1000, bit 12 without the valid bit 14, taken; `sh tests/host/ata_identify_mutate.sh` drops the check and then reads bit 12 without bit 14, and each fails its row. The restriction stays, as milestone 47's plan allows while it is reported: the block Readme calls it one, and the Lenovo's disk has 512-byte sectors.
12. The kernel does not boot on 8 MiB (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: a crash at boot, no data lost): the recovery kernel and image on a QEMU machine with `-m 8` stop in `panic: stopped: Unrecoverable error: page fault while in kernel mode` before the login; at 16 MiB they boot and grow a root. A machine too small should be refused by name in the memory manager's first report, not faulted in. It breaks no stated contract; the smallest memory the Readme names is what `tests/run memory` runs. Depends on nothing. Fixed when a boot at 8 MiB prints the refusal and stops; a mutation that removes the check faults again. Fixed in milestone 50 (`40f6465`): the memory report refuses a machine with less than `FRAME_MIN_MANAGED_KIB`, 1 MiB, left after the kernel's reservations, and stops; `tests/run memory` boots the recovery pair at 8 MiB and requires the refusal with no fault, and with the check removed the row saw the fault again. The recovery image on 10 MiB, 1408 KiB managed, boots; on 9 MiB, 384 KiB, the kernel reached `/sbin/init` and refused it by name, which is above the floor and not a fault.
13. An exFAT append walked the chain from its first cluster (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: a slow result, no data lost; fixed there): `ex_write` cleared the node's chain hint after every write (`rach2/sys/fs/exfat.c`), so the next append's walk to the tail started at the first cluster, one FAT read per cluster of the file, and a file written in 1 MiB pieces took longer with each piece: the fourth gibibyte of a 5 GiB write took more than thirty minutes where the first took fifteen, and the harness cut it. It broke the exFAT Readme's claim of a bounded walk per operation in effect, since the bound was the file's length. Fixed in milestone 47's file phase, since the 5 GiB row cannot run without it: the hint is kept across an append, which changes no cluster the chain had; `tests/run large` at `RACH2_LARGE_MIB=5120` shows it.
14. A request slow past five seconds fails the mount (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: a wrong result, no data lost): a request that a sleeping device has not answered in five seconds is quarantined and, with every slot of the device past its deadline, the device is declared stalled (`rach2/sys/block/request.c`, `REQ_DEFAULT_DEADLINE`), the write fails with `EIO`, the buffer stays dirty and the file system turns the mount read-only: `block: request deadline: 1 slots quarantined`, `block: device stalled`, `vfs: read-only: /big`. In QEMU on a host busy with a second machine's disk the answer came late, not never, and the volume was lost to the run; a disk that answers late is not a disk that went away. It breaks the block Readme's claim that a slow device is waited for. Depends on nothing. Milestone 47 raised the deadline from five seconds to thirty and then, on 2026-09-07, to two minutes, after a full set from a clean tree lost the `large` suite's mount to a write unanswered for thirty-one seconds while twenty machines ran on one host; the lag device keeps its own five seconds, which is what tests a device that never answers. That is a wider margin, not a fix. Fixed when a late completion of a quarantined request is taken as the answer and the mount keeps writing, with the stall declared only after a bounded number of deadlines in a row; `tests/run queue` with a disk throttled below one request in five seconds shows it, and a mutation that fails the request at the first deadline fails the row. Fixed in milestone 50 (`839c70d`): the deadline is a period, thirty seconds, and a request is waited for through four of them in a row before it is given up, so a request answered after a deadline is answered and its caller takes the answer; the lag device keeps one period of five seconds, which is what its rows test; `rach2.blockdeadline=S` sets the period for a row. `tests/run queue` adds an ext2 volume on a disk throttled to 16 KiB a second under a period of one second: a 32 KiB request of the write's end passes its deadline and is answered after it, the answer is taken, the file reads back and the mount stays writable; with the request given up at the first deadline the device stalled and the mount went read-only.
15. A KRFFS node could not be made where its id fell astride two units (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: a wrong result, no data lost; fixed there): `krffs_put` refuses a field whose changed bytes reach the medium in two units, the rule that keeps a reader from seeing half of a live field, and applied it to the id and the parent of a node being created, which no path reaches until its name is written last; so a create whose free node happened to place the 64-bit id across a 4 KiB boundary failed with `refused: a field at N would reach the medium in two pieces`, and `tests/run mount` failed its dirty row once the staged probes' sizes moved the volume's free space there. Fixed in milestone 47's file phase: the id and the parent of a node being made, and of a copy being filled before it is linked, are written without the rule. What remains: a rename in place changes a live node's id under the rule and can be refused by the same layout; `tests/run krffsmount` shows the create. The row the entry lacked is in `tests/host/krffs_hosttest.c` (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close): `straddle_create_rows` grows `/spacer` to 3375 bytes so that the next node's id lies at 4092, four bytes on each side of the 4096 line, sets `mount.grain` to 4096, and requires `krffs_create_at("/across")` to make the node with its whole id on the medium; `sh tests/host/krffs_id_mutate.sh` writes the new id through `krffs_put` again (fault `new-id-rule`) and the row fails. `krffs_mkdir_at` still wrote a directory's id and parent under the rule; that is entry 170.
16. The network does not come up on a warm restart (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: the machine is unreachable after a reboot, no data lost): in `tests/run sealed` the first boot of the sealed disk gets its lease and answers SSH, and the second boot, made by `sync; reboot -f` sent over SSH inside the same QEMU, prints `init: network: ifup -a did not configure the interface` with nothing at all from `udhcpc`, so the machine comes back to its login prompt and never answers SSH again; the console log of the run of 2026-09-07 shows `udhcpc: lease of 192.168.0.15 obtained` on the first boot and no `udhcpc` line on the second. The same reboot done by hand at the console, with the same network device, does get its lease, so what differs is the reboot arriving over SSH while dropbear holds the connection. `tests/run drill`, `drill-ext2` and `metal` also failed in the same set and also reboot; whether they share this cause is unverified. It breaks the Readme's claim that the installed machine is reached over SSH after a reboot. Depends on nothing. Fixed when `tests/run sealed` passes its second and third boots; a mutation that skips the network device's reset on a warm start fails it. On 2026-09-07, in the closing set from a clean tree and after the AHCI acknowledge fix, `tests/run sealed` passed every boot, including the kernel installed over SSH and the reboot after it. Whether that fix cured this or the failure is intermittent is unverified, so the entry stays open until a row reproduces it or a run of sets passes without one. Closed in milestone 50: no row reproduced it and the sets since passed without one. `tests/run sealed`, whose second and third boots are the warm restarts, passed in milestone 47's closing set of 2026-09-07, in both of milestone 48's full sets of 2026-09-08, in milestone 49's closing set of 2026-09-09 (521 s), and in the set before it that a concurrent build voided, where its one failure was a disk rewritten under the machine and not the network. The fix was milestone 47's AHCI acknowledge; the entry is left with that as its cause. A further instance from milestone 50's phase 2 (2026-09-10): in the full set after the clean build, `tests/run selfbuild-ext2` failed in the pool on `musl/pwauthmusl`, the probe that times the credential lock's acquire against its retry budget, with the probe's own status 1 and no console kept; alone it passed in 369 s with `PWAUTHMUSL OK`, and `selfbuild` on KRFFS passed in the same pool. The shape is a timed assertion losing to the pool's load, the class this entry names; `selfbuild.sh` now keeps the probe boot's console as `DISK.probes.log`, so the next instance says what the probe saw. Closed in milestone 50 as inferred: the AHCI acknowledge of milestone 47 is the cause the record keeps, since every set since passed the warm restarts and no row reproduces the fault, so no mutation can be made of it.

17. The test preflight checks tool names but not host access (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: verification fails after a full build, no data lost): in a restricted host session, `rm -rf build && tests/run all` passes the tool checks in `tests/run` and builds the product, then `large` fails before guest boot because `hdiutil attach` cannot attach its fixture. `exfat_hosttest` cannot create its external volume, and `exfat` cannot attach its written volume for the host checker. Reproduce the creation failure with `hdiutil create -size 16m -layout NONE -fs ExFAT -volname RACH2CHECK /tmp/rach2-exfat-check.img`; the observed error was `Device not configured`. In the same session, binding a TCP or UDP socket, including on loopback, returns `Operation not permitted`, and QEMU reports failed forwarding rules or a failed Unix monitor-socket bind. `tests/run large` and `tests/run netif` repeat their failures alone. `sealed` fails before its guest boots, so that result says nothing about entry 16's warm restart. This breaks the verification rule that conditions which will stop a full set are checked before it starts; executable discovery alone does not establish that the host can run the tests. Depends on a host policy allowing DiskImages operations and TCP, UDP and Unix socket binds. Fixed when preflight reports an unavailable capability before building, and the full set passes on a host that permits those operations. A preflight test with a formatter that fails and a denied socket bind must refuse before invoking the build; a mutation that checks only executable presence must fail that test. Fixed in milestone 50 (`f6b1bcf`): `tools/setup/hostcheck.c`, compiled by `tests/run` before anything is built, binds a TCP, a UDP and a Unix socket, and creates, attaches and detaches a disk image with `hdiutil` when a chosen suite names it; a refusal names the operation and stops the run. `tests/run runner` drives a checker that refuses and an `hdiutil` that fails and requires the run stopped before its build; with the check dropped both rows fail.

18. A pointer block evicted under pressure can reach the disk before its bitmap (2026-09-07, milestone 47; severity: a wrong result after a power cut, no data lost while running): an ext2 write opens a batch and defers its pointer writes to `batch_end`, which writes the bitmaps and descriptors first (`rach2/sys/fs/ext2.c`, `meta_deferred` and `batch_end`), but the buffer cache may choose one of those deferred pointer buffers as a victim and write it out first (`rach2/sys/fs/bcache.c`, the victim scan and `write_buffer`). With a sixteen-buffer cache, write thirty-two blocks of a sparse file from logical block 1035 so the write crosses into double indirection: the old single-indirect block becomes the least recently used buffer while the allocations keep refreshing the bitmap and descriptor buffers, and it is written first. A power cut between the two writes leaves a pointer naming a block the bitmap still marks free, which `e2fsck` then repairs. It breaks the milestone Readme's claim that the bitmaps and descriptors reach the medium before the pointers that name their blocks; the claim is narrowed there to the batch's own write until this is fixed. Depends on nothing. Fixed when the cache does not write a deferred metadata buffer of a device with an open batch before that batch's bitmaps and descriptors have gone out, which needs a hook the file system registers rather than a rule the cache can know on its own; a host test that fills the cache during such a write and records the order of the writes shows it, and a mutation that drops the hook fails that test. Fixed in milestone 50 (`c23fb87`): the file system gives a dirty buffer a place in its device's order, `bcache_dirty_ordered`, first for the batch's bitmaps and descriptors and after for everything else it dirties, and every path that writes keeps it: an eviction or the memory reclaim that chose an after buffer writes the device's firsts before it or passes it over while one is held or failed, a sync writes the firsts before the rest in requests of their own, and a run never joins the two; the fresh indirect block's zeroing is deferred into the batch as well. `bcache_hosttest` shows the eviction, the held first and the sync orders, and `ext2_hosttest` writes thirty-two blocks from logical block 1035 through a cache cut to sixteen buffers and requires every pointer block's first write after the bitmaps' and descriptors'; with the mark dropped both fail.

19. The device table cannot be filled from one modelled hub (2026-09-08, milestone 48; severity: a proof not run, no fault in the kernel): a USB device's record is now a frame taken when it appears, so `USB_MAX_DEVICES` bounds a table of pointers and not the memory the records take, and the milestone's plan asks for a row with more devices than the old twelve. Raising it to sixteen and then to twenty-four left `hub_hosttest`'s fill row short: with a modelled hub of fifteen or more ports the keyboard on port fifteen never comes up, the hub is enumerated ten times over, and the table reaches fourteen entries instead of the count asked for. Reproduce by setting `USB_MAX_DEVICES` to 16 in `rach2/sys/usb/core.h` and `model.hub_ports` to 15 with a keyboard on every port in the fill row of `tests/host/hub_hosttest.c`; the row reports `run=15 free=1 ports=15`. The kernel drives fourteen of the fifteen, so the fault is in the model or in a port-fifteen path neither has exercised before; nothing in the suites fails, since the table is twelve. It breaks no stated contract; it leaves one of milestone 48's phase-1 proofs unrun. Depends on nothing. Fixed when `USB_MAX_DEVICES` is above twelve and a hub with that many ports fills the table in `hub_hosttest`, and when the `usb` suite boots more than twelve devices and configures every one; a mutation that drops the last port from the hub's scan fails those rows. Fixed in milestone 50 (`8f25f72`): the fault was the model's, whose `xhci_submit` refused any slot number above fifteen, so the sixteenth device's first transfer failed and the core closed it; the model now refuses only past its own table, `USB_MAX_DEVICES` is sixteen, the fill row's hub is high-speed with sixteen ports so the hub and fifteen keyboards fill the table and the sixteenth keyboard, on a port the route string writes as fifteen, is the one refused, and `tests/run usb` fills the table with seventeen devices across two hubs and configures sixteen across two hubs on two root ports; the probe reads `/proc/usb` whole. With the hub's last port dropped from its scan both the fill row and the suite fail.
20. The installed configuration is saved only on the root it describes (2026-09-08, milestone 49; severity: an update refused, no data lost): `rach2-install --update` reads the accounts, the SSH policy and the host keys off the root it is about to overwrite, so a machine whose root no longer mounts cannot be updated without losing them; milestone 49's plan asks the installer to save that configuration, passwords as hashes, on the recovery partition at install time, and asks the update to compare the two and report a difference. The installer writes no such archive (`utils/rach2-install`, the accounts section) and the update refuses by name when the root does not read. Reproduce by damaging the root's superblock on an installed disk and running `rach2-install --update`: it stops with "carries no root of this system that can be read". It breaks the milestone's own decision on updating, which is written in `dev-log/49-installer/plan.md`. Depends on nothing: the recovery partition is a FAT32 volume this kernel mounts and writes, which is where the archive would go. Fixed when an install writes the archive, an update of a machine whose root does not read takes the archive's configuration, and an update whose two copies disagree says so; a row that damages the root between the install and the update shows it, and a mutation that skips the archive fails that row. Fixed in milestone 50 (`e5abd75`): the install writes the four files, passwords as their hashes, into `rach2-config/` on the recovery partition, found by the recovery kernel on it; `--update` compares the archive with the root's files and names each that differs, keeping the root's, takes the archive when the root does not read and puts the image into the last ext2 entry of the table, and writes the archive again from what it kept. `tests/run installer` requires the archive after an install; `installer-repair` tampers with it, requires the difference named at the update, zeroes the root's superblock and requires the next update to take the archive, and boots what it left; with the refresh skipped the damaged-root update refuses.

21. The fallback kernel cannot be written on its own (2026-09-08, milestone 49; severity: a repair path missing, no data lost): `bootctl` has verbs for the candidate slot, the menu, the trial and the saved entry, and none that writes the fallback kernel from a committed candidate (`utils/bootctl.c`, `bootverb.c`); milestone 49's plan asks for `bootctl fallback FILE`, read back and booted once through `bootctl trial fallback` before it is trusted, and refused while the candidate is unproved. Today a fallback kernel changes only when an install moves the pair, so a machine whose fallback is old keeps it. Reproduce by looking for the verb: `bootctl` prints its usage without one. It breaks the milestone's decision on updating. Depends on nothing. Fixed when the verb exists, refuses a candidate that has not been committed, reads its write back, and a row installs a fallback, boots it once through a trial and returns; a mutation that skips the readback fails that row. Fixed in milestone 50 (`193911b`): `bootctl fallback` copies the committed candidate slot into the fallback file and reads it back, refused while the candidate is not the saved default, while a trial is armed, and on a disk whose fallback file is shorter than the slot, which every disk built before this has, since `mkdisk_grub.sh` now pads the fallback to the slot's length; `tests/run sealed` writes it after the commit, trials it and boots it, and `bootctl_hosttest` refuses the short and the armed cases and catches a dropped write through the readback; with the readback dropped that row fails.

22. The sitting has not been rehearsed at the machine's own size (2026-09-08, milestone 49; severity: a proof not run, no fault in the tree): milestone 49's plan asks for a rehearsal in QEMU on a disk of the Lenovo's recorded size and layout, from the save set milestone 47's `growset` computes through the install, the grow and the restore, with the saved ranges compared against what changed. The rows written run on a 256 MiB disk with a 138 MiB image; the emulator hashes at a megabyte or two a second, so the same rows at 238 GiB and 1655 MiB are hours. Reproduce by reading `tests/system/suites/installer.sh`: every row's disk is made by `dd if=/dev/zero count=256`. It breaks no stated contract; it leaves the sitting's own procedure unrehearsed at its true size, which is what milestone 50's phase 3 depends on. Depends on nothing but time. Fixed when the rehearsal has run once behind a variable, as `RACH2_LARGE_MIB` and `RACH2_GROW_LENOVO` are, with its times recorded against the ten-minute budget; a run that writes outside the computed save set fails it. Fixed in milestone 50: `tests/run installer-rehearse` is the small form, 190 s, and `RACH2_REHEARSE_LENOVO=1` runs it on a disk of 488397168 sectors as a qcow2 overlay over a sparse base, the save set of the image's 3389440 sectors and 1851 ranges of the grow saved onto an exFAT disk in 1 MiB write calls. The run at size on 2026-09-10, after entries 25 and 27, took 3086 s in all: the save 738 s, the install and the grow 1022 s against the sitting's budget of 600 s on the machine, the restore and readback 1291 s, most of the restore being the overlay's own allocation of 4 KiB clusters on the host; every sector the install wrote lay in the save set, every range read back as the bytes saved, and the restore wrote nothing outside the set. Before entry 25 the same run's save alone ran at 1.3 MB a second and was stopped after 17 minutes with the first range unfinished. Run again at `5dcd987` after phase 2's review: 2698 s, the save 512 s, the install and grow 966 s, the restore and readback 1181 s, every check OK. Run again at `f52de94d` after phase 2h, once the kernel stacks were cleared (entry 26): 2650 s, the save 559 s, the install and grow 915 s, the restore and readback 1136 s, every check OK. Run again at `b984b33c`, milestone 06's close, since entry 49 had rewritten the ext2 write path between: 1456 s, the save set the image's 2365440 sectors and 1855 ranges of the grow, every check OK, so the install, the grow to 232 GiB and the boot of that grown root hold with the batched writer.

23. The guest build links to the program's own name (2026-09-09, milestone 49; severity: a wrong result, no data lost): every program rule in `utils/guest.mk` and the one in `ish/guest.mk` links straight to the finished name, `cc -static -o diskctl diskctl.c`, where `rach2/guest.mk` links its kernel to `kernel.new` and renames it, so a link the machine stops part way leaves a file newer than its source and the next `make -f guest.mk` calls it built. Reproduce on Rach2: start a build, kill it during a link, run it again, and the partial program is not relinked. The compiler unlinks the destination and writes the executable into it, so the partial file is what the next build reads, and two makes started in one tree write the same name with nothing between them. It breaks no stated contract; it leaves a build the machine stopped part way looking finished. Depends on nothing. Fixed when each guest rule compiles to a name of its own and renames it after the compiler succeeds, and when a build interrupted during a link is relinked by the next one. Fixed in milestone 50 (`e28bb16`): every program rule in `utils/guest.mk` and the ish rule link to `NAME.new` and rename it, and `tools/verify/guestcheck.c` requires that form of every rule, which the style suite runs; a rule put back to its own name fails it. A link the machine stops leaves `NAME.new`, which the next make overwrites, so the interrupted link is done again; that is the rule's proof, since a build interrupted on the machine is not a row the suites run.

24. Two suites built one image at once (2026-09-09, milestone 49; severity: a wrong result, no data lost; fixed in milestone 49): `krffs/manifest_deps.sh` printed a staged directory as a prerequisite of the image that carries it, and the source trees a `tree` line stages, `utils`, `rach2` and `ish`, are also the names of phony targets, which make holds to be out of date every time. Every image built from such a manifest was therefore out of date the moment it was built, so a suite in the pool rebuilt it, and two suites reached that point together: on 2026-09-09 the `installer` and `installer-repair` suites both ran `mkdisk_grub.sh`, whose unrenamed output had one fixed name, one of them failed with `mv: build/images/rach2-sealed.disk.new: No such file or directory`, make deleted the disk both of them needed, and `sealed` then failed to boot it. Reproduce before the fix with `make -n build/images/rach2-metal-disk.ext2` on a tree that has just built it: the builder is named again. It breaks the runner's claim that `prepare-all` builds the shared images once before the pool (`tests/system/suites.mk`) and the rule of 2026-09-04 that two builds of one image must both finish. Depends on nothing. Fixed: `manifest_deps.sh` prints the files under a staged directory, `mkdisk_grub.sh` and `mkinstallstick.sh` name their temporaries with the builder's process number, and `tests/run imagerace` requires that no manifest stages a directory, that a just-built image is not out of date, and that a file written below a staged directory does not make it so.

25. The storage path was slow by its bookkeeping (2026-09-09, milestone 50; severity: a slow result, no data lost; fixed in milestone 50): measured in QEMU on an IDE disk, a raw read or write ran at 16 to 19 MB a second while a file written on exFAT ran at 2.4, on a 3.6 GB volume at 1.3, a file read back on either format at 5, and a file written on FAT32 at 0.6 falling to 0.1 as it grew, so the rehearsal's save of 3.7 GB was a day and the sitting's would have been hours. Four causes, each found by counting cache gets and disk commands per MiB through `/proc/disk` rather than by reading: `rach2/sys/fs/bcache.c` checked the hold rules by walking every buffer of the cache on every `bcache_get`, sixteen thousand of them at 512 MiB, chose a victim the same way, and rescanned the cache for each run a sync wrote; `rach2/sys/block/ata.c` declared no request size, so the cache wrote and read a page a request with a FLUSH after each; `rach2/sys/fs/fat32.c` cleared a node's chain hint after every write and walked the chain from the file's first cluster to its tail at the next append, quadratic in the file's size; `rach2/sys/fs/exfat.c` wrote the bitmap bit and the FAT entry through for every cluster of a run and walked the bitmap's chain from its first cluster for every bit. Fixed: the cache keeps each process's held buffers on a list in its process record, its keyed buffers in release order and its empties on a list, gathers a sync's blocks in batches of the lowest 128, and fills a run of eight blocks on a miss that follows the last fill; the ATA disk declares 64 sectors; FAT32 keeps the tail as the hint and hints the walk of a new run; exFAT sets a run's bits and entries in the cache and syncs and flushes before the join, and hints the bitmap's walk; both formats flush the device after each ordering sync, which a stick's own cache needs. After the fix the same rows measure exFAT written at 13 MB a second and read at 13, FAT32 written at 6 and read at 16. `tests/run diskrate` held each path under a bound three times the measured time, and the mutation that walked the cache for the hold rules again failed five of its seven rows (the exFAT write at 24 s, its read at 33, the FAT32 write at 117, its read at 50, the small files at 88); the suite has judged no time since `0d5991e4`, so that proof went with the bounds. The other two changes move a row without failing it, since the cache's walk was most of their cost: exFAT's run written through again takes its write row from 4 s to 13, and FAT32's hint cleared again leaves its row at 9 s at 64 MiB, so those two are kept for what they were measured to save and are not proved by a mutation. The recovery kernel and the install stick are not rebuilt by `make`, so the first measurements of the fix were of the old code on a stale stick; the rule is in CLAUDE.md. The proof is a count now (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close). The cache counts every buffer a walk of its lists reads, `bcache_stat_visits`, printed as `cache-visits` in `/proc/disk`, and `tests/host/bcache_cost_hosttest.c` holds it on a cache of 1024 buffers: a hit with three buffers held and its release to at most 8, each of 1024 misses on the full cache with its release to at most 8, and a sync of 1024 dirty blocks on a device of 64-sector requests to twelve walks and eight reads a block, 20480, where a walk for every run written is about 140000. `sh tests/host/bcache_mutate.sh` puts back, one at a time, the hold rules' walk of every buffer (`hold-walk`), the victim chosen by a walk of every buffer (`victim-walk`) and a sync that walks the cache for every run it writes (`sync-rescan`, its batch cut to one block); each fails its row, and each restored copy passes. Entry 226 gave the rows lower bounds as well, a hit and its release at least 5 buffers, the 1024 misses at least 3072 in all and the sync at least eleven walks, since a counter that counted nothing passed every upper bound, and a fault, `uncounted`, that fails all three. The milestone's closing review found one walk the counter missed, the buffer a removal from the list finds, and it is counted.

26. A pipe once progressed a tick at a time (2026-09-09, milestone 50; severity: a slow result once, not reproduced): `dd if=/dev/disk0 bs=1M count=128 | sha256sum` took 438 s in one boot of the recovery system and 15 s in every other, before and after the fix of entry 25, with no kernel message; 32768 pipe fills in 438 s is 13 ms each, one timer tick, so the writer or the reader may have been waiting for the tick rather than for the wakeup. Seen again on 2026-09-10 in the `pipehash` row of `tests/run diskrate`, 64 MiB in 68 s on the ordinary kernel where the same row took 7 s in every other run, 4 ms a fill; sixteen repetitions in one boot with the monitor sampling the call chains all ran at the usual rate, so the state that brings it on is not the row's own. Caught the same day with the sampler running, twelve runs of the row in one boot of which the fifth, sixth and seventh took 91 s and the rest 8: in the slow runs the kernel's time is in the reader's copy loops, `pipe_read`'s `copy_to_user` and `pipe_ring_get`, at thirty times their usual cost with no more bytes moved and no kernel message; the state comes and goes with the runs, which differ only in where the user program's buffer lands. That is the emulator's self-modifying-code path: TCG re-translates a page's code at every write into it, and a read buffer that shares a page with the program's code makes every copy into it a re-translation. The kernel has no part in it and a machine has no such path. The `pipehash` row of `tests/run diskrate` runs three times and takes the least, so one run at the kernel's rate is what the bound judges. A change to the pipe's writer, waking the reader once per call and when the ring is full rather than after every 512-byte piece, was tried the same day and taken back: it made no difference to the emulator's state, and `musl/pwauthmusl`'s stop-and-continue row, which reports through a pipe under a ten-second wait, failed on the machine-built kernel alone once with it in; the writer wakes the reader as it did. The sitting measures `dd | sha256sum` on the machine, where the rate is the kernel's alone. Closed as the emulator's. Reopened in milestone 50 for phase 2h: the pipe row failed in the set of 2026-09-11 with all three of its runs slow, so the placement experiment is run as the phase says and its times kept. Run in milestone 50 (`bb31ddf2`), phase 2h item 3: `tests/placeprobe.c` reads 32 MiB through a pipe into a buffer whose first page has an executed `ret` and into one that has none, three runs each; near 5600 to 6190 ms against far 470 to 1570 ms, a read into a page of translated code costing six times a read into a plain page, which is this entry's kind of cost and not its size, since the runs recorded were eleven and thirty times slower over their whole length. The explanation stands for the direction; the magnitude is not reproduced by a buffer in a code page alone, and the row keeps its three runs and its least. `tests/run diskrate` runs the experiment in a boot of the test image and prints the near and far runs and the pipe row's three times, as the experiment's record, since the suite judges no time; in the same hour the pipe row ran at 100, 54 and 54 s in its os-real boot while the placement boot beside it read at 68 MB/s, so the state is the boot's and not the kernel's. Reproduced on 2026-09-11 in item 4, when the second set's pipe row ran at 151, 112 and 55 s: in one os-real boot, `dd | sha256sum` run three times is at the kernel's rate the first time, 6 to 7 s, and slow in the second or the third in most boots, whether or not a raw read came before, since the programs of one run exit and the next run's buffers may land in the frames that held their code; a spinning process beside the pipe makes a run take 167 s, a quantum per hand-off, but nothing runnable was found beside the pipe in a slow run; a pool of pipe records the kernel owns from boot, so that no ring lies in a frame that held a program's code, was tried and did not remove the slow runs, so the frame on the emulator's slow path is the program's buffer, a frame a program before it executed from, and the pool was taken back. The row runs first in the boot now, before any other program has run and exited, and three times with the least taken, which is the first run's; the machine has no such path. Cause found on 2026-09-11 in item 4 (`5ac04aa9`): not the program's buffer but the process's kernel stack, allocated at fork from the heap uncleared, in frames that held a finished process's code; the emulator keeps a frame with a translation in it on its slow store path, fork writes only the top of the stack, and this unoptimised build's `k_memcpy` and `k_memset` store to stack variables for every byte, so every copy loop of that process runs at the slow path's cost for its life, which is the run-long state the row saw. Proved by QEMU's `memory_notdirty_write_access` trace: 184,547 four-byte stores in 0.3 s of a slow run, all inside the two kernel stack pages of `dd` and `sha256sum`. Fixed: `kstack_alloc` clears the pages; the pipe row ran 6, 6 and 7 s, and the installer's manifest check ran at 18 to 21 MB/s in four boots of four where three of five had run at 0.3 to 0.8 MB/s. The installer hashes its image with `sha256sum FILE` now (`c3bb59ed`), a shorter path measured faster, not the fix. The machine has no such path, and the cleared stack costs it 16 KiB of writes a fork. The fix has a row of its own (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close): `sh tests/host/kstack_mutate.sh` extracts `kstack_alloc` and `kstack_free` from a private copy of `syscall_proc.c` into `tests/host/fixtures/kstack.c`, whose heap fills every page it gives with 0xC3, an x86 `ret`, for the code a frame last held, and requires every byte of a new kernel stack to be zero; the clearing removed, and the clearing of the top page alone, which fork writes anyway, each fail it, and the host suite runs its `--check`. The machine's own rate, read on the Lenovo on 2026-09-25 by `manual_probes.sh`'s `pipe` mode on the final kernel, `4ae77083`, before the root was replaced for entry 223: 128 MiB of the root's partition through `dd` into `sha256sum` in 2.10, 2.11 and 2.10 s by the machine's clock, 2.22 s by the host's around each SSH command.

27. An exFAT directory grew slower with every file in it (2026-09-09, milestone 50; severity: a slow result, no data lost; fixed in milestone 50): measured by the creation of files in one directory of an exFAT volume in QEMU, counted through `/proc/disk`, a create cost 21 disk commands in an empty directory and 261 with 600 files in it, and took 25 ms rising to 130. Three causes in `rach2/sys/fs/exfat.c`: `free_slots` wrote the filler entry at the last slot of every sector it passed on every scan, a write and a flush per sector of the directory per create; every set a lookup passed had its name hashed again with a walk of the compressed up-case table from its start per character; and every 32-byte entry of a scan was a cache get of its own. Fixed: the filler is written only where the end marker sits, the mappings of the first 2048 code points are read once at mount by one walk, and a scan reads each cache block of the directory once into the mount's record. After the fix a create costs 2.6 commands at any size and 500 creates take 3 s where they took 35. The `exfiles` row of `tests/run diskrate` and `tests/run exfat` cover it; the rehearsal's save of 1852 range files is where it was found, and that save now writes each file in 1 MiB calls, since the format syncs and flushes the disk at every write call. Each of the three fixes has its mutation now (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close). `scan_cost_rows` in `tests/host/exfat_hosttest.c` make 220 files in one directory of the volume whose up-case table is larger than the 16 KiB a mount keeps, and hold the twenty creates after the first two hundred, one of which grows the directory, to 60 device writes, 4 barriers and 4000 cache gets, and a lookup of the last name to 200 gets. `sh tests/host/exfat_scan_mutate.sh` puts back the filler written at every scan that passes a sector's last slot, which fails the write bound, and the cache get for every entry and the up-case walk from the table's start, which each fail the get bound. Read from the code and not run, the `exfiles` row of `tests/run diskrate` would fail the per-entry get by far, some 190000 hits against its 65000, and pass the filler, some 25500 commands against its 35500, and no counter sees the up-case walk on that volume, whose table the mount keeps; so the host rows are the proof of the filler and the walk.

28. A run of the suites does not own what it uses (2026-09-11, milestone 50; severity: a wrong result, no data lost): this week two suites shared a directory and each killed what ran under it at its start; two suites built one tool at once in the pool; a suite killed with the signal that removes nothing left a lock the next run waited ten minutes on; a suite edited while a run read it failed the run; the two `netif` suites write the same untagged logs; and the pool holds every suite's output until the whole pool ends, so a failure is seen an hour late and cannot be stopped. Reproduce with two `tests/run` invocations at once, or one killed and started again. It breaks the Verification section's rules that a run is watched and that a killed run is looked for before the next. Depends on nothing. Fixed when each invocation and each suite has private mutable paths and shared artifacts are prepared once; when the runner tracks and reaps the process groups it starts, recovers an abandoned lock or mount without killing another live run, and prints each suite's result the moment it ends with the whole log kept; when a result is bound to the hashes of the scripts and artifacts that produced it, so a script changed under a run voids that run's result by name; and when `tests/run runner` has rows for two invocations at once, a parent interrupted with descendants alive, an abandoned lock, a shared tool built once, and a changed input, each protection with a mutation that fails its row. Fixed in milestone 50 (`6aeb58a`): the runner is a process group of its own and holds `build/.run.lock` with its pid, group, scratch directory and arguments; a second run is refused by name, a lock with no owner file yet is refused as one being taken, and a dead run's lock is taken over by rename only when nothing of its group and no machine of its ledgers is alive. What the run owns is read from the process table, so a process the harness put in a group of its own is reached, and the run's end stops it, on a signal and on an ordinary end alike, waiting until it is gone before the lock is released. The pool prints each suite's result the moment it ends and `RACH2_STOP_ON_FAIL=1` stops what is queued and what is running. The scripts are hashed at the start, the products once built, and both again at the end, whatever the result; a change voids the run by name. `prepare-all` builds every product a `run-` target names, held by rule 15 of `tools/verify/check.sh` from make's database and `prepare-all`'s recipe; the two `netif` suites write tagged logs. `tests/run runner` has the rows: two runs at once, a lock being taken, a dead run's lock with and without survivors in its group or its ledgers, TERM and HUP with a grandchild that ignores TERM, a process that left the group stopped at an ordinary end, a script and a product changed under the run, a product built once for two suites, streaming, and the stop on the first failure; each with a mutation that fails its row. Codex's as-built review of the phase found nine faults in the first version, the lock's non-atomic takeover, descendants outside the group, the background make's ignored signals, the stop's late collection, an empty closure, the inventory's paths and timing, rule 15's seeds, an interrupted run's unprinted output and HUP's status, all fixed before the phase's commit. A further instance at the close, kept as this entry's example: `imagerace` asks make whether a shared image is out of date, and the fifteen other workers of a pool run make on the same tree; on 2026-09-12 one rebuilt the test image between this suite's touch of a staged program and its question, so the suite read the image as current and failed a check that passes alone. The rule is that a suite which reads shared build state cannot run beside builders, so the suite runs alone. Milestone 52 replaced that rule (`dev-log/52-suites/plan.md:41`): `imagerace` mutates targets of its own, the runner's list of suites run alone is gone, and only `style` and `host` run outside the pool (`tests/run:783-791`).

29. The console loses typed input while the machine is loaded (2026-09-11, milestone 50; the kernel half of entry 2; severity: a wrong result, no data lost): COM1's receive interrupt is not enabled (`rach2/sys/serial.c`); the timer wakes a waiting reader to drain the UART later, or drains it itself when no session waits, so whether a byte is taken before the next one arrives depends on what is scheduled, and the line discipline's buffers are finite (`rach2/sys/tty/tty.c`). Under the pool's load a typed line arrives with its first characters lost or not at all; the last full set lost six pooled suites to it, the sets before four and two, more since this milestone made the pooled machines compute at the processor's speed. Reproduce with `tests/run all`, or a typed-line suite beside a CPU-bound boot. It breaks every typed-line suite's contract that the machine receives what the harness typed. Depends on nothing. Fixed when the point at which bytes are lost is measured (bytes the host wrote, bytes the UART received and overran, the queue's occupancy and drops, bytes the reader got), when receive service is bounded and independent of the shell being scheduled, the debug shell's ownership kept and no echo blocking in interrupt context, within a stated supported rate; when a row types numbered, checksummed lines under CPU and disk load, with a blocked reader and as type-ahead during a foreground job, control characters and session transitions included, and every line arrives; and when the service disabled, or a byte dropped on purpose, fails that row. The pseudo-terminal failure of `musl/pwauthmusl` and the monitor's silence under load that put `usb` in the lone group are reproduced and judged apart, since COM1 carries neither. Fixed in milestone 50 (`83ec801`, `ae97360`): the loss was measured first. A `/proc/console` file counts the path from the UART to the readers, and its first reading under a flood said where the bytes went: the shell's own mechanism was not a drop at the UART but a partial line, drained by the tick into the canonical line buffer while the shell's job ended, left there when BusyBox ash switched the console to raw mode to read its next line, and lost, which is how `cp build/rach2/kernel` arrived as `uild/rach2/kernel`; a raw read now takes a partial line before the queue. COM1's bytes are taken by its interrupt, IRQ 4, into a ring of 4096 bytes, and by the tick as well, so a port whose interrupt is off is still served; a parked reader is woken and drains the ring itself, a session nobody reads for has its bytes moved into the discipline at once, and the debug shell's ring is left alone. Nothing is dropped: the ring stops taking at full and masks the interrupt until a reader made room, the discipline is filled only while it has room for a line, and the UART's full FIFO holds QEMU's sender; the first version drained eagerly and the counters showed the read queue overflowing (`queue-drops 3304`) in one boot. A signal character typed behind held input is found in the ring and fed, since it discards that input anyway. `tests/run conload` types 360 numbered, checksummed lines at a machine with five spinners and a writer syncing a file: 200 into a parked reader, all within three seconds and through the interrupt (350 ms; 2040 ms at the tick's pace with the interrupt disabled, which the row refuses by its `irq-drains` count); 60 as type-ahead; 200 to a foreground child then the interrupt character behind them, which must kill it (`flush-bytes 7400`), then 60 more; and 40 across an exec. Three mutations fail it: a byte dropped in the interrupt's service, the interrupt left disabled, and the signal search dropped from the interrupt-side pump. `musl/pwauthmusl` did not fail under ten host spinners: `tests/run default` passed in 184 s under that load, so its pooled failure is not host processor load alone and stays with entry 2 as an instance to catch with the counters in the next set. The usb suite under the same load did not lose its monitor, but two rows failed, recorded as entry 36. Codex's design review corrected the first design (the fallback could not receive, the rate claim was unsupported, stage C asked for lines the interrupt character discards, overruns were cleared by the transmit poll, the exec stage was not a session handoff), and its as-built review found the four faults fixed before the commit: the signal character held behind a full queue, the flush that left the UART's FIFO, a paste's echo suppressed, and a partial line dropped at a full pty queue. Since milestone 53 the row judges no time: `tests/conloadprobe.c` requires the port's interrupt to have served bytes during the stage and every line to arrive, and prints the milliseconds for the record.

30. The suites wait by the clock (2026-09-11, milestone 50; severity: a slow and a wrong result, no data lost): the typed-line suites drive the machine with fixed sleeps, 1474 s of `sleep N` in `tests/system/suites` in all, `sleep 6` per typed line in `account`, and the harness's `wait=N` pumps the console for N seconds after the token it waits for has arrived, five `wait=60` stages in each `netif` variant; a sleep that is too short types into a busy machine and fails the row, and one that is long enough on a loaded host is waste on an idle one. Reproduce by reading the suites. It breaks nothing stated and costs the set minutes and its reliability. Depends on entry 29 for the drops it hides. Fixed when a step waits, under a bound, for fresh consumed output and not for the clock, an echo or an old prompt never satisfying a new step, the interactions that need type-ahead (a password typed before its prompt in `account`) keeping their protocol, and the intervals that are the assertion (a lease renewed, nothing changed, a sustained load, a latency) kept as intervals; when a row delays readiness past the old sleep and passes, suppresses it and fails within the bound, and feeds a stale token and is refused; when each changed stage is timed before and after; and when one type-ahead row is kept so that better pacing cannot hide entry 29. Fixed in milestone 50 (`a489582`, `1406c2a`): the harness has `until=SECS`, the wait after `send=exec /probe` that ends half a second after the run's `--ok` token arrives in output that came after the step began, never past `--timeout`; the seventeen such sites in `suites.mk` (ping, packet, raw, netif, stress, creddisk, saltnoent and passwdmusl, with their variants) use it, and `wait=` keeps its meaning everywhere an interval is the assertion or nothing is printed, which Codex's design review classified site by site: the lease renewed in its time, the address kept while init acts, the key minted with nothing printed, the pings' completion, the shutdowns after a lease, and the small settling waits. `tests/system/drive.sh` is the console driver for the suites that boot QEMU themselves: a feeder holding the fifo open and typing spooled lines in order, `drive_expect` waiting for fresh output after a cursor that stands at the last match or the last typed line, `drive_prompt` for a shell prompt last, and `drive_login`; ssh, procfiles, shutdown, crash, exitline, login, selfbuild, make and tcc type through it, each line once the output it answers has arrived, and `account` keeps its clock-paced feeder as the type-ahead row, since `passwd` reads its prompts back to back. `tests/run drive` proves both against fake machines that choose when they are ready: a prompt at four seconds past a feeder's two still gets its line, a prompt that never comes fails the step within its bound, a token printed before the step is refused, two lines typed back to back are both typed, a machine ready at once is not skipped, `until=` ends at the token and `wait=` does not, a token printed before the until began does not end it, and an until never outlives the run's timeout; the cursor dropped fails the stale-token row and the token check dropped fails the until row. The times: tcc 117 to 14, make 113 to 44, ping 72 to 21, shutdown 92 to 26, login 59 to 30, exitline 60 to 14, procfiles 141 to 23, netif 381 to 88, dhcp 450 to 405, ssh 84 to 12, crash 18 to 11, selfbuild 1725 to 1250 seconds, the first number the last pooled set's and the second a run alone after the change, so the pool's share of the first is not the driver's. Since `1e2248db` (2026-09-18) `account` types each line at its prompt through the harness, and the type-ahead row is stage B of `tests/run conload`.

31. The set runs the same scenario more than once (2026-09-11, milestone 50; severity: a slow result): every network suite exists once per NIC model, `dhcp` and `netif` 900 s of pairs, and several `netif` scenarios choose the same cards whatever `NIC` says; the three installer suites each build the same sticks and hash the same image in their own setup, though their histories differ (a fresh install, a repair from a destroyed disk, the rehearsal's save, install and restore) and must stay separate; `sealed`, `mount`, `usb` and `usbdisk` were put in the lone group for a load they may no longer lose to, 20 serial minutes after the pool; and the pool's width, twice `hw.ncpu`, was measured before this milestone's storage fixes changed what a pooled machine costs. Reproduce by reading `tests/registry` and `tests/system/suites.mk`. It breaks nothing stated. Depends on entries 28 to 30. Fixed when a scenario-to-contract inventory of the network suites says which rows depend on the driver and the rest run once; when the installer suites share what is immutable and demonstrably identical, private copies or overlays for the rest, the fresh install and the whole rehearsal kept independent of the repair; when each resulting suite passes alone and together with the targeted mutations still caught and its stages timed apart; when the four lone suites are tried again under a measured load and kept alone only where they lose to it, `diskrate` alone while its bounds are an idle host's; and when the pool's width is chosen from completion time and failure rate measured after the changes, the longest suites started first. Fixed in milestone 50 (`f6d6cccc`): the network suites' inventory is in `tests/system/suites.mk`: the rows whose subject is the card, netif's first two boots, raw's first boot with its wire check, dhcp's first boot with its broadcast check, and the whole of ping, packet, dgram, netconsole and ssh, run on both cards, and the rows above the driver run once in the first card's suite, the second card's run saying which suite covers them; `default-e1000` is gone, since no probe of the suite sends a frame and the e1000 suite boots the card; the installer suites share the sticks and the sealed disk that `prepare-all` builds once, keep their three histories apart, and print each row's and each setup's seconds; `usbdisk`, `sealed` and `mount` are pooled, `usb` alone until entry 36 is fixed and `diskrate` alone for its bounds; the registry is ordered by the last set's times, longest first. The set of 2026-09-10 at twenty took 61 min and failed four suites: mount and queue on rows of their own (entries 37 and 38), and install and installer to the load, the guest's kernel build and a 32 MiB hash and write each running past a deadline they meet alone; so phase 2g's set runs at twelve, as the design declared before the set, and the two widths are compared by their completion times and failures. Milestone 52 removed the group of suites run alone: every suite but `style` and `host` runs in the pool (entry 28).

32. The storage proofs are weaker than the storage changes (2026-09-11, milestone 50; the review's deferred tests; severity: a check that cannot see a fault): the FAT drivers' host devices write straight into their image with a flush that always succeeds, so a barrier removed or moved between a join and a length, or before a chain's frees, changes nothing they judge, and QEMU's crash rows judge one cut and not an adversarial order; the cache host test has no interleaving for the two defects unit 1 found (a run fill's staged bytes surviving a write in between, an `after` written before a `first` dirtied during a sleep); the ext2 order row judges a pointer block's last write against the bitmaps' first writes, so a bitmap written earlier that does not yet allocate the block satisfies it; the FAT32 hint and the exFAT batching survive reversion under `tests/run diskrate`, whose hold-walk mutation proves the cache walk and not them; `tests/run queue` has the late answer but not the deadline's boundary, the pool's exhaustion or a completion after quarantine. Reproduce by the reversions named. It breaks the Verification section's rule that a check is proved by a mutation. Depends on nothing. Fixed when a shared host device model with pending and durable writes, an order chosen between barriers, flush failures and a cut that discards what is pending judges FAT32 and exFAT, and each barrier removed leaves an invalid durable state the model catches; when the two cache interleavings are rows built on the fake device's callbacks, each fix reverted apart failing its row; when the ext2 row judges each publication of a nonzero pointer against the allocation state on the disk at that write; when operation-count rows fail the hint's and the batching's reversions; when the queue suite has the deadline's boundary, the pool's exhaustion and a completion after quarantine with short periods; and when `tests/run imagerace` proves source-to-image propagation in a disposable build tree, a probe's source changed and exactly `make test-image` run, the broken dependency compiled into its fixture.
