Depends on entry 1, the removal with completions still pending, and is the quieter half of it. The third close omits removed disks and mounts from the listings and rejects VFS access before a cached answer can be returned. FAT32 and exFAT rows warm their caches, pull the emulated stick, and require failed access and successful unmount after close. Both fixed rows pass, both fail with the removal check disabled, and both pass after the restored source is rebuilt; the first and restored kernels match byte for byte.

60. The acceptance still expects the root on partition 4 and selects an unnamed stick (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found while preparing the third close; severity: a wrong result and an unapproved device selected for writing). Run `metal_accept.sh` against the new three-partition install: its root row requires `/dev/disk0p4` where the installed system has `/dev/disk0p3`. With no serial argument and a non-recovery stick connected, it selects the first stick for its write rows. The first breaks the accepted layout's contract; the second breaks the requirement that the owner name the device before writing it. Depends on phase 8's layout and entry 3's device selection. The script now expects partition 3, requires a serial before selecting a stick and fails if a requested serial is absent. Proved when the emulated acceptance passes on the current layout, an unnamed stick receives no write, and the installed machine passes after the owner has selected its test stick. The QEMU rehearsal passed in 30 s on 2026-09-15, including the untouched unnamed stick, serial selection and two recovery-stick refusals. Its RAM root does not exercise the installed-root assertion. Closed on 2026-09-20 against the installed notebook: `metal_accept.sh 192.168.0.106` passed all 72 rows, with the root mounted writable from `/dev/disk0p3`, the boot taken from the candidate slot, the partition table read as 2048, 133120 and 266240 for 131072, 131072 and 488130928 sectors, and the connected stick refused for writing by name, since its table carries a Rach2 recovery ISO.

61. A failed table read bypasses growroot's write check (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found while preparing the recovery grow; severity: a possible write to a changed partition table). The writer checks the disk signature, partition start and overlapping entries only when its first read returns all 512 bytes. It then reads the sector again and writes that second copy without validating it. Reproduce with an I/O stub that fails or shortens the first writer read and returns a changed table on the next, or changes the table between the two successful reads. This breaks growroot's stated check of the table through the writer's descriptor. The target's type and count were not checked either. Depends on the recovery grow. The writer now requires a complete sector, validates its signature, target entry and free space, and changes that same buffer without another read. The ten host cases pass with the fixed source, fail with the old read order compiled in, and pass again after restoration. They require zero writes on failed reads and changed tables, and exactly one read and write of a valid table. The 64 MiB grow and complete restoration also pass with this writer; the table phase uses four commands where the extra read made five.

62. The acceptance treats an unrequested boot diagnostic as an absent disk (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found before the third close's machine acceptance; severity: a wrong result and disk checks omitted). Run the acceptance on the installed system without `rach2.diskdiag`: `/proc/disk` begins with `state not-asked` but its registry lists the mounted Samsung SSD. The script used the first field and reported that no disk answered, skipping the model, size, partition and MBR records. This breaks the acceptance's claim to report the machine's disks. Depends on the disk registry. The script now reads `disk 0` and `identity 0`; the QEMU rehearsal omits `rach2.diskdiag` and requires the registry identity and MBR hash in the acceptance log. The revised registry row passed on the notebook and in QEMU; `tests/run style host metal` passed on 2026-09-15 at 08:24, with metal in 40 s. The missing whole-disk window exposed entry 63 rather than invalidating the registry result. This entry is closed.

63. The acceptance reports the hash of an empty MBR read (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found in the third close's physical acceptance; severity: a false disk measurement). On an installed boot without `rach2.diskdiag`, the whole-disk node is absent. The MBR row piped `dd` into `sha256sum`, hid the read error and accepted the digest of zero bytes. This breaks its claim to measure one sector. Depends on entry 62 exposing that previously skipped row. The row now skips an unpublished node and requires a successful 512-byte read before hashing a published one. The QEMU rehearsal compares the sector with the host's, requires acceptance to fail when its reader returns zero bytes, and checks the restored reader. The complete, empty and restored read phases passed in `tests/run metal` on 2026-09-15 at 08:24. Closed on 2026-09-20: the acceptance run against the installed notebook reported `disk: /dev/disk0 is not published; no MBR read on this boot`, which is the explicit skip, and went on to read the three partitions from the registry.

64. Repeated RTL8139 overflow warnings evict the boot inventory (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found in the third close's metal rehearsal; severity: the diagnostic record is lost during ordinary network traffic). Run the repeated acceptance invocations in `tests/run metal` on the RTL8139 guest, then read `/dev/bootlog`. In the reported run, the 16345-byte record began at 8.56 seconds with repeated `rtl8139: rx: the ring filled; draining it` warnings and contained neither the USB inventory nor the APIC record. This breaks entry 50's retained boot-log condition and makes later acceptance checks fail. Depends on the RTL8139 receive path and the bounded kernel log. Milestone 50 fixes it now because it loses evidence and prevents repeatable acceptance. Overflow is now logged once per boot while every event is still counted and its retained frames drained. A host model delivers 1024 overflow events, requires one warning and complete delivery and accounting, and must fail with the warning guard removed from the compiled driver before passing again with it restored. The metal suite separately requires the live log to retain its early boot records after all acceptance traffic. Both proofs passed on 2026-09-15: the private fixed and restored drivers counted and drained all 1024 events with one warning, and the mutant failed the warning checks with status 1. `tests/run style host metal` then passed, with style in 47 s and metal in 40 s, including the independent live-log check. This entry is closed.

65. A failed process-group call stops a suite before exec (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found in the third close's first full set; severity: the runner produces a false suite failure if the requested group already exists). On the M4 Mac mini with macOS 27.0 and Darwin 27.0.0, the set at `fbace279` passed 87 of 89 suites at twenty workers. `bootorder` failed in zero seconds on the child's `setpgid(0, 0)` returning EPERM; `runner` failed the same way in a nested `passing` suite. Running either alone had passed. The parent also calls `setpgid(child, child)`, but the logs record neither process nor group IDs. Apple's published XNU implementation returns success for an already matching group, so the parent winning that race alone does not establish the cause of these two errors. The broken contract is treating a call's failure as failed group setup without checking the resulting membership. Depends on the host helper shared by all suites. Milestone 50 fixes it now because retries would conceal a failure of the verification machinery. The child now accepts an error only when its actual group equals its PID; otherwise it reports both IDs and the saved error and exits 2. A fixture orders the parent before the child and supplies EPERM with correct and wrong groups, checks a real session-leader EPERM, and checks child setup after a parent error. The selftest runs those cases. The mutation compiles the old unconditional exit into a private helper and must fail the checks, then a restored build must pass. At handoff through `eb3db0a8`, the owner reports the focused `runner` and `bootorder` runs green. On 2026-09-15 the worker ran `sh tests/system/runutil_group.sh --mutate`: all four fixed cases passed, the unconditional-error mutation failed the assigned-group and session cases, and the rebuilt restoration passed. Unit B also passed the runner suite with 147 of 147 selftest checks. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed. The original Darwin error's trigger is still not established.

### Third-close review, 2026-09-15

The six read-only units reviewed `baea1e65..eb3db0a8`. Entries 66 through 88 record their remaining findings and the additional interruption coverage the owner requires. The owner reviewed disposition on 2026-09-15 and permits carrying only findings that cost nothing to carry. Entries 84, 85 and 87 carried the remaining coverage extensions for the reasons beside them; their tests were added later, in `21900c6d`, `3be136fc` and `44cd4d70`, and each entry records its proof. Entry 82 and the subsequent corrections in entries 90 through 93 are closed at the accepted source `78ad9d96`: the owner's twelve-suite focused run passed, followed by all 87 suites with the runner's defaults and no rebuild during suite execution. Entries 80, 81, 83 and 89 are closed against Unit E's complete results and commits; entry 86's source and documentation correction is committed. The earlier fix units are closed against the results below. Every review finding was closed or explicitly carried at the third close, and the carried ones are closed since. The subsequent recovery sitting closes entries 53 and 58; the final disposition and its limits are recorded in [Readme.md](Readme.md#remaining-dispositions).

66. Failed acceptance commands become skips (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 4 finding 1; severity P1: false acceptance). Make SSH return no output and nonzero status for the root command-line read, or fail creation of the image's temporary directory. `tests/system/accept/metal_accept.sh` used conditional pipelines and `account | tr`, losing status and leaving `fails` zero. This breaks its PASS contract. Depends on the SSH and su transports. Milestone 50 fixes it now: required commands preserve status, suppress failed output and record failure across subshells; root selection uses checked command-line and mount reads, and failed image preparation or transfer is a failure. The private transport checks and failure-record mutation pass. The owner reports the mutation proof and `tests/run style metal` passed, style in 47 s and metal in 41 s. Closed in unit A (`e6783882` in this tree).

67. A replacement MBR reader supplies its own digest (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 4 finding 2; severity P1: false disk evidence). Set `RACH2_ACCEPT_DD` to a guest program that prints a digest and exits 1 without saving a sector, or writes fabricated bytes successfully. `metal_accept.sh` accepted the first program's stdout through the failed transport and gave either replacement authority over the evidence. This breaks the one-sector measurement contract. Depends on entry 66. Milestone 50 fixes it now: the override is rejected, `/bin/busybox dd` supplies the sector, its stdout is discarded, and read status and length precede hashing. The transport fixture rejects digest-shaped failed output and the override. The QEMU complete, empty and restored read proof now mutates a private script. The owner reports all three rows passed in the 41 s metal run. Closed in unit A (`e6783882` in this tree).

68. Acceptance reuses a boot log without identity (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 4 finding 3; severity P1: false acceptance). Supply a healthy earlier boot's log through `RACH2_ACCEPT_BOOTLOG` while the current ext2 check reports lost blocks or orphan inodes. The override prevents `metal_accept.sh` from reading the current fault. This breaks the requirement to judge this boot. Depends on live `/dev/bootlog`. Milestone 50 fixes it now by rejecting the override and fetching the live log on every invocation. A clean check cannot cancel another check's fault. The transport fixture rejects a saved log and detects a live fault beside a clean record. The owner reports the revised live-log rows passed in the 41 s metal run. Closed in unit A (`e6783882` in this tree).

69. Repeated password prompts disclose the password (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 4 finding 4; severity P1: credential disclosure). After su authenticates, run `printf 'Password:'; read -r value`. `tests/system/runner/ptydrive.c` answers again with terminal echo enabled, and `accept/su_command.sh` records that output in `raw` and `text`; `PROMPTED` is only boolean. This breaks the helper's no-password-output contract. Depends on the PTY driver shared by SSH tests. Milestone 50 fixes it in unit B: the driver counts each prompt as it arrives, sends one complete answer, fails on another prompt and redacts the password before writing stdout. A possible prefix remains buffered across reads and is redacted at termination; carriage returns are removed before matching, and control bytes in a password are refused. The child does not inherit the password variable. `password_test.sh` compiles the actual driver and runs the actual su helper with a dummy peer over a real PTY. Seven checks inspect `raw`, `text` and returned output, including an echoed and split credential, repeated prompts and a partial credential at EOF. Restoring repeated answers, removing filtering and restoring the boolean count each fail the expected check; every rebuilt restoration passes. The owner reports the mutation proofs and all seven shared suites passing: style 45 s, host 227 s, metal 47 s, ssh 13 s, ssh-e1000 12 s, installer 318 s and runner 246 s with 147 of 147 selftest checks. Closed in unit B, `4046e5ea` and record `20430cdc`.

70. A selected disk name also matches a different disk (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 4 finding 5; severity P1: a write to an unapproved disk). Select serial belonging to `disk1`, with `/dev/disk10p1` preceding `/dev/disk1p1` in `blkid`. The prefix match in `metal_accept.sh` selects disk10's volume. This breaks the serial's authority over writes. Depends on disk and partition naming. Milestone 50 fixes it in unit B: the selected name must end at the device colon or be followed by `p`, a complete decimal partition number and the colon. The transport fixture runs the full script with disk10p1 listed before disk1p1, identical labels and approval of disk1's serial. It requires a write of the expected bytes to the file representing disk1 and no change to disk10. Restoring the old prefix match writes disk10 and fails that assertion before its acceptance status is considered. Fixed and restored cases pass in both transport modes. This is a command-selection proof with simulated disk files; the real file-system paths remain the metal suite's proof. The owner reports all 26 transport checks and the wrong-disk mutation passing, with restored checks green; metal passed in 47 s. Closed in unit B, `4046e5ea` and record `20430cdc`.

71. A wrong grow range list passes its own checks (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 1 finding 1; severity P1: overwritten sectors without saved originals). In the Samsung plan replace `488378368 2064` with `488380432 2064`. `tools/dev/machine_grow.sh` accepts the order, bounds, seed coverage and unchanged byte total, although the list omits resize metadata. This breaks the saved-sector requirement. Depends on the seed superblock, root offset, target geometry and `machine_grow_plan.sh`. Milestone 50 fixes it before saving or growing. Required proof: independently derive the full write set from the checked seed and target, reject the changed range before any SSD write, and accept the generated small and Samsung sets. Unit C implements machine-side regeneration through the shared recovery growset. Native fixed, omission-mutant and restored checks pass for the small and Samsung geometries. The owner's Unit C small grow passed the complete guest refusal and interruption matrix. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `b6804d95`, recorded by `749fb52a`.

72. An invalid GRUB environment prevents root restoration (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 1 finding 2; severity P1: a damaged root cannot be restored by the procedure). Corrupt the GRUB environment header, then restore. `machine_grow.sh` calls `keep_recovery` before restoring root ranges, while `utils/bootslot.c` refuses an invalid header; retry repeats the same failure. The saved `boot.img` is unused. This breaks repeatable recovery. Depends on the complete boot backup and boot-environment writer. Milestone 50 fixes it before the sitting. Required proof: repair from the verified boot backup when parsing fails, keep recovery saved, replay an interrupted repair and restore, and perform no environment write when recovery is already verified as the default with no trial armed. Unit C prepares only the recovery environment in RAM from immutable boot.img and compares three boot extents before writing. Native checks pass for a valid unchanged environment, an invalid header, a torn repair and retry; removing repair and forcing a rewrite are detected. The owner's Unit C small grow passed invalid-header repair, interrupted repair and retry, and target recovery boots without USB. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `b6804d95`, recorded by `749fb52a`.

73. The boot backup is not compared with the disk (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 1 finding 3; severity P2: an unusable saved boot partition passes verification). Corrupt `boot.img` without changing its length before its initial checksum is made. `machine_grow.sh` and the host authenticate the corrupted copy, while `check_disk_copy` compares only `backup.bin`. This breaks the independent backup check before the first SSD write. Depends on complete reads of the boot extent. Milestone 50 fixes it before the sitting. Required proof: compare all boot bytes with a fresh SSD read and reject same-length corruption before any write. Unit C compares all 64 MiB against a fresh disk read before publishing save and again before grow. The native proof rejects a self-checksummed last-byte corruption, detects removal of the comparison and passes after restoration. The owner's Unit C small grow passed the complete guest refusal. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `b6804d95`, recorded by `749fb52a`.

74. Power loss removes the documented restore command (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 1 finding 4; severity P2: the recovery instructions cannot resume). Lose power after the seed overwrite, boot recovery and follow `recovery-grow.md`'s restore section. `/tmp/grow-plan` no longer exists. The same RAM lifetime invalidates its claim that all logs survive. This breaks recovery from the saved stick alone. Depends on the staged `grow-tools.tar` and persistent backup files. Milestone 50 fixes the procedure before the sitting. Required proof: start a fresh RAM root, remount the store, extract and check the tools again and resume directly at restoration, recording which logs survive. Unit C documents fresh device identification, mounting, archive extraction, program and seed verification, and direct restoration after power loss. RAM-log survival is qualified. The owner's Unit C matrix passed all four retries from fresh RAM roots and target recovery boots without USB. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `b6804d95`, recorded by `749fb52a`.

75. The recovery rehearsal covers only completed phases (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 1's rehearsal limit and the owner's requested proof; severity P1: interrupted destructive operations are unproved). `tests/system/grow_machine.sh` saves, grows completely, erases the MBR and restores completely. It neither interrupts those phases nor damages the environment. This fails to establish the procedure's interruption contract. Depends on entries 71 through 74. Milestone 50 extends the small rehearsal before the sitting. Required proof: interrupt save, grow and restore separately, restart with lost RAM state, repair an invalid environment, and compare every restored byte with the original except the deliberately saved recovery environment. Interrupted save must leave the SSD unchanged. Unit C adds four transfer-boundary interruption cases, a damaged header, fresh RAM roots, exact restored-byte comparisons and recovery boots from the target without USB. The expanded matrix runs only under RACH2_GROW_SMALL=1. The owner reports all four interruption rows passed in Unit C: save, grow, restore and repair each recovered the original bytes and booted the target without USB. The matrix took 634 s and grow 652 s; style, host, selfbuild and installer also passed. Unit D repeated the matrix in 620 s, grow in 643 s. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `b6804d95`, recorded by `749fb52a`; final sitting readiness still depends on the remaining close.

76. Poll measurements omit the running kernel's identity (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 6 finding 1; severity P1: unsupported attribution of evidence). Install a before-halt candidate but measure before rebooting, or measure after falling back. `tools/dev/machine_net_in.sh` records program hashes but cannot connect a run to `poll_kernels.sh`'s artifacts. This breaks entries 54 and 57's attribution to particular kernels. Depends on booted-slot and artifact identity. Milestone 50 corrects the existing claims now; no new measurement or kernel installation is added to the sitting. Entries 54 and 57 and phase 6 withdraw the per-kernel comparison while retaining byte counts, guest-tick readings, counter observations and artifact hashes. Required proof for future measurements: reject a wrong booted slot or kernel before timing and log the correspondence. The review establishes no actual wrong-kernel run. Closed by the record correction in unit D; no measured kernel comparison is claimed. Built on 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close), for the measurements to come: `tools/dev/machine_identity.sh` finds the booted slot on the machine's `/proc/cmdline` and the running kernel as the SHA-256 of the booted slot's first bytes, read from `/dev/bootpart` at the extent `bootctl status` prints, and refuses another slot, no slot, an armed trial and another kernel; `machine_net_in.sh` calls it before the transfer and `machine_load.sh` before any session, with the names in `RACH2_MEASURE_SLOT` and `RACH2_MEASURE_KERNEL`, and `poll_kernels.sh` prints `RACH2_MEASURE_KERNEL=PATH` for each kernel it builds. `sh tests/system/machine_measure_test.sh --mutate` runs the drivers and the check against a fixture machine: eight identity cases, both drivers stopping before anything starts on a refusal, and a mutation for each comparison and for a refusal ignored by either driver. Two rewrites of the booted slot leave no mark the check can see: `bootctl fallback` on a machine booted from the fallback slot, and `bootctl kernel` followed by `bootctl cancel` on one booted from the candidate.

77. Guest tick time is reported as elapsed time (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 6 finding 2; severity P2: overstated network rates and load duration). Hold interrupts masked over timer periods while `machine_net.c` samples `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`. `syscall.c` derives that clock from delivered `kernel_ticks`; missed ticks shorten its denominator. The 10 ms resolution also permits zero readings. This breaks a claim of independently measured wall time, including the 196 to 202 MB/s record. Depends on the guest clock used by network, load and grow tools. Milestone 50 labels network output, load timestamps and grow output as guest tick readings and qualifies the recorded rates and phase times beside their numbers. `tools/Readme.md` gives independent host timing around complete loopback and inbound commands: it bounds the transfer interval but includes SSH and command overhead. No new wall-time rate is asserted. Required proof: timestamps from an independently advancing clock bracket the same operation; quantized or lost-tick guest readings cannot establish wall-time rates alone. The owner reports Unit D's measurement proofs and `RACH2_GROW_SMALL=1 tests/run style host grow` passed: style 45 s, host 235 s and grow 643 s. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `35ef9c3d`, recorded by `769c3347`. No new network wall-time measurement is claimed.

78. The load count includes completions after each deadline (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 6 finding 3; severity P2: overstated hourly throughput). Start a build at second 3599 that finishes at 3799. `tools/dev/machine_load.sh` checks the deadline before starting and counts the late completion. Twenty sessions also start their intervals separately after copying sources. This breaks the claim of 400 completed builds within one common hour. Depends on entry 77's clock limitation. Milestone 50 corrects the record to 400 successful completions across twenty separate session windows. `machine_load_session.sh`, sent through SSH by the load driver, records each build's start and completion ticks and separates successes strictly before the deadline from later ones, including equality. Failed builds remain failures. Required proof: a fixture spanning the deadline counts one late success separately; a common-hour claim requires one common independently timed interval. The owner reports Unit D's deadline mutation detected and all eight restored measurement checks passing, followed by green style, host and small grow. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `35ef9c3d`, recorded by `769c3347`.

79. Grow measurement proofs never exercise measured phases (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 6 finding 4; severity P2: false confidence in phase counts). Replace all samples with labelled zeros. `tests/host/growroot_hosttest.c` never passes `--measure` and its ioctl always returns EBUSY; `tests/system/suites/grow.sh` accepts all-zero samples. This breaks the claim that these checks prove phase attribution. Depends on `growroot.c`'s measurement and subprocess paths. Milestone 50 adds a complete `--measure` host run with real child processes updating shared simulated counters at the operation boundaries. The QEMU row wraps the real ext2 tools with separate `/proc/disk` reads and compares every reported tool edge to those reads; all four phases must issue commands. Zero time differences remain allowed at the clock's resolution. Required proof: execute all measured phases with independently controlled distinct clock, command and flush changes, reject constants and wrong phase attribution, and retain the table-writer cases. The host fixed, zero-sample and wrong-attribution mutation phases and restorations pass in unit D; the six parser fixtures pass. The owner reports the ten table cases and four measured phases, mutations and restorations, six parser cases and the small grow all passed in Unit D. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `35ef9c3d`, recorded by `769c3347`.

80. The USB mutation uses an unverified probe image (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 3 finding 2; severity P2: a proof can test stale assertions). Build `test.krffs`, edit `tests/usbdiskprobe.c`, then run `tests/system/usbdisk_mutate.sh` without rebuilding the image. It copies the old image while rebuilding only the private kernel. This breaks the source-to-artifact requirement for mutation proofs. Depends on test-image staging. Milestone 50 fixes it. Required proof: rebuild the probe and private image from the reviewed source, or verify their exact correspondence; changing a probe assertion must change the artifact run in each phase. Fixed in Unit E: compile the current probe in private scratch, stage a minimal image and compare its extracted program with that binary. Resume requires current probe and VFS source equality and verified artifact hashes. The native proof changes an assertion and checks the extracted program; reusing the old binary fails, and restoration passes. Entry 89 corrected the image guard and the native proof's swallowed status. The owner passed the revised native mutation proof, style in 45 s, and both private USB kernel mutations, each with fixed and restored statuses zero, the expected mutant failure and equal fixed and restored kernels. Closed in Unit E, `90a399c2`, `ae1b4061` and record `23edd25e`.

81. A removed parent prevents unmounting its child mounts (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 3 finding 1; severity P1: mount and device resources remain held until reboot). Mount A at `/mnt/a`, mount B at `/mnt/a/b`, close descriptors and remove A. `rach2/sys/fs/vfs.c` returns EIO while resolving B through A, and EBUSY when unmounting A because B retains its reference. This breaks removal teardown through the supported unmount interface. Depends on pathname resolution and mount ownership. Milestone 50 fixes it in Unit E. Required proof: after removal, unmount B then A without reading A's medium, release claims and device records, and retain busy checks for open descriptions. Implemented: after EIO, a unique active mount below a removed ancestor is found by its recorded target. Existing drain and busy checks remain; live children flush normally, removed devices receive no writes. Thirteen native checks and the missing-lookup mutation pass. The two existing USB pull boots now mount the other partition below the first and require child teardown, parent teardown, device reclamation and replug. The owner passed style in 44 s, host in 234 s, mount in 263 s, usbdisk in 158 s and usb in 198 s, including the child-teardown rows. The native missing-lookup mutation and restoration also passed. The owner subsequently passed both private kernel mutations, including `--nested`, with equal fixed and restored kernels. Closed in Unit E, `90a399c2` and record `23edd25e`.

82. A detached worker outlives a passing suite (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 5 finding 1; severity P1: false PASS with surviving work). A suite starts an unregistered child in a separate process group, redirects its output, then exits zero without waiting. The old supervisor returns before checking surviving descendants, and ancestry no longer identifies the reparented worker. This breaks final-verdict liveness. Milestone 50 fixes it with a pipe created before the suite forks. Descendants inherit its writer across exec, setsid, setpgid and reparenting, without an environment variable. The initial implementation and native proof were committed through `651e6805`, but the second full set reopened this entry: seven suites failed the token check after their own PASS, and two failed for other reasons. The owner's corrected contract requires an identified holder outside every recorded group for an immediate failure. Recorded groups receive TERM and fifteen seconds to stop, measured after TERM; only an identified survivor fails after that grace. An empty or unavailable holder listing is inconclusive and cannot fail the suite. The new proof runs ten cases, including both detached forms, clean reaping, ordinary shutdown, a group in another ledger, a six-second shutdown, a persistent recorded-group survivor, unavailable inspection and interruption, with an unrelated process retained. Four mutations remove the check, reject recorded groups immediately, shorten the grace or reject an unnamed holder; each must fail its affected proof, followed by a rebuilt passing restoration. The ordinary-user macOS limits remain explicit: deliberately closing the writer, or execing a program that closes inherited descriptors, escapes the check; the pipe cannot collect an exited worker's status. No privileged process boundary is required. Closed in milestone 50 at `78ad9d96`, with code `f04160e0`. The owner's twelve-suite focused run passed, followed by all 87 suites with no overrides. The full runner log includes all ten fixed ownership cases, the three machine-identity cases, both SSH cleanup cases and 148 of 148 selftest checks. The limits above remain explicit.

83. Interrupted USB mutation leaves a FIFO that blocks continuation (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 3 finding 3; severity P2: the advertised resume fails). Interrupt `usbdisk_mutate.sh` while a mutant or restored command uses `$work/output`, then invoke `--resume` or `--restore`. Cleanup stops its users but leaves the FIFO, so the next `mkfifo` fails. This breaks restart at the failed phase. Depends on supervisor and recorder cleanup. Milestone 50 fixes it. Required proof: interrupt both phases, verify all users have stopped and the FIFO is removed, then complete each continuation with the source restored and rebuilt. Fixed in Unit E: cleanup stops and waits for the command and recorder, removes the FIFO, then restores the source. The native proof interrupts each phase, checks that its command is gone and its source restored, then runs another logged command in the same directory. Retaining the FIFO fails the expected check; restoration passes. This proves the command continuation used by resume; it does not claim a completed QEMU continuation. The owner passed the final native proof and both complete private kernel mutations. Closed in Unit E, `90a399c2`, `ae1b4061` and record `23edd25e`.

84. FAT acceptance infers unmount from a flush increase (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 4 finding 6; severity P2: false persistence evidence). Let creation and cached readback succeed and unmount fail. FAT writes already increase flushes, so the counter and `accept` output can pass without unmount. The file is deleted before any remount readback. This breaks the row's unmount and persistence claims. Depends on entry 66's status correction and the FAT row in `metal_accept.sh`. Disposition on 2026-09-15: the failed-unmount false PASS is fixed in Unit A. `run` records nonzero remote status and discards its output; the checked final command of this row is umount. `vfs_do_umount` calls `block_flush` before successful release. Carried: an independent FAT persistence witness in this acceptance row, which milestone 50 does not add. The aggregate flush increase alone does not attribute a flush to unmount, and this row reads and deletes its file before unmount; it must not be cited as remount or offline readback evidence. Carrying that coverage adds no failing operation or rerun: `usbdisk` already writes, unmounts, remounts and reads FAT data, and checks the image on the host. The SSD grow has its own byte comparisons and recovery boot proofs and does not depend on this stick row for persistence. The later test must inject failed unmount, then require successful remount readback and independent host bytes. Implementation prepared in the entry-84 worktree and committed in `21900c6d`: the row now requires remount readback, and the rehearsal injects a busy unmount and compares independent host FAT bytes before deletion; `sh tests/system/metal_fat_mutate.sh` supplies separate compiled faults for remount and host-byte detection. The proof ran here on the testing host: `sh tests/system/metal_fat_mutate.sh` ends with `pass, both compiled faults failed and restored kernels matched`, and `tests/run metal` passes in 87 s with the new rows. This entry is closed.

85. Metal rehearses no installed-root or absent-window branch (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 4 finding 7; severity P2: changed acceptance branches are unproved). Break the partition-3 root assertion or the absent whole-disk-node branch; `tests/system/suites/metal.sh` still boots its RAM root with published USB whole-disk nodes. This breaks the requirement to rehearse what the sitting will run. Depends on a small installed ext2 disk with boot slots and no `rach2.diskdiag`. Implemented on 2026-09-20 on base `72711d6e` and committed in `3be136fc`. `metal.sh` now runs `accept/installed_test.sh`: a 35 MiB GRUB disk with real kernel slots and an 8 MiB ext2 root. The current command-line, disk and ext2-root sections of `metal_accept.sh` run in the guest against live procfs, device nodes and `bootctl`. A writable partition-3 candidate passes; partition 4, a read-only root and a real fallback boot each produce their required rejection. Every row exercises the absent-window skip while retaining root checks. `sh tests/system/accept/installed_mutate.sh` passed all four fixed rows, detected separate staged mutations accepting partition 4, accepting read-only mounts and failing the skip, and detected a compiled `bootctl` mutation reporting fallback as candidate. Each restored row passed after source restoration, touching, rebuilding and restaging. The trap stops test processes before restoring; extracted guest files match their inputs, the mutated binary differs, and the rebuilt original matches the first binary. Proof logs and artifacts are in `build/metal-installed-proof.zUDGaf`. The final four-row rerun, ShellCheck, suitecheck, prosecheck and `git diff --check` passed. The complete metal suite and a full set were not run then; the metal suite has run in every full set since. These are serial boots with `rach2.rescue`, without a host network connection; they prove the extracted branches, not SSH, boot-log checks or the complete acceptance procedure. No production defect was found. The old transport fixture's live-clean and live-fault cases supplied ext2 mounts but `rach2.ip=none`, so they did not set `INSTALLED=1`. Taken here as committed work: the four installed-root rows run in `tests/run metal`, which passes.

86. The exit contract includes reclamation done only by the reaper (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 2 finding 1; severity P2: an incorrect lifetime contract). In normal exit, let the parent's SIGCHLD handler run before waitpid. Files and cwd are released, but pages, descriptor-table storage and the zombie record remain until `do_wait` in `rach2/sys/syscall.c`. Failed exec also leaves remaining mappings for reaping. The broad comment in `rach2/sys/proc/exit.c` and `syscall.c` claims more than the implementation. Depends on all three exit callers and the reaper. Milestone 50 corrects the comment and owning documentation without changing that existing lifetime. Required proof: source audit names exactly what precedes publication and what a successful wait reclaims. Fixed in Unit E and closed in the accepted source `78ad9d96`. The helper comment forbids blocking or interrupt preemption after publication and distinguishes file/cwd release from later storage reclamation. The syscall comment and kernel Readme match. Source audit: `sys_exit` and `exec_fail_destroyed` leave child mappings to `do_wait`; signal termination frees its mappings and clears cr3 before scheduling; `do_wait` frees remaining mappings, the kernel stack and process record before returning a zombie status. This changes no runtime behavior. `do_wait` is now in `rach2/sys/syscall_proc.c:1600`.

87. The exit helper test does not exercise its callers or a real reaper (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 2 finding 2; severity P2: incomplete teardown proof). Delete `proc_exit_cleanup` from a real death path or add a blocking operation after it. `tests/host/exit_hosttest.c` still passes because resource release, scheduling and notification are substituted. Its forced waits do prove the helper's intermediate ordering. This breaks any claim that this test alone proves all three complete death paths. Depends on entry 86's lifetime contract. Disposition on 2026-09-15: carried as additional integration coverage; milestone 50 does not add the three caller mutations. The host-test Readme now explicitly limits this proof to the helper and says that removing a production call escapes it. The source audit in unit 2 found all three calls present, every sleeping release before publication and no scheduling or permitted interrupt preemption before final teardown; it identified no runtime defect. Entry 86 states that contract precisely. This costs no repeated run, machine action or known resource failure to carry, and no acceptance claim depends on these host substitutes proving an actual reaper. The future integration test must drive normal exit, signal termination and failed exec with a real waiting parent; removing each call or allowing a post-publication wait must fail. Closed on 2026-09-21 in `44cd4d70`: `sh tests/system/exit_mutate.sh` passed the `normal`, `signal` and `exec` rows with real waiting parents, made each row fail with its cleanup call removed and with a real wait inserted after publication, and passed each row again after restoring, touching and rebuilding its sources, with all six restored kernels matching the original byte for byte.

88. Inbound measurements retain their default known-hosts file (2026-09-15, milestone 50, unit 6's secrets observation; severity P3: an unnecessary address and public-key association remains). Run `tools/dev/machine_net_in.sh` without `RACH2_NET_KNOWN`; cleanup leaves `$work/known_hosts` beside the logs. No private key or password is disclosed. This differs from the temporary per-system host-key file described in the root Readme. Depends on distinguishing a helper-created file from a caller-owned file. Milestone 50 removes only the default scratch file after the final cleanup connection, on completion, failure and interruption. A caller-supplied file remains caller-owned. Required proof: default scratch files disappear on both paths while an explicitly supplied known-hosts file remains. All five native cleanup cases and the removal mutation passed locally in Unit D. The owner reports the measurement proof and all eight restored checks passing, followed by green style, host and small grow. Fixed in milestone 50 and closed in `35ef9c3d`, recorded by `769c3347`.

89. A private USB image fails its guard while the native proof passes (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found in Unit E validation; severity P2: a false proof and a blocked mutation run). Run `usbdisk_script_test.sh --mutate`, then `usbdisk_mutate.sh`. The first reports success; the second stops because the generated `probe.manifest` has no allowlist entry. `image_case` called `build_probe` before `|| return`, disabling errexit inside it and hiding the guard's failure behind the final successful byte comparison. This breaks the claim that the native proof executes the production image construction successfully. Depends on setidcheck's manifest-stem lookup and shell conditional status rules. Milestone 50 fixes it in Unit E: a registered `usbdisk-proof.manifest` template declares no set-id files, requires the probe node and forbids init; a private copy changes only the compiled source path. Both guard checks and extracted-byte equality remain required. The native driver invokes the builder in a fresh fail-fast shell. A compiler returning 23 with previous artifacts present must retain that status; an unlisted manifest must fail before extraction. Restoring the old conditional invocation swallows the compiler failure and fails the proof. Fixed and all restored phases pass locally, including the original stale-probe and FIFO mutations. The guard's manifest sweep passes 19 profiles and its self-test passes 124 cases. The owner passed the revised native proof, style in 45 s and both private kernel mutations, including matching fixed and restored kernels. Closed in Unit E, `ae1b4061` and record `23edd25e`. No shared suite is invalidated by a product change: this correction changes only private construction, the native script proof and its manifest policy.

90. The liveness check fails shutdown and unnamed holders (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found in the second full set; severity P1: false FAIL and an incomplete runner proof). Run the set at its ordinary twenty-worker width. Tcc, ssh, ssh-e1000, shutdown, drill, drive and account print their own PASS and then fail the token check. The named holders are in recorded groups; four suites have no named holder. This breaks entry 82's distinction between detached work and work the runner can stop through recorded groups. Depends on shutdown timing, holder inspection and group ledgers. Milestone 50 corrects the verdict as specified in entry 82. Required proof: inside-group and six-second shutdown cases pass, an outside-group worker fails and is stopped, a named survivor after the fifteen-second TERM grace fails, and an unavailable listing cannot fail. Mutations reverse each rule. Closed in milestone 50 at `78ad9d96`, with code `f04160e0`. All twelve focused suites and all 87 full-set suites passed, including the ten fixed ownership cases in runner. The earlier five-case pass remains evidence only for its original cases. Since 2026-09-24 `sh tests/system/runutil_owned.sh --mutate` gathers the proofs of entries 90 to 93 against the final runner; it passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25, each fault detected and each restored run passing.

91. The install rehearsal uses a partial first ext2 group (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found in the second full set; severity P1: the rehearsal cannot compute its backup set and continues after failed setup). Run `tests/run installer-rehearse` with Unit C's strict calculator. The retained head has 16384 blocks of 4096 bytes, a 64 MiB root, but declares 32768 blocks per group. `utils/growset.c` rejects `blocks < bpg`; its safety checks require a complete first group. The script then records a failed setup but continues saving, installing and restoring with an incomplete range list. This breaks the rehearsal's backup coverage prerequisite. Depends on `mkext2.sh`, the installer image recipe and `growset`. Milestone 50 keeps the calculator strict, builds this fixture with 16384-block groups, verifies that value in the superblock, and exits immediately if the initial range computation fails. Required proof: the rebuilt installer artifact passes range generation and the install/restore write-map checks in `installer-rehearse`; restoring the old group size must fail setup before any rehearsal write. Closed in milestone 50 at `78ad9d96`, with code `f04160e0`. Installer-rehearse passed in 103 seconds in the focused run and 192 seconds in the full set. The latter log records three ranges beyond the image and requires both the install/grow and restore writes to remain inside the save set. Its proof is among those `runutil_owned.sh --mutate` gathers (entry 90).

92. The machine check treats old PIDs as current machines (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found in the second full set; severity P2: an unexplained machine verdict and possible stale cleanup target). Sealed prints PASS, then the old five-second `kill -0` check reports PID 58584 still running. The log records no process state, group, start time or command. The shim retains both machine and relay PIDs across all boots; a zombie or reused PID satisfies that old check. Sealed kills and waits for every QEMU, and the token check completed before this failure. The old record cannot establish whether 58584 was an exited relay or a reused PID; neither is asserted as the measured cause. The demonstrated defect is a PID-only verdict with no identity evidence. Depends on QEMU shim ledgers and native process inspection. Milestone 50 records group and start time before exec, checks matching live identities after TERM and fifteen seconds, excludes zombies and reused PIDs from verdict and ledger cleanup, and prints PID, group, start time and command for a survivor. Required proof: a real process with a mismatched saved start time and an unreaped zombie are excluded, while a matching live survivor fails and is killed; removing the start-time comparison fails the proof. Closed in milestone 50 at `78ad9d96`, with code `f04160e0`. The full-set log contains passing reused-PID, zombie and live-survivor checks. Sealed passed in 463 seconds in the focused run and 535 seconds in the full set. The old log still cannot identify the state of PID 58584. Its proof is among those `runutil_owned.sh --mutate` gathers (entry 90).

93. SSH flood cleanup kills only pipeline shells (2026-09-15, milestone 50, found while reading the second full set; severity P2: surviving host children). The forty `sleep 300 | nc` pipelines are launched in background subshells. The suite kills those shell PIDs without reaping the sleep and netcat children. SSH and SSH-e1000 report many token holders in their recorded suite groups; source inspection identifies the flood's untracked children. This breaks standalone suite cleanup, even when the runner can stop their group. Depends on both SSH suites' shared shell script. Milestone 50 connects directly tracked sleep and netcat children through FIFOs and kills and reaps both on normal completion, failure and interruption. Required proof: the production functions and cleanup traps reap all eighty socket-free fixture children on completion and TERM; omitting sleep ownership leaves children and fails, followed by passing restoration. Closed in milestone 50 at `78ad9d96`, with code `f04160e0`. The full runner log confirms both fixed cleanup cases reaped all eighty children. SSH and SSH-e1000 passed in 13 and 14 seconds in the focused run and both passed again in the all-green full set. Its proof is among those `runutil_owned.sh --mutate` gathers (entry 90).

### Recovery sitting, 2026-09-16

The two physical grows complete entries 53 and 58 with identical command and flush counts. The first sitting found the following three faults, all fixed in `00a93ace` at accepted source `8ce6fbd5`; none is carried. The first restore used an operator workaround. The second sitting completed the full cycle with the committed code on the same disk and real stick, adding the machine proof to the host/QEMU results and review. The installed root is restored and clean, recovery remains saved and no candidate trial is armed.

94. Restore output can exhaust the recovery RAM root (2026-09-16, milestone 50, found at the recovery sitting; severity P1: recovery stops with the installed root partly restored). Follow `recovery-grow.md`'s instruction to save the complete detached command output under `/tmp`, then restore the Samsung's 3037286400-byte range set in the same 4 MiB recovery root used for the preceding work. The operator reports that the first restore filled that root and stopped part way, without a diagnostic that could be saved; a retry with the transcript on the stick completed. `utils/reflash.c` prints a completion line per invocation, and `machine_grow.sh` invokes it for each saved range, including the many roughly 1 MiB metadata ranges. This is not a progress line for every MiB inside one call. The successful transcript is 110764 bytes for 1856 ranges; it does not by itself account for 4 MiB, and the failed run's free-space history was not retained. The defect is the procedure's unbudgeted output sharing recovery's finite scratch space. It breaks CLAUDE.md's requirement that the prepared recovery path undo the write, and the helper's restoration/retry contract. Depends on transcript placement, the RAM-root free space, per-range output and entry 95's work files. Fixed in milestone 50: the helper saves complete command output on the checked exFAT store, tests allocation there before a target write, and checks the producer and log writer separately. Failure is also reported on `/dev/console`, independent of redirected stderr. Reflash needs no output change because its completion records no longer consume RAM-file-system space. Proof: the native replay restores all 5932200 sectors in the sitting's exact 1856 ranges, verifies every byte and gap boundary, and retains all completion records. The separate QEMU pressure proof fills the shipped 4 MiB RAM root to zero available space, then runs save, grow and restore in one boot with guest-side captures and unchanged RAM usage. With the exFAT log and redirected transcript full, the actual failed append names `restore.log` on the independent console and returns status 1. Freeing space and retrying restores every original byte, checks the root and boots the target without USB. Moving command logs back to RAM fails before a target write; the restored implementation passes again. The native proof also detects an ignored log-writer failure and passes after restoration. These complementary tests prove full transfer and guest allocation; the hardware cycle is recorded separately. The slow guest cases remain behind `RACH2_GROW_SMALL`. The owner passed the focused style, host and small grow run in 46, 240 and 984 seconds, and the separate guest mutations detected RAM work and RAM logs before a target write, with restored cycles passing. The closing review found no blocking issue. [notes.md](notes.md#final-third-close-2026-09-16) records final acceptance and the proof limits. The committed code subsequently completed save, grow and restore on the same Lenovo SSD and real stick. Restore returned status 0 with all 1856 completions and matching original range digest and e2fsck counts. The RAM root never filled and ended with 194 KiB free. An earlier grow attempt with an operator transcript open on the store was correctly refused before its first SSD write. The [second sitting](notes.md#second-recovery-sitting-with-committed-code) completes this entry's machine proof.

95. The recovery helper puts all work files in `/tmp` (2026-09-16, milestone 50, found at the recovery sitting; severity P1: scratch exhaustion can stop restoration after a disk write). `tools/dev/machine_grow.sh` unconditionally creates `mktemp -d /tmp/machine-grow.XXXXXX`. Plan copies, expected ranges, command statuses, boot preparation and check logs all consume the 4 MiB RAM file system, and successive actions leave their directories there. Reproduce by running save, grow and restore in one recovery boot with the real range-list size and accumulated logs; also fill the remaining scratch space before a status or range-list write. At the sitting the operator changed the work-directory parent to `/mnt/grow`, and the completed restore reports `/mnt/grow/machine-grow.mbnNPK`; the repository helper at `0b0c76c4` still used `/tmp`. It breaks the promise that recovery can restore the verified backup with the shipped RAM root, even when the external backup has enough space. Depends on entry 94, store identity checks and the grow's deliberate store unmount during measurement. Moving every work file to that mount unconditionally is not a complete fix: measurement still needs bounded scratch while the store is unmounted, and an unmounted `/mnt/grow` must not silently become RAM storage. Fixed in milestone 50: the helper verifies the canonical store mount and its non-target device before creating any work file there. All action directories remain beside `saved`. The measured grow captures at most 65537 output bytes in process memory across the store unmount, then remounts and checks the saved log and final producer status; overflow or a missing status fails. Grow first checks that the store can be unmounted before any target write. Mount commands use `-n` so BusyBox does not update `/etc/mtab` in the full RAM root; `/proc/mounts` is authoritative. Proof: native rows check the required mount, work location, successful, failed and oversized measurement output and the remount sequence. The QEMU pressure cases retain all action directories, range calculations and status writes through one boot with zero free RAM-file-system space. Every successful restore leaves that usage unchanged and boots the original target without USB. Restoring `/tmp` work placement fails before a target write, and the restored implementation passes a complete cycle. The previous save, grow, restore and boot-repair interruption matrix also passes with the changed helper, including fresh-boot retries, in 632 seconds. The owner subsequently passed all three focused suites and both mutation scripts; the repeated interruption matrix took 624 seconds. The closing review found no blocking issue. Closed at `8ce6fbd5`; [notes.md](notes.md#final-third-close-2026-09-16) records the final results. The second sitting proves the committed work placement on hardware: save used `/mnt/grow/machine-grow.ImEKhD` on the real stick with 222 KiB of RAM-root space remaining, and all four action directories stayed on that store. Grow unmounted and remounted it around measurement; restoration completed with the original counts and 194 KiB free. The [second sitting](notes.md#second-recovery-sitting-with-committed-code) completes the machine proof.

96. The grow helper labels an output-write failure as unsupported geometry (2026-09-16, milestone 50, found at the recovery sitting; severity P2: a false diagnosis delays recovery). Run `check_plan` in `tools/dev/machine_grow.sh` with a valid seed and too little space for `expected-ranges.txt`. Its `growset` command or output redirection fails, and the wrapper reports `the seed geometry is not supported` for any nonzero status. The operator encountered this with a full recovery file system. `utils/growset.c` already checks `fflush(stdout)` and `ferror(stdout)` and reports `writing the range list failed`; the wrapper is the source of the incorrect geometry claim. The retained `machine-grow.OINpah/expected-ranges.txt` is truncated; it is not a complete diagnostic trace of the failed process. This breaks the tools' diagnostic contract by identifying a seed-layout fault when output storage failed. Depends on entry 95 and preservation of the calculator's status and stderr. Fixed in milestone 50: the wrapper preserves the calculator's stderr and identifies `expected-ranges.txt`; growset includes the system error in its write diagnostic. `machine_grow_space_test.sh --mutate` passes ten fixed cases, detects the old false-geometry diagnostic, and passes ten restored cases. Its file-size-limit case produces a real failed partial write and `growset: writing the range list failed: File too large`; the wrapper reports the output pathname. A genuinely unsupported target keeps its geometry error. The same proof detects RAM work placement and an ignored log-writer error, with restored passes. The recovery artifact contains the new calculator diagnostic. The owner subsequently passed the native mutation proof, with ten cases after every restoration, and all three focused suites. The closing review found no blocking issue. Closed at `8ce6fbd5`; [notes.md](notes.md#final-third-close-2026-09-16) records the final results. The fixed growset and helper were verified by their changed strings on the notebook and used throughout the second complete recovery cycle. The helper correctly diagnosed the operator's open transcript as a busy store and refused grow before an SSD write. That hardware refusal proves its own condition; the native partial-write mutation remains the proof of the output-error diagnostic. Together with the successful cycle on the real stick, these complete the entry's acceptance; see the [second sitting](notes.md#second-recovery-sitting-with-committed-code).

97. The ATA driver polls the status port after every sector it writes (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found while measuring the testing host under its nested KVM; severity P2: every port access is a hypervisor exit, so a write costs ten to forty times what it costs in the emulator, and the 4 KiB write rows of diskrate outran the runner's silence limit). Run `RACH2_QEMU_ACCEL=kvm tests/run diskrate` on the WSL host: the 64 MiB FAT32 write measured 235.78 s against 24.55 s emulated, the raw read 20.91 s against 21.15 s, and a 16 MiB write in 4 KiB calls, 40976 commands, was still running after 300 s of silence. `rach2/sys/block/ata.c` moves data with `insw` and `outsw` and waits for each command by reading the status register in a loop; the emulator answers a port read in a function call, a first-level KVM in about a microsecond, a nested one in tens, and a write completes only after the emulator's own write to the image, during which the loop spins. This breaks no contract of the driver's, only the time every write-heavy suite takes on a virtualized host. Depends on nothing. Implemented in milestone 52 at `189caf95`: bus-master DMA and interrupt-backed sleeping waits, with the per-channel bounce buffer in `df6e63e2` and polling backoff in `1b1132fa`. The open disposition was stale. The original exFAT/raw-read time ratio is not an acceptance bound: diskrate now judges command, status-read and wait-tick counts and prints times for the record. Entry 97 coverage added on 2026-09-20 on `fix-97` and committed in `72711d6e`: eight native write rows check LBA28 and LBA48 at 512 bytes, 4 KiB, 32 KiB and across the bounce-buffer boundary, every byte and adjacent sectors, one DMA command and flush per 32 KiB, no data-port transfers and bounded polling. Diskrate runs these rows before its guest boots, requires DMA on all three IDE disks and checks each write interval separately. The obsolete polled-only description in `ata.h` is corrected. Proof: `sh tests/host/ata_rate_test.sh --mutate` passes under AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer, compiles a forced-PIO write path that fails the DMA and poll-count checks, restores and rebuilds to a pass, then repeats for a 512-read initial spin. A 32 KiB write and flush use 24 status reads when fixed, 32846 with PIO and 1030 with the longer spin. The script mutates a private source copy, stops its test before restoration, restores in a trap, touches the source and recompiles every phase; complete logs and binaries are in `build/review-proof/ata-rate.RYau3F/`. The production ATA object also compiles with `-ansi -Werror`. This local proof covers the driver against a model, not interrupt delivery or the recorded nested-KVM rate. The requested KVM run remains unverified here: this host's QEMU rejects `-accel kvm` as an invalid accelerator. The guest assertions were not run. The guest assertions ran here: `tests/run diskrate` passes under KVM on the testing host in every full set since, and the rate rows hold. Taken in `72711d6e`. This entry is closed.

98. One `make` does not restage an image after rebuilding a probe it stages (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found while proving the console-load probe on the testing host; severity P2: a suite run by hand after an edit boots the old probe and reports the old result). Edit `tests/conloadprobe.c`, run `make -j32` once on a built tree, and read `/conloadprobe` back from `build/images/test.krffs` with `build/krffs/readfile.krffs`: the probe binary in `build/tests/` is new and the copy in the image is old; a second `make` restages it. The image rule names `build/tests/conloadprobe` as a prerequisite, but the probe is rebuilt by the `tests` sub-make that the image reaches only as an order-only prerequisite, and make judged the image by the probe's time as it stood when the root make began. `tests/run` is not affected, since it runs `make components` in one invocation and the suite target in another. This breaks the Build section's contract that a later `make` rebuilds only what changed and everything that changed. Depends on nothing. Fixed in the `fix-98` worktree based on `c52d6aa3` and committed in `72711d6e`. The root Makefile orders each staged probe, musl test, utility and Doom file after its component and runs an empty recipe so make reads its updated time. Each component still builds once per invocation, and unchanged images stay unchanged, including under `make -n`. Imagerace now runs `imagerace_probe.sh` against a private copy of the production root and probe Makefiles, with the real compiler, image builder and reader and a one-probe manifest. Proof: `sh tests/system/imagerace_probe.sh --mutate` passed the changed-source row, failed with the staged-probe dependency rule removed, and passed after restoration and rebuild. The mutation compiled the edited probe to 10780 bytes while the image retained the original 10732 bytes; extraction and byte comparison detected it. Fixed and restored binaries were identical. A shell trap restores the private sources after stopping the build; each phase touches the source and removes the old binary before compiling. Only this host row ran, with no guest or full suite. The recorded cause reproduced without a correction. The full set then failed `imagerace`: the ordering rule makes make read a staged file's time again after its component runs, and that second read takes back the time `make -W` supplies, so the suite's `-W` row could no longer ask whether a rebuilt program restages its image. The row now reads the production tree's own database instead (`c3fda6c2`), where the image names the program among its prerequisites and the program waits for the component that builds it, and the behaviour itself stays with `imagerace_probe.sh`, which performs the edit and the build against a copy of these makefiles. `tests/run imagerace` passes in 6 s. This entry is closed.

99. A woken sleeper waits a whole round-robin rotation for each answer (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found when the ATA driver began to sleep for its commands; severity P2: a process doing disk I/O beside five spinners finishes one command per rotation, and the console-load probe's writer finished no 256 KiB file in the fifteen seconds of its loaded stages under either accelerator). Run `tests/run conload` with the driver sleeping: `CONLOAD writer files by stage C: 0`. `rach2/sys/proc/sched.c` picks the next READY process after the one scheduled last and gives a woken process no place ahead of the spinners, so a caller woken by a device's interrupt runs after every runnable spinner has had its tick. A first attempt on the same day, a `woken` mark set by `wq_wake_all` and preferred by `schedule`, failed 17 of the 72 probes of the default suite and the lag writers of fatwrite, so a preference for the woken must be bounded and proved against them. This breaks no contract, only the throughput of an I/O-bound process under load; the console-load probe now waits for its writer's first file under a completion limit instead of requiring one within the stage. Depends on nothing. Implemented on branch `fix-99` from base `c52d6aa3`; no commit was created, as requested. A wait-queue wake grants one preferred selection, followed by an ordinary round-robin selection; preference does not advance the ordinary cursor. Selection is masked against interrupts, and selection or resumed execution consumes the wake mark. `conloadprobe` requires and reports writer completions separately in stages A, B and C, and its suite rejects absent, repeated or zero counts. Each count is taken when the stage's console work ends; zero fails without the former extra wait for a file. These stage counts depend on console and device speed; the native dispatch proof is independent of both. The production scheduler and wait queue are exercised by `sched_hosttest` under ASan and UBSan: a sleeper precedes a spinner, ordinary service follows preference, and five spinners each run within fourteen dispatches despite two repeated wakeups. `sh tests/host/sched_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, compiles three separate mutations that remove preference, remove its bound or move the ordinary cursor on preference, and detects each with status 1. Its trap restores the private source after stopping any test process; each restoration is touched, rebuilt and passes with status 0. Sources, binaries, checksums and logs remain in `build/tests/sched-proof.n8FIBi`. The C89 kernel and conload probe build, and the wait-queue and exit host tests pass. Guest acceptance remains unverified: `tests/run conload` and `tests/run default fatwrite` are refused by the sandbox's TCP, UDP and Unix socket preflight before a guest starts. Both accelerator runs and the guest regression results remain required; the native proof does not establish disk throughput. The current default suite has 73 probes; the 72 above records the earlier attempt. The guest acceptance ran here on 2026-09-20 under KVM. It failed first: `musl/pwauthmusl` hung and took the sixteen probes after it, which is entry 137, a race in that probe rather than a fault of the preference; `tests/run default` passes 74 of 74 with the preference in the kernel once that probe is fixed, and passes with the preference removed as well. `tests/run conload` now records a completed writer file in the loaded stages where it recorded none, and `fatwrite`, `selfbuild` and `selfbuild-ext2` pass. The writer's completions are no longer judged at all: how many 256 KiB files a writer finishes inside a stage is that stage's length and the machine's rate, and the same stage measured 4720 ms alone, 1320 ms alone again and 930 ms in a set of twenty machines, finishing one file, none and none. The probe prints each stage's count for the record and the suite requires one line for each stage, which is what says the probe reached them. What the scheduler owes a woken writer is a count, the dispatches it waits, and `tests/host/sched_hosttest.c` holds that bound where no other machine's load can move it. This entry is closed.

100. An ext2 truncate or unlink writes three requests for every block it frees (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found when the large suite stalled after its rate line under the emulator full set; severity P2: a shrink of 64 MiB takes a hundred seconds alone on an emulated disk and outran the runner's silence limit beside other suites, and a delete of a large file costs the same). Run `tests/run large` in the emulator with `disk-commands` read from `/proc/disk` around the probe's truncate: about 49000 commands for the 16384 blocks freed. `punch_level` in `rach2/sys/fs/ext2.c` writes each freed block's pointer slot through, then its bitmap byte, then its group descriptor, since a free runs outside the batch a write opens, whose order, bitmaps first, is the wrong one for a free. This breaks no contract of the driver's, only the time every truncate and unlink takes, in every suite that deletes what it wrote. Depends on nothing. The fix is a batch for a free with the order reversed, the pointer blocks and the inode `first` and the bitmaps and descriptors `after`, each block written once at the end; the test that shows it fixed is `bigfileprobe`'s count of the disk's commands around its truncate, bounded by a few per pointer block freed, which the write-through form exceeds by a hundred times, with the ext2 host test's interruption matrix still walking every stop of the truncate and unlink sequences clean. Fixed the same day: the free batch in `ext2.c`, the count in `tests/bigfileprobe.c`, the ext2 host test's 329 stops walked.

101. The text console redraws the whole screen for every byte (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found when the USB key latency rows dropped their record under KVM; severity P2: `k_putc` blitted all two thousand cells to the adapter after each byte, and a scroll did the same, to memory that is an uncached bus cycle on hardware and a trapped access under a hypervisor; the key latency probe's reader was still writing its `KEYLAT READING` line when the key arrived, and under nested KVM read it 20.4 ms later, where the emulator's reader read it in 0.3 ms, and every line the kernel or a program prints costs the same). Run the usb suite's row 12 under KVM on the old driver: `usb: latency:` is not recorded, since the read comes past the record's two ticks; or run `tests/host/vga_text_hosttest.c` against it, whose rows count the cells a byte and a scroll write. This breaks no contract of the console's, only the time every console write takes, which the latency rows' record measures under KVM and every boot pays on the machine. Depends on nothing. The fix is a driver that writes each byte's cell once, scrolls by moving the CRTC start address through the eight screens the adapter maps, copies the rows that stay to the front of that memory once in 179 lines, and places the cursor once per write; the test that shows it fixed is the host test's counts, one cell a byte and eighty a scroll, and the latency rows' record under KVM. Fixed the same day: the driver, the holds in `kio.c` and `klog.c`, the host test with four mutations proved.

102. A polled disk wait held the emulator's completion out and then panicked (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found when the self-build suite's second copy panicked with `pagecache: unrecoverable disk fault` under KVM in a full set, with no record before it; severity P1 under a hypervisor: a page cache fill, which polls with interrupts off, gave up after ten million status reads and the kernel stopped; the same loop on hardware costs nothing). Run `tests/run selfbuild` under KVM on a host running the full pool: the ATA driver's polled wait in `rach2/sys/block/ata.c` read the status ten million times in a row, each read a trap that takes the emulator's lock, and QEMU's main loop, which completes the DMA, did not get the lock in that time; the wait returned `-EIO` without a record and the fault handler panicked. This breaks the block layer's contract that a polled read of a present sector completes, on any host where the emulator's completion needs the guest off the device for a moment. Depends on nothing. The fix is a polled wait of a few hundred plain reads, which is what the emulator's DMA needs, then rounds of ten thousand `pause` instructions off the device and a read, bounded by rounds, and a record at `KLOG_ERR` naming the channel, the status and the wait when it gives up; the sleeping wait's spins before its sleep go from 64 to 8, since a DMA command answers by interrupt and each spin is a trap. The test that shows it fixed is the self-build suite under KVM in the full pool, three sets, and the disk rate suite's status reads per command, which fall from 65 to 9 under the emulator. Fixed the same day.

103. A polled ATA command was issued into another command's data phase (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found when the self-build suite under KVM in the full pool ended its second copy in `No space left on device` on a disk half free and then a kernel fault at a garbage address, and in another set in a polled wait for data that saw the drive ready with the bus master's interrupt pending; severity P1: torn pages of the file system's own headers, silent until the allocator or a walk reads them; the emulator shows a drive in a DMA transfer as ready with DRQ set and BSY clear, which the hardware may too). Run `tests/run selfbuild` under KVM beside the pool: a page cache fill, which cannot sleep, waited for BSY clear alone in `rach2/sys/block/ata.c`, passed at once while the channel owner's DMA was in flight, wrote a PIO command the drive refused, and then read the DMA's data phase as its own sectors, or waited for data that never came once the DMA had ended. This breaks the block layer's contract that a read returns the sectors it named. Depends on entry 102, whose record named it. The fix is a wait for the drive idle, DRQ clear as well, before a command from a caller without the channel; the owner's DMA written and started with interrupts off, so no command is left waiting for its engine where such a caller would wait for it until its bound; and a data-port transfer run with interrupts off whoever the caller is. The test that shows it fixed is the self-build suite under KVM in the full pool, three sets, and a host test of the driver over a model drive that shows DRQ during a DMA and refuses a command then. Fixed the same day, with `tests/host/ata_hosttest.c`, whose five mutations of the driver each fail a row.

104. Two processes could enter the root file system in the middle of each other's operation (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found when the self-build suite's second copy left a node's header overwritten with the file's own text, under KVM in the loaded pool and once under the emulator, and a directory read then walked into that text and faulted; severity P1: a torn ring, silent until read). Run `tests/run selfbuild` in a full pool: `kfs_write`, `kfs_read`, `kfs_truncate`, `kfs_fsync` and `kfs_readdir` in `rach2/sys/fs/vfs.c` took no lock, which they did not need while the disk driver polled, since a process in a system call was not switched away from; the driver sleeps for its commands now, inside a write's sync points, and a second process entering the ring then, to grow a file of its own, moved nodes under the first one's feet, and the first went on writing at the old place. This breaks the file system's contract that a write lands in its own node. Depends on entry 103, whose kept disk showed it. The fix is the mount's lock, the one the namespace operations already hold, taken around the five data operations. The test that shows it fixed is the self-build suite under KVM in the full pool, three sets. Fixed the same day. The lock is then taken in the order it was asked for: a process that released it and took it again at once, the self-build's writer filling a module, kept a waiter from ever taking it, and the zombie probe's victim, waiting to open a file, failed instead, under KVM and on the machine-built kernel. A first form had the release yield to a waiter, and the review found that a switch there reached `open` between its release and the registration of its descriptor, where an unlink could free the node it held; each caller takes a place in a line now and waits its turn, so no new place to switch exists. The deterministic proof came on 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close): `sh tests/host/krffs_root_lock_mutate.sh` extracts `kfs_read`, `kfs_write`, `kfs_truncate`, `kfs_fsync`, `kfs_readdir`, their `_locked` bodies, `ns_my_turn`, `vfs_ns_lock` and `vfs_ns_unlock` unchanged from `vfs.c` and compiles them over the real KRFFS core in `tests/host/fixtures/krffs_root_lock.c`; each operation must hold the mount's lock at every access it makes to the image and leave it free, and, with two threads of which one runs at a time, a holder that lets the lock go while another process waits and asks again at once must take it after the waiter. The host suite runs its `--check`. The whole script removes the lock from each operation in turn and the waiting line from the lock, so that `ns_my_turn` answers from whether the lock is free alone; each fails its row, and each restored copy passes.
105. An init that exited under the debug shell with a child still runnable never returned to the shell (2026-09-18, milestone 52, found in the first acceptance after the review, under KVM, when the zombie probe failed with its writer left running and the default suite then scored the probe as hung and the twenty-three probes after it as never run; severity P1: a hang that costs a whole suite, in the mode every probe suite runs in). Reproduce with a probe that forks a spinning child and exits, on a kernel whose `sys_exit` schedules before the return: the machine prints the child's and the probe's exit records and no prompt. `sys_exit` in `rach2/sys/syscall.c` called `schedule()` before the return to the shell, on the assumption that no other process was ready; a runnable child took the processor, and the exited init, a zombie, was never picked again to make the return. The signal-death path in `rach2/kernel.c` never scheduled. This breaks the debug shell's contract that a program's exit returns the shell. Depends on nothing. The fix: `sys_exit` schedules only for a process that is not init; the init that exited under the debug shell returns at once, and what it left behind is frozen by the shell's resume, as after a signal death, and runs again under the next program the shell starts. That leftover found the second half: its exit wakes no parent, and when the next program is asleep nothing is ready, so the non-init exit's `schedule()` returned and the halt after it, with interrupts off, kept the machine; the proc probe hung that way under the emulator in three sets.
