The exit idles for the next interrupt now and leaves through the scheduler. A first form froze the leftovers and freed their zombies at the next program's start; the review found that a leftover asleep in the disk driver with a mount's lock held would then hold it forever and the next program's lookup would wait on it with the scheduler off, so an init exiting under the debug shell now ends every other user process first, killed and reaped while init still runs. The test that shows it fixed is `survivorprobe` in the default suite, which forks a child that runs until the kernel ends it and exits while it runs: the runner requires the kernel's return record after the probe's exit, which the old schedule never printed. Fixed the same day.
106. The lag wrapper lost a request when two callers entered it together (2026-09-19, milestone 52, found in the acceptance after the review, where the fatwrite suite's writers row failed in all six sets, under both accelerators; severity P1 for the suites: a device declared stalled and a mount gone read-only under a test device every lag row boots). Run `tests/run fatwrite` on a kernel whose mount lock's release yields: `LAGPROBE FAIL: a writer failed` after `block: request deadline: 1 slots quarantined` and `block: device stalled`. `lag_submit` in `rach2/sys/block/lag.c` ran the transfer in the caller's context and marked the queue slot used only afterwards; the ATA driver under it sleeps for a command since entry 102, and a second caller entering meanwhile, the other writer once the lock's release handed the processor over, found the same slot free, took it, and the first caller's return overwrote its request, which was never answered and was quarantined five seconds later. This breaks the wrapper's contract that every request submitted is answered after the lag. Depends on entry 104's hand-off, which made the overlap certain where before it was chance. The fix: the slot is claimed before the transfer, with interrupts off, and is not the tick's to deliver until the transfer has returned. The test that shows it fixed is `tests/host/lag_hosttest.c`, whose backing device enters the wrapper again, or runs the timer, from inside a transfer, and whose rows require both requests answered once and a slot whose transfer has not returned left alone; the committed wrapper fails three of its rows. Fixed the same day.

107. A send on a stream this end had shut down failed with EPERM (2026-09-19, milestone 51, found when Codex wrote send(2) from `socket.c` and reported that a failed stream send with no recorded reason returns a bare -1, which musl reports as `EPERM`; severity P3: a wrong error name, no loss). Reproduce with a connected loopback stream: `shutdown(fd, SHUT_WR)`, then `send`: `EPERM` where POSIX names `EPIPE`; a nonblocking `read` of the same socket answered `EPERM` where the blocking read answers the end of input. `tcp_dead_error` in `rach2/sys/network/socket/socket.c` returned -1 when the block had no `so_error`, and `socket_tcp_try_recv` returned -1 for a socket whose block a shutdown had detached. This breaks the contract of send(2) and read(2) as the pages now state it. Depends on nothing. The fix: the dead-connection error takes the name POSIX gives the case, `EPIPE` for a sender and `ECONNRESET` for a reader, and the detached nonblocking read is the end of input. The test that shows it fixed is the row added to `tests/loopprobe.c` in the default suite, which shuts a client down and requires `EPIPE` from its send and 0 from its read; the previous kernel fails it. Fixed the same day.

108. The recovery image wrote a marker of its fixed address that nothing read (2026-09-19, milestone 51, found when the manual checker asked for a page for `rescue-static` and its sheet was gathered from the script; severity P3: dead code and three stale comments, no loss). `krffs/rescue-static.script` wrote `/var/run/rescue-static` for `bootctl` to read, the lease script removed it on `bound`, `utils/bootctl.c` defined its path, and a drill row proved the lease removed it; commit `99b740e2` had removed the menu pair the marker chose between and the code in `bootctl` that read it, and left the writer, the remover, the define and the row. This breaks the rule that a comment states the current contract: three of them described a reader that did not exist. Depends on nothing. The same commit had left the disk builder's static token set, `RACH2_DISK_STATIC_APPEND` and the `RACH2_STATIC_TOKENS` placeholder no entry carried, and comments in `tools/image/mkdisk_grub.sh` and `tools/inspect/cfgcheck.c` describing entries that come in pairs. The fix removes the marker's write, its removal, the define, the row, the dead token set and the Makefile variable behind it, and the comments now describe what is there: one recovery entry in the menu and one in the core image's configuration, and a script that takes the sealed address when no lease arrives. The test that shows it fixed is `tests/run drill`, which still proves the fixed address is taken and no longer asks for the marker, and the sealed disk's build, which substitutes the placeholders that remain. Fixed the same day.

109. The sealed root had no periodic sync and its init retried a failing exec every second (2026-09-19, milestone 51, found when Codex proofread update(8) against the manifests; severity P2 on the installed machine: data written through the buffer cache waited for a sync nobody asked for, and init forked a child that failed to exec `/bin/update` once a second for the life of the boot). `krffs/manifests/rach2-metal-disk.manifest` staged every other utility but not `update`, which os-real's `exec-dir` and the metal stick's manifest stage; `init` spawns it in every build and respawns it a second after it exits. This breaks the contract of update(8) and of the installed root's durability that the utilities Readme states. Depends on nothing. The fix stages `/bin/update` on the sealed root. The test that shows it fixed is the sealed disk's boot in `tests/run sealed`, whose `ps` now lists `update`, and the manifest check that the image build runs. Fixed the same day.

110. The manual's pages on a KRFFS disk root made every lookup evict the compile's hot set (2026-09-19, milestone 51, found when the first full set's self-build suite ran past its old 295 s to more than 1500 s while every other suite kept its time; severity P1 for the suites, since the set could not close, and a slowdown of every compile on a KRFFS disk root). Run `tests/run selfbuild` under KVM on a tree whose os-real manifest stages the manual: the guest's first kernel build takes more than twenty minutes where it took under five, and without the manual 199 s. A lookup on a KRFFS volume walks every node in the ring (`krffs_find_child` in `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c`), reading each header through the page cache, whose `PC_SLOTS` was 1024 frames, 4 MiB, tuned in milestone 47 for a compile's hot set; the manual's pages added a header page for each of its nodes, the walk's pages and the hot set no longer fit together, and every lookup then refilled the cache from the disk. Rendering each alias as a file, four hundred more nodes, made it worse first, and staging the aliases as one table for a reader script of this project (`krffs/man.script`, BusyBox's man applet no longer built) took those nodes away; the pages alone still crossed the limit. This breaks the rule that a suite's time is the machine's and not the tree's contents. Depends on the ring walk, which stays as it is. The fix gives the page cache 2048 slots, 8 MiB; the self-build suite then runs in 156 s with the manual. The test that showed it fixed was `tests/run selfbuild` under KVM against its recorded time. Fixed the same day. An index over the ring, so that a lookup reads one node and not every one, is the fix that would make the cache size indifferent; it is not built, since it changes the format's lookup, and the count below says when the ring outgrows the cache instead. The proof is a count since 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close): `tests/host/pagecache_ring_hosttest.c` is a model over `pagecache.c` at the slots `pagecache.h` sets. Its ring is the os-real image's header pages with the manual staged, 817 nodes with their headers on 492 pages, 66 of them the manual's, counted from `build/images/os-real.krffs` of 2026-09-21, and its compile has 560 pages of its own, the size at which the ring without the manual fits 1024 slots beside it and the ring with the manual does not. After a warm round, three rounds of the compile's pages and two lookups over the ring at `PC_SLOTS` must read nothing from the backing; two control rows at 1024 slots read nothing without the manual and the whole ring again every round with it. `sh tests/host/pagecache_ring_mutate.sh` sets `PC_SLOTS` to 1024 and the row fails.

111. The ext2 mount read a null mount when the root inode was not a directory (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the ext2 fuzz harness in its first 113 inputs, once the harness stopped answering from the block cache; severity P2: a kernel fault at mount on a damaged or crafted volume, no loss). Reproduce with `tests/fuzz/corpus/ext2/rootfile`, the seed volume with inode 2's mode changed to a regular file, replayed through `build/tests/fuzz/ext2` under the sanitizers, or mounted on the kernel. `ext2_mount_on` in `rach2/sys/fs/ext2.c` refused the root through `ext2_put(mnt, root)` before `mnt->priv` held the mount, and `ext2_put` reaches the mount through `mnt`; the same call stood in the volume check's failure path and the superblock store's. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53: the three paths drop the node through `node_drop(em, root)`, which the mount wrapper's cleanup would also have done, and the input is in the ext2 corpus the host tier replays, which is the test that shows it fixed.

112. The run script for a disk boot named a make target that did not exist (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found in phase 9 when the kernel Readme's claims were read against the tree; severity P3: one documented path could not be followed, no loss). Reproduce with `(cd rach2 && ./run_from_disk.sh)` on a clean tree: the script asks for `build/images/os-real-grub.disk` and says to build it with `make os-real-grub-disk`, which no rule defines, so the BIOS and MBR boot path had no way to be run by hand. The suites build GRUB disks of their own in `tests/system/suites.mk`; the one a person boots was lost when the disk gained its ext2 root and the old KRFFS disk rule went. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53: the root Makefile has `os-real-grub-disk`, which builds the os-real ext2 root on the third partition with the recovery partition beside it, and the kernel Readme names it; the test that shows it fixed is the target building and `run_from_disk.sh` finding its disk. The rule has a test since 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close): the `grub-disk-rule` row of `tests/system/build_rules_test.sh` reads the make database, `make -pn`, of a private copy of the build and requires `os-real-grub-disk` to have a rule that makes the disk and every prerequisite of the disk's rule to be a target or a file of the tree; `--mutate`'s `grub-target` fault renames the rule and the row fails. The target stays, reversing phase 8's removal of it, because `rach2/run_from_disk.sh` is the one path that boots a disk through the BIOS, the MBR and GRUB by hand; no suite boots it and the images target does not build it. Since 2026-09-24 a tree with a recovery key names it `os-real-grub-with-key.disk`, and the script boots whichever name exists. The disk was built on the testing host on 2026-09-25, as `os-real-grub-with-key.disk`, and booted through the BIOS and GRUB 2.12 to its login prompt, on the source of `72b909dc`.

113. A product the build made and a suite's make remade failed the run as an undeclared image (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the close's first full set after a build from clean; severity P2: a set that passes every suite reports failure, and the next set is started to see why). Reproduce with `rm -rf build && make -j32 && tests/run all` on a machine with enough threads: the parallel build leaves `build/images/test.krffs` at the same second as a program it stages, a pooled suite's own make restages it, and the run ends `run: images: undeclared: build/images/test.krffs`, with its `.checked` stamp and its `.d` file beside it, although all 88 suites passed. The check in `tests/run` asks whether a file made under `build/images` belongs to a suite of the run, and a product of the run's own target belongs to none. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53: `check_images` skips the products `take_products` recorded, and the dependency file make writes beside each, since `check_hashes` is what proves a product did not change; the runner's self-test has a suite that remakes a product, which must pass, and one that writes a file no product covers, which must still fail the run by name.

114. The disk rules named tools that expanded to nothing, and the KRFFS tools were built without the warning policy (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the build; severity P3: a changed image tool did not rebuild the disks that use it, and three host programs compiled without `-Werror`). Reproduce the first with `make -pn | grep '^build/images/rach2-sealed.disk:'`: the rule's prerequisites held no `mkmbr`, `fatchains` or `grubembed`, because `DISK_TOOLS` is defined in `mk/images.mk`, which the root Makefile included after the rules that name it, and a prerequisite list expands where it is read. Reproduce the second with `make -n -C krffs`: the compile line had `-g -std=gnu99` and none of `mk/host.mk`'s `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, since `krffs/Makefile` sets its own flags and includes no host policy. Both depend on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53. The first fix included the image fragment before the rules that use its variables; that made the recovery ISO's rule lose its target and fixed the stick's rule to the unkeyed name, so `0a137fcd` put the include back at the end of the root Makefile, after the recovery ISO, the sealed disk and the tools are named, which its own rules need, and defined the tools the disk rules name before those rules, which gives the two disk rules their three tool prerequisites each (`dev-log/53-audit/notes.md:110`); `krffs/Makefile` includes `mk/host.mk` and adds `HOSTCFLAGS` to its own, which found a signed comparison in `fsviz.krffs.c`; the one object compiled from musl's vendored crypt is exempt by name, since a pinned tarball's code is not this project's to warn about. The test that shows it fixed came on 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close): the `disk-tools` and `images-include` rows of `tests/system/build_rules_test.sh`, whose `--mutate` faults `tools-late` and `include-early` put back each wrong order and fail their row.

115. A process could halt the machine by forging a segment selector in its own signal frame (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the process arm; severity P1: any unprivileged process can stop the whole machine, and a machine on the network can be stopped by anyone who can run a program on it). Reproduce with `tests/sigprobe`'s fourth row: a handler writes `0xFFFF` into the saved `gs` of the frame the kernel built for it and returns. `safe_user_seg` in `rach2/sys/syscall_proc.c` kept any selector whose low two bits were 3, and `isr_return` loads `gs`, `fs`, `es` and `ds` with `mov` while still at ring 0, so a selector whose index is past the GDT's seven entries raises a general-protection fault in kernel mode, which the exception handler panics on. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53: the coercion keeps only the two selectors this kernel has, flat user data and the per-process TLS entry, and gives flat user data for anything else. Proved by mutation on the testing host: with the check back to the RPL alone `tests/run default` reports `sigprobe no shell return (hang?) FAIL`, which is the machine stopping; with the fix it passes, and restored it passes again.

116. The socket calls answer for the first interface whatever the socket is bound to (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the network arm; severity P3 on a machine with one card, P2 on one with two: a program that reads its own address or binds to a card gets the wrong answer and sends on the wrong one). Four faults of one shape, each read in `rach2/sys/syscall_net.c`. `SO_BINDTODEVICE` checks the name and then stores a boolean, `entry->sock->bound_device = 1`, so a socket bound to `eth1` transmits through and receives from `eth0`. A packet socket's `getsockname` writes `NETDEV_IFINDEX` and `net_mac` whatever the socket was bound to. An IPv4 `getsockname` reports `net_ip_addr` although `tcp_connect` stores the routed interface's address in the control block. And `accept` refuses a raw socket by type but waits on the accept queue of a datagram socket or of a stream that never listened, so such a call blocks until it is interrupted instead of answering `EINVAL`. To reproduce, boot with two cards (`tests/run e1000` attaches the second) and read `/proc/net` beside what a socket answers. Implemented on `fix-116` from `c52d6aa3`; the fix is uncommitted as requested, so its commit is pending. `SO_BINDTODEVICE` stores the registered index and reads back that interface's name. Packet `getsockname` reads the packet binding and reports its index and MAC, or index zero with no hardware address for an unbound or all-interface socket. IPv4 `getsockname` snapshots the TCP control block's local address under the network lock when one exists. `accept` returns `EINVAL` for datagrams and streams that are not listening, preserving `EOPNOTSUPP` for raw sockets. `netifprobe second` adds rows for each contract, including active and accepted TCP connections on the second address. The affected kernel objects compiled with `i686-elf-gcc -ansi -Werror`, and the guest probe compiled. `tests/run netif` stopped before its build because the sandbox refused TCP, UDP and Unix socket binds; no guest result is claimed. The native `netif_hosttest` compiles the real syscall handlers and packet bindings against two model interfaces. `sh tests/host/netif_mutate.sh` passed, then separately compiled six mutations: the device index forced to one, the option name forced to eth0, the packet index forced to one, the packet MAC taken from eth0, the TCP address replaced by the primary address, and the accept guard removed. Each exited 1 at its named assertion under AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer. After each mutation the script restored and touched its private source copy and explicitly recompiled; all six restored runs passed. Its trap stops the test child before restoring the source. The entry's traffic claim was wrong: no transmit or receive path reads `bound_device`, so the old boolean did not route traffic through eth0. The option remains bookkeeping only, as `setsockopt(2)` documents; packet traffic is selected by `bind` with `sockaddr_ll`. The two-card probe belongs to `tests/run netif`, not `tests/run e1000` or `netif-e1000`. Taken and committed here, in `72711d6e`, with `08035b93` for the probe; `tests/run netif netif-e1000 dgram packet socket` passes, and the probe's own invalid-accept row opens its sockets without the flag this kernel refuses, which is entry 137's neighbour. This entry is closed.

117. A blocking connect can wait out a handshake that succeeded, and a stale framebuffer descriptor can take the display from its owner (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the ABI and network arms; severity P3: two paths a program can be left in, neither reached by the images this project ships). `connect_done` in `rach2/sys/syscall_net.c` accepts only `TCP_ESTABLISHED`, so a peer whose FIN arrives before the caller is scheduled leaves the connection in `TCP_CLOSE_WAIT` and the caller asleep with its handshake timer already stopped; the check after the wait then reports `ECONNREFUSED` for a connection that was made. `FB_MODE_TEXT` in `rach2/sys/syscall.c` leaves graphics mode and clears the owner on any descriptor of the framebuffer's type, so a process holding a stale descriptor takes the display from the process that owns it, and `FB_MODE_13H` on a descriptor still open after that answers success without putting the mode back. The monotonic clock is the third of this shape: `clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC)` divides the 32-bit tick count, so the clock it reports steps backwards about every 497 days. All three predate this milestone and depend on nothing. Implemented on `fix2-117` from `72711d6e`; the fix is uncommitted as requested, so its commit is pending. Connect completion accepts `CLOSE_WAIT` in the wait predicate and both blocking and nonblocking results. Framebuffer mode and palette requests require the caller to own the display; mode 13h can reacquire an unowned display and actually enters graphics mode. The monotonic clock snapshots the existing uptime seconds and subsecond ticks with interrupts masked, retaining the signed 32-bit seconds ABI. `loopprobe` adds immediate close after accept and requires successful connect followed by EOF; `devprobe` checks graphics reentry, an inherited descriptor and a stale descriptor across two owners. Proved locally in this worktree with `sh tests/host/syscall_mutate.sh`: the real handlers pass under ASan and UBSan; six separately compiled mutations remove the connect wait, blocking result and nonblocking result fixes, framebuffer ownership and reentry, and the uptime clock read; each fails its named assertion, and each restored source is touched, recompiled and passes. `sh tests/system/framebuffer_mutate.sh` passes both guest probes in QEMU from a checked 2 MiB image, fails `devprobe` with each framebuffer mutation compiled into the kernel, then restores, touches and rebuilds the source; both probes pass again and each restored kernel matches the fixed kernel. Both scripts restore source in a trap after stopping test processes and retain full logs and artifact checksums under `build/syscall-proof.*` and `build/framebuffer-guest.*`. The kernel builds with `i686-elf-gcc -ansi -Werror` and links without libgcc. The entry counted two faults but described three. The loopback row alone does not force FIN before the client resumes; the native wait model forces that order. The 497-day rollover is checked with injected counters, not elapsed guest uptime. No full test set or hardware run is claimed. Taken and committed here, in `8fa19da2` as `tests/host/syscall_hosttest.c` and `tests/system/framebuffer_mutate.sh`, renamed from the entry number they were written under; `tests/run default console socket` passes. This entry is closed.

118. The fixture makers' readers crash on the volumes they are meant to judge (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the makers; severity P2: a host tool the file system tests rely on dies on a damaged image instead of naming the fault). Four faults in the readers. `tests/host/mkexfatfixture.c`'s `--read` copies a path component into a 600-byte buffer without bounding it, so a component of 600 bytes writes past it, and it trusts a contiguous subdirectory's first cluster and length without checking the extent, so a damaged image reads outside the allocation. `tests/host/mkfatfixture.c`'s long-name check hands its decoder `lfn_pending * 13` units although its buffer holds 260, and the sequence validator accepts ordinals past the twenty the writes are bounded to, so a crafted twenty-one entry sequence reads past a stack buffer. `mkexfatfixture --read` also stops at `ValidDataLength` and reports success, so a file grown by truncate exports shorter than its recorded size. They depend on nothing. Fixed in the `fix-118` working tree based on `c52d6aa3` and committed in `72711d6e`. Path components are compared in place, contiguous directory extents are checked against the heap before allocation or access, and exports write `DataLength` bytes with zeros after `ValidDataLength`. FAT long names require one start entry and ordinals from one through twenty. Repeated starts can also inflate the decoder count and have their own rows. `exfatcheck_hosttest` and `fatcheck_hosttest` compile the fixture tools under ASan and UBSan and require the expected bytes or a normal refusal with its diagnostic. Proved in that worktree by `sh tests/host/fixture_readers_mutate.sh`: both fixed tests pass; five separately compiled mutations restore the path copy, remove the extent check, shorten the export, remove the ordinal bound, and allow repeated starts, each failing its named row; every source is then restored, touched, rebuilt, and passes again. Bounds mutations report invalid memory accesses under the sanitizers. The script restores source in a trap after stopping test processes and keeps complete logs and source and binary checksums under `build/fixture-readers-proof.*`. No guest suite is needed for these host readers. The original entry counted three faults but described four; all four are reproduced. The host tier runs both tests. This entry is closed.

119. A directory read with a buffer too small for one record kept the description locked for ever (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the file system arm; severity P2: a program that asks once with a short buffer can never read that file again, and nor can anything holding a copy of its descriptor). Reproduce on a mounted volume with `getdents64(fd, buf, 1)`: the refusal returns `EINVAL` without releasing the lock `fd_ofd_lock` took, so the next read of that description waits in an uninterruptible sleep for a lock nothing will release, holding the mount with it. `mountprobe` has the row now: the short read must answer `EINVAL` and the listing after it must still work. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53; proved by mutation on the testing host, where the description kept on that path fails `tests/run mount` at the mountprobe boot and the fix passes it.

120. A disk's ioctl asked of any other device read past the device table (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the kernel faults this milestone fixed; severity P2: an unprivileged `ioctl` on an ordinary device reads kernel memory outside the table and can fault). Reproduce with `ioctl(open("/dev/null", O_RDONLY), 0x125F, 0)`: `dev_ioctl` dispatches `BLKRRPART` for every device, and `disk_node_of_fd` in `rach2/sys/fs/devfs.c` cast the descriptor's record to a disk node without asking whether it was one, so `node->part` read past the entry and `disk_rescan` could be given an unrelated pointer. The accessor had checked the record's operations before this milestone's move of the device from a descriptor's `priv` to a field of its own. `devprobe` has the row now: the request must answer `ENOTTY`. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53. That row cannot fail without the check; entry 207 added the one that does.

121. A one-shot signal handler jumped to address zero instead of running (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the process arm's comments, which claimed the reset was for the next signal; severity P2: a handler installed with `SA_RESETHAND` never runs and the process dies of the jump). Reproduce with `sigprobe`'s fifth row: install a handler with `SA_RESETHAND` and raise its signal. `signal_setup_frame` in `rach2/sys/syscall_proc.c` cleared `sa->handler` for the reset and then took `tf->eip` from the field it had just cleared, so the first delivery entered zero. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53: the handler this delivery enters is taken before the reset, and `sigprobe` requires a one-shot handler to run on its signal.

122. A signal frame gives an SA_SIGINFO handler a context smaller than the one it reads (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the same reading; severity P3: a handler that reads its `ucontext_t` reads the frame's own saved floating-point image, which is its own memory and not another process's). The frame's `uc` field is 128 bytes and musl's `ucontext_t` is larger, so a handler that inspects the third argument reads past it into the frame. Nothing this project ships reads it, and the read stays inside the process's own stack. It depends on the pinned musl i386 layout. Fixed in a worktree based on `c52d6aa3` and committed in `72711d6e`: `sig_frame.uc` reserves all 348 bytes of musl 1.2.5's `ucontext_t`, still zeroed, before the saved floating-point image; `fpuprobe` uses the image's new offset. `musl/sigmusl` reads every context byte in an `SA_SIGINFO` handler, checks the zeroed placeholder and returns through musl's restorer; the default suite now runs it. Proved in that worktree with `sh tests/system/sigcontext_mutate.sh`: the fixed kernel passes under FXSR and `rach2.nofxsr`, rebuilding with `uc[128]` fails both at byte 128, and the source restored, touched and rebuilt passes both again with a kernel identical to the first. The script's trap stops the test processes before restoring the source. Focused `sigprobe` and `fpuprobe` runs through the serial harness also pass in both modes. The entry's size fault and restriction to the process's own stack were confirmed. This entry is closed.
123. An unreadable `/sbin/init` dropped the machine to the debug shell (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the boot path's comments, which claimed the fall-through was for an image without an init; severity P1: anyone at the console of a machine with a damaged disk reaches a kernel shell that authenticates nobody and can write raw sectors). Reproduce by making the root's `/sbin/init` unreadable, an I/O error or a symbolic link loop, and booting without `rach2.rescue`: `boot_init_process` in `rach2/kernel.c` returned on any `vfs_resolve` failure and the caller made the debug shell the supervisor. It breaks the refusal the same file already makes for a root that did not mount. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53: only `-ENOENT` falls through, and any other failure prints the refusal and halts, the way `refuse_unmounted_root` does. The row came on 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close): `tests/system/init_refusal.sh`, run by `tests/run bootline`, boots an ext2 root whose `/sbin/init` is a symbolic link to itself and requires the refusal naming ELOOP, -40, and no shell in the ten seconds after it; `sh tests/system/init_refusal_mutate.sh` puts back the old fall-through, `rc != 0` for `rc == -ENOENT`, and the row fails with the debug shell reached.

124. The recovery shell's disk round-trip overwrote a GPT disk's backup header (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the same reading; severity P2: on a GPT disk the write destroys the backup partition table, which only a repair tool restores). Reproduce by running `disk write` in the recovery kernel's debug shell on a disk whose LBA 1 holds `EFI PART`: the command wrote the last sector, which the comment called scratch space past every partition, and on a GPT disk that sector is the backup header. It breaks the shell's own claim that the round-trip disturbs no file system. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 53: the command reads LBA 1 and refuses a GPT disk by name. The row came on 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close): `tests/system/gpt_write_refusal.sh`, run by `tests/run parts` on the recovery kernel, boots the parts suite's GPT disks and requires the refusal, no round-trip and every byte of each disk as it was; `sh tests/system/gpt_write_mutate.sh` takes the refusal out, where the row must fail on the GPT disk with no protective MBR (entry 206); it passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25. Writing the row found that a disk whose primary header is gone was still written; that is entry 172, and the row has a third disk for it.

125. Two KRFFS directories could hold one identifier (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex review of the file systems' comments, which claimed the identifier was unique; severity P2: two directories share their children, so a file made in one is reached through the other). Reproduce by making `/bC` and `/cb`, whose names give the same DJB hash, 0x596ee4, and so the same mixed identifier, and then a file under one: `krffs_mix_id` in `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c` mixes the parent's identifier with the name's 64-bit DJB hash, and a child names its parent by identifier alone. The hash was called 32-bit here and in the source until 2026-09-24. It breaks the hierarchy the v2 format's `parent_id` scheme describes. It depends on nothing, and widening the identifier would change what an existing image resolves. Fixed in milestone 53 by refusing the second name: `krffs_mkdir_at` looks for the identifier on the medium before it makes the directory. A fix that keeps both names needs a format change and belongs with the next one. The row came on 2026-09-24 (milestone 06's close): `id_collision_rows` in `tests/host/krffs_hosttest.c` make `/bC` with a child `kept` and require `mkdir /cb` to be refused, `/cb` and `/cb/kept` to resolve to nothing, `/bC/kept` to resolve, and the root to list `bC` alone; `krffs_id_mutate.sh`'s `id-taken` fault removes the refusal and the row fails. The host image stager made such a pair without a word; that is entry 171.

126. Closed in source: HDA rate encoding and codec capabilities (2026-09-21; found 2026-09-20 in milestone 53; severity P3). The finding was real: 32000 was encoded as 44100 and 96000 as 48000, while the recorded rate retained the request. The driver now reads each programmed DAC's effective PCM and stream-format masks, using the audio function group unless Format Override is set, and intersects the two DACs when both are programmed. All twelve advertised rates have exact encodings. The write path resamples to a supported rate; callbacks reject unsupported rates. `sh tests/host/hda_mutate.sh` passes the real driver against a model codec under ASan and UBSan; separate compiled faults in the 32000 and 96000 encodings, capability check, inheritance and fallback each fail a named row, and restored, touched and rebuilt sources pass. `sh tests/system/hda_mutate.sh` runs the staged diagnostic in QEMU and reads `requested 32000 programmed 32000 format 0x0A11 dac-format 0x0A11`; a compiled removal of the proc path fails its open row, and restoration passes with the original kernel bytes. The metal RAM, metal disk and os-real manifests now stage `audiotest`; `/proc/audio` reports capabilities and the last programmed rate and format readbacks. On a system updated with these changes, `audiotest RATE 2 440` and `cat /proc/audio` work over SSH without `rach2.rescue` or a person at the console. Read on the Lenovo on 2026-09-21 over SSH, on the kernel of `b984b33c` installed through `bootctl`: its codec is `0x14F1510F`, whose `/proc/audio` capability word is `pcm 0x000A0560`, which names 44.1, 48, 96 and 192 kHz at 16 and 24 bits, and `audiotest` left `requested 48000 programmed 48000 format 0x0011 dac-format 0x0011 hp-format 0x0011`, a rate and a width that word advertises. The owner was at the machine and heard that tone from its speakers, loudly enough to startle them, so the path from the encoder to the codec's output is confirmed on the hardware. What no reading and no listener judged is the frequency the codec actually clocked out; a measurement at each advertised rate is still the only way to see that, and nothing in the tree depends on it.
127. Closed in source: HDA connection-list decoding and routing (2026-09-21; found 2026-09-20 in milestone 53; severity P3). The finding was real: the shared connection reader sent a response-word number as the entry offset, truncated long entries and did not expand ranges. It now reads offsets in steps of four or two, preserves fifteen-bit long NIDs and expands ranges in selector order. Discovery and playback use those indices, reject malformed or over-capacity lists, and do not substitute an unrelated DAC. NIDs requiring indirect addressing are retained in lists but cannot alias a directly addressed widget. `sh tests/host/hda_mutate.sh` compiles short-offset, long-offset, truncation, range and selector-programming faults; each fails its named model-codec row and each restored build passes. `/proc/audio` now exposes the selected primary and headphone paths, the optional mixer, expanded indices, and the selected widgets' packed and expanded connection lists. Those records can be compared over SSH on an updated system; the former claim that nothing about audio could be read remotely no longer describes this source tree. The guest mutation proof also covers directory and stat identity, short reads, snapshot lifetime and the no-audio report. Read on the Lenovo on 2026-09-21 over SSH, on the kernel of `b984b33c`: `/proc/audio` reports `connections nid 23 long 0 raw 0x10 0x11` and the same for nid 22, each expanding to `16 17`, and the primary pin 23 and the headphone pin 22 both select dac 16, which is in those lists. So the reader's expansion and the path it chooses agree on that codec, and the SSH transport carried both readings. Output through the speakers is judged: the owner heard `audiotest`'s tone from them on that boot. The headphone pin was configured and not listened to, since nothing was plugged into it.
128. A runner selftest row judges by the clock (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by running the selftest on a loaded machine; severity P3: the row fails on a busy host and passes on a quiet one, which is a false failure and not a fault of the runner). Reproduce by running `sh tests/system/selftest.sh` while a build runs: the row at `tests/system/selftest.sh` that requires a stopped set to have ended within four seconds failed here with a compile running beside it and passed alone on the next run. It breaks the rule that a suite asserts on a count and not on the clock. It depends on nothing. Fixed in `f5a12bef`, which removed the four-second assertion and checked suite markers; the entry had not recorded that change. Completed in the uncommitted worktree based on `72711d6e`: the selftest calls `selftest_stop.sh`, whose FIFO fixtures replace the remaining two-second sleep. The failing suite waits for the other active suite to start, and only the queued suite can release that suite to finish normally. Eight checks require the runner's failure status and stopped and unstarted reports, both active starts, no queued start and no normal end. Proved with `sh tests/system/selftest_stop.sh --mutate`: the fixed helper passes all eight; a compiled mutation suppressing the child's failure status fails six; removing the shell runner's stop-on-failure assignment fails five while the run still returns failure; each source restoration, touched and rebuilt, passes all eight with a helper identical to the first. The trap stops the runner before restoring private sources. Logs, events, helper copies and checksums are in `build/selftest-stop.V31AVZ/`, with the complete proof in `build/review-proof/128.log`. The sandbox denied process listing; cleanup through recorded groups completed. No guest or full suite was run, and no commit was made. Taken and committed here; the runner suite passes in every set since. This entry is closed.

129. The USB latency rows judge by the clock (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found by the closing Codex round reading `tests/system/Readme.md` against the suite; severity P3: the rows fail on a host slower than the one whose numbers they hold, which is a false failure and not a fault of the kernel). Reproduce by running `tests/run usb` on a loaded machine: two rows of `tests/system/suites/usb.sh` read the microseconds `rach2.usblatency` stamps and require the handler's entry within 1000 us of the interrupt and the read within 2000 us of the wakeup. Those are absolute times and not a multiple of a baseline the same boot measured, which the rule on suites asks for, and the system tests' Readme claimed no suite judged by the clock at all. It depends on nothing. Seen twice in this milestone's close on the testing host, both times on a machine that had just built something: the reader's row read 10598 us against its 2000, and the same row passed twice on a quiet host minutes later, on the same kernel and on the ordinary one. Implemented in the uncommitted `fix2-129` worktree on base `72711d6e0fe2`; no fixing commit exists yet. The two rows use one checker and count work before the blocked console reader is dispatched: no intervening USB pump beside the user-mode spinner, and at most 4096 completed bytes beside `getrandom`. Timer pumps during that call are diagnostic, since one service interval may take several interrupts on a slow host. Times remain diagnostic, and the recorder no longer drops reads after 20 ms. The reader is armed with interrupts masked through wait-queue registration, and the scheduler freezes the counts when it selects that reader. `sh tests/system/usb_latency_test.sh --mutate` compiles the production recorder and scheduler with host substitutes, passes records whose entry offset is -30000 us and whose wake-to-read interval is 80000 us through the same checker, rejects excess work, excess pumps, absent dispatch and malformed records, then proves two compiled mutations. Restoring the 20 ms cutoff fails the slow-record assertions and checker; removing the dispatch hook fails the dispatch assertions and checker. Each restored source is touched, recompiled and passes; the script's trap stops the active test before restoring its private sources. The focused scheduler and terminal tests pass under ASan and UBSan, and the C89 kernel links under `-Werror` without libgcc and passes the ELF check. The original description of the first interval was wrong: entry is relative to the software drain, not to the controller raising an interrupt; the controller supplies no timestamp. Guest acceptance remains open: QEMU's Unix monitor socket is refused with `Operation not permitted` in this sandbox, so `RACH2_USB_ROWS=latency tests/run usb` and a guest mutation removing immediate preemption or the `getrandom` service point still need to run in an environment that permits that socket. Taken here with two corrections its own proof could not see, both found by `tests/run usb` on the testing host: the record printed `%u`, which this kernel's formatter does not have, so the counts read as the letter `u`; and the checker compared fields of a line that ends in the console's carriage return, so the last count never looked like a number. With the conversions and the carriage return fixed the suite passes. Taken and committed here, in `c004be7c`; `tests/run usb` passes. This entry is closed. The guest mutation the entry owed is `tests/system/usb_latency_mutate.sh` (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close): it builds harness kernels from a private copy of `rach2/` and `mk/` and runs the two rows of `usb_latency.sh` on each; without the preemption that follows a device interrupt, both `sched_preempt()` calls of `rach2/sys/isr.c` removed, the spin row must fail its count of pumps with `usb-test: invalid dispatch record: spin`, and without the service point in `sys_getrandom` the random row must fail its count of bytes with `usb-test: invalid dispatch record: random`; each restored source must pass both rows with a kernel equal to the first. It passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25.

130. The install stick cannot feed the documented root update (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found in the sitting; severity P2: the update path `recovery(7)` names cannot be run from the stick the build produces). Reproduce by booting the install stick on the notebook and trying `rach2-install --update IMAGE /dev/disk0`: the command takes a 1 GiB ext2 root image as a file, and there is nowhere on the machine to put one. The recovery system's RAM root has 208 KB free, the stick's exFAT volume is full to 1.8 MB because it carries the whole sealed disk, the recovery image stages no `loopctl` and publishes no loop nodes, so the root inside that sealed disk cannot be opened as a file, and the only writable space is the root partition the update would overwrite while reading from it. It breaks `rach2-install(8)`'s update path and phase 13's order. It depends on the stick's layout. The test that shows it fixed is a stick whose volume carries `rach2-metal-disk.ext2` beside the sealed disk, the 60 GB the stick leaves unpartitioned being room enough, and `rach2-install --update` run from it on the machine. Closed on 2026-09-21: `mk/images.mk` stages `rach2-metal-disk.ext2` beside the sealed disk and depends on both images and the recipe. The owning Readmes and both manual pages describe the mounted-stick source. `make rach2` passes under `-ansi -Werror`. `sh tests/system/install_stick_test.sh build/images/rach2-install-stick.img build/images/rach2-sealed.disk build/images/rach2-metal-disk.ext2 build/fstick-proof/product` passes: the product stick boots through USB, its staged root equals the 1 GiB build input, and recovery updates a disk image from that file, checks readback, preserves a local group and replaces an old root marker. Bulk transfers use IDE in QEMU. `sh tests/system/install_stick_mutate.sh` passes with a 64 MiB root, fails when the rebuilt stick omits that file, then passes after the private Make rule is restored, touched and rebuilt; the restored stick equals the first. Logs are under `build/fstick-proof/` and the mutation script prints its artifact directory. A later Lenovo sitting must confirm the physical stick boots, the update reads its image over USB, and the updated root boots with the installed credentials. No hardware was written for this proof. This entry is closed. The stick's boot is confirmed: on 2026-09-21 the development drive was written with `rach2-install-stick-with-key.img` from `0799e9c5`, plugged into the Lenovo and booted. The recovery system came up on its RAM root with the disk policy read-write, took a DHCP address and answered SSH on the key the image carries. `rach2-recover` listed the machine: `/dev/disk0` as a Samsung SSD 870 EVO 250GB, serial S6P2NX0T381638Z, 250059350016 bytes, its three partitions at 2048, 133120 and 266240 as the table records them, and `/dev/disk1` as the drive it booted from, with the ISO at sector 1 for 13343 and the exFAT volume at 14336 for 4986880. The kernel mounted that volume over real USB, listed the three staged files, and unmounted with 10 opens and 10 releases. The update ran on the machine on 2026-09-24, reading the root image from the stick over USB: its first run stopped at entry 161 and its resumed run dropped the SSH keys, entry 162, and with both fixed the final installer's update ran on the normal path and kept the account files and root's key. The accepted kernel was then written with `bootctl`, tried and committed, and the machine is reached over SSH with the keys it had before (`dev-log/06-review/results-2026-09-20.md`, the sitting of 2026-09-24 and what was accepted).

131. The saved sectors do not fit on the install stick (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found in the sitting; severity P3: the rule that a disk write is undoable from the stick alone is not met, though the stick can still restore the whole disk from its sealed image). Reproduce by saving the boot and recovery partitions, 64 MiB each, onto the booted stick: its volume has 1.8 MB free. The saves went to the Mac instead, where each was hashed against what the machine reads. It breaks the hardware rule's requirement that a copy of every sector about to be overwritten is saved on the host and on that stick. It depends on entry 130, since both are the stick's layout. The test that shows it fixed is a stick with room for the saves and a restore performed from the stick with no host. Closed on 2026-09-21: the product rule adds 256 MiB for metadata and saved sectors. The rebuilt product volume has 259600 KiB available after both images are staged. The QEMU row in `tests/system/install_stick_test.sh`, also called by `tests/run installer`, saves both complete 64 MiB partitions there before the update, erases both afterwards, then boots recovery again and restores solely from those files with no host network connection. Every saved and restored byte matches the original partitions, and the exFAT check passes. `sh tests/system/install_stick_mutate.sh` passes, fails the guest space check with a rebuilt four-MiB allowance, then passes after restoration, touch and rebuild; both restored images equal the first. Its trap stops test processes before restoring the private rule. The proof uses IDE for bulk transfers and covers these two partitions, not a backup of a grown root. A later Lenovo sitting must confirm both saves fit on the physical stick, verify their host copies before a write, and restore and read them back from the stick without a host supplying data. This entry is closed. Two of those three are confirmed. On 2026-09-21 the boot and recovery partitions, 67108864 bytes each, were written onto the booted stick's volume through the kernel's exFAT writer at 16.5 MB a second, and each read back with the same SHA-256 as the same range read from the disk; 127516 KiB of the volume remained free. The host copies are wider than the two partitions: the whole save set an install would overwrite, 1856 ranges and 3173597184 bytes computed by `growset --lba 266240` over the image's ext2 head against 488130928 sectors, was copied to `build/machine-save/2026-09-21/` with its ranges and a manifest, and the same ranges read again on the machine hash to the same `4d0e0894de9d5b12872e3b71b14380de60de134d9a49c27612781bf7ba22e00a`. The restore from the stick writes the disk, so it waits for a sitting with the owner present. The third is no longer owed: on 2026-09-24 the owner made the Lenovo and the flash drive a sandbox, rewritten with no backup, restore or hash taken for their safety (CLAUDE.md, Hardware), which retires the rule this entry served.

132. A stick built on one machine cannot be reached from the other (2026-09-20, milestone 53, found in the sitting; severity P3: the sitting stopped until the private key was copied between machines). Reproduce by building the install stick on the testing host and booting it with the Mac's key: the recovery system offers `publickey` and refuses. `krffs/gen_demo_key.sh` makes a key where it runs and the key files are outside the repository, so the two machines hold different ones and each image trusts only its own. It breaks nothing the manual promises, but it makes an image built on one machine unusable from the other. It depends on nothing. Fixed in the uncommitted worktree based on `72711d6e`: `make recovery-key RECOVERY_EXTRA_KEYS=/path/operator.pub` replaces the recovery list with the builder's root keys and the operator's public keys, validates each extra key, and publishes only a changed, complete list. The default command preserves an existing recovery list. `tools/Readme.md` gives the two-command stick build; the private key stays on the operator's host. `sh tests/system/recovery_key_test.sh --mutate` passes on the host: a 1 MiB KRFFS image staged from the recovery manifest's key directive contains both identities; omitting the operator key fails the extracted-image assertion; restored, touched sources and a rebuilt stager and image pass. A second mutation, compiled into the stager, truncates the staged list after the builder's key and fails the same assertion; restoration and recompilation pass with the original executable and image bytes. The script restores its private sources in a trap, and the style suite runs its fixed rows. Logs are in `build/entry132-proof.log` and `build/recovery-key-test.zyYtbz/`. Shell syntax, ShellCheck and focused prose checks pass. The entry's key mismatch is confirmed, but the files are inside the checkout and ignored by Git, not outside it; recovery also uses a separate preserved list, and the demo generator already accepts extra public keys. This proof covers image contents, not an SSH login or a physical stick. No commit was made. Taken and committed here, in `06d48efa`; the style suite runs the key rows. This entry is closed.

133. A reader asleep on a stream another process shut down never woke (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 4; severity P1: the reader blocks for ever and only a signal ends it). Reproduce by forking with a connected stream descriptor, letting the child block in `recv` on an empty ring, and calling `shutdown(SHUT_RDWR)` in the parent: `tcp_close` in `rach2/sys/network/tcp/tcp.c` detaches the socket from its control block both ways, so no later answer from the peer can reach that socket's wait queue, and nothing woke it. `socket_tcp_recv` already handles a control block that went while it slept, which is the wake this was missing. `socket_poll` had the same hole: a shut-down stream reported no events at all. It breaks `shutdown(2)`'s promise of end of input and the readiness contract in `rach2/sys/io/Readme.md`. It depends on nothing. Fixed in milestone 06's second run: the shutdown wakes the socket's waiters after `tcp_close`, and `socket_poll` answers `POLLHUP` with the caller's read and write bits for a stream whose block has gone. `tests/loopprobe.c` has the row: a forked reader parked on the accepted end, a `SHUT_RDWR` under it, and the child required to read the end of input and exit zero. Proved by mutation on the testing host: with the wake removed and the kernel rebuilt, `tests/run default` fails with `loopprobe no shell return (hang?)`; restored and rebuilt, it passes.

134. A signal posted as a process went to sleep was not acted on until the deadline (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 1; severity P2: a handled signal takes up to the whole sleep to arrive, and a `nanosleep` of a minute answers a minute late). `sys_nanosleep` in `rach2/sys/syscall.c` set `wakeup_tick` and then `PROC_BLOCKED` with interrupts on, and `signal_post` in `rach2/sys/syscall_proc.c` wakes only a process already blocked, so a signal that arrived between the two statements was recorded and left. `proc_wake_sleepers` looks at the deadline and not at pending signals, so nothing else took it up. It breaks the promise in `rach2/sys/proc/signal.Readme.md` that an acting signal interrupts a blocking wait. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the pending check and the transition to `PROC_BLOCKED` run inside `kirq_save`, so a signal either is seen before the block or arrives to a process already blocked. The fix is read against `signal_post` and the kernel builds and passes `tests/run default`, but it has no row of its own yet: a row written here hung, because a lost wake still ends in the handler running at the deadline, so status alone cannot tell the two apart and the difference is a time. The row it owed is here. `/proc/stat` reports `sleeps_interrupted`, the count `sys_nanosleep` raises where it returns `EINTR` with time still left, so a sleep a signal cut short raises it and one that ran its whole length does not. `sigprobe` forks a child that asks for thirty seconds, signals it a tenth of a second later, and requires both the child's own call to have answered `EINTR` and that count to have risen; neither assertion is a time. The masking itself is checked natively: `tests/host/syscall_hosttest.c` compiles the real handler against a model that records the mask of its first pending question, and `sh tests/host/syscall_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails at `the pending check runs with interrupts masked` with the mask taken off the check and the block, and passes again restored, rebuilt and byte-identical. The second review of entry 215 later found, as entry 221 records, that the native row could not see the order of the block and the unmask, and that the signal could land before the child slept; the row now requires the process blocked when the mask is lifted, and `sigprobe` waits until the child is asleep before it signals. This entry is closed.

135. Vendor builds misread a long Make option as dry-run mode (2026-09-20, milestone 06, second review run, phase 6; severity P2: named suites cannot start in a worktree without the vendor outputs). On macOS GNU Make 3.81, run `tests/run host` or `tests/run style` with BusyBox, Dropbear, TinyCC and pdpmake absent. `tests/run:462` passes `--no-print-directory`; the stamp recipes at `Makefile:161`, `183`, `191`, `203`, `212` and `225` search the first word of `MAKEFLAGS` for `n`, match that long option, print `dry run`, and skip the vendor build. The output checks then fail. This breaks the root Readme's named-suite procedure. Depends on the Make flag parser, not the eight phase 6 fixes. Fixed here: the test is in `mk/dryrun.mk`, included by the root Makefile and read by the six vendor stamps as `$(DRY_RUN)`. The first word of MAKEFLAGS is taken only when it is not an option, which is what says it holds the short flags, and the long forms and a detached `-n` are matched as whole words beside it. That second half is needed: GNU Make 3.81 writes ` --no-print-directory -n` for `make -n --no-print-directory`, so a rule that read the first word alone would call a real dry run a build and run the vendor script it was asked to describe. `sh tests/system/dryrun_test.sh --mutate` proves it on this make: the five flag rows pass, the first-word rule put back in the private copy skips a build it should have run, and the restored rule passes every row again. The style suite runs the fixed rows.

136. The FUSE reader's host test needs a header the testing host has not got (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 6; severity P3: the host tier does not build, and nothing of the system is at risk). Reproduce by running `tests/run host` where FUSE is not installed: `tests/host/krffs_fuse_hosttest.c` compiles `krffs/krffs_fuse_operations.c`, which includes `<fuse.h>`, and the tier stops with `fatal error: fuse.h: No such file or directory`. The unit that wrote the test wrote a stub header beside it under `tests/host/fuse` and declared it with a `host-cflags:` line; the stub was committed as `tests/host/fuse.h`, outside that include path, and the test was removed rather than left breaking the tier. The reader's own fix, which validates the whole ring before a FUSE dispatch, is in. It breaks the tools Readme's statement that FUSE is needed by no build and no suite. It depends on nothing. Implemented on `fix2-136` from `72711d6e`; the fix is uncommitted as requested, so its commit is pending. The test is restored and the existing stub moved to `tests/host/fuse/fuse.h`, matching its `host-cflags:` line and the KRFFS Readme. The host runner's compile command for this test builds and passes under ASan and UBSan on macOS without a system `fuse.h`. `sh tests/host/krffs_fuse_mutate.sh` proves it in a private tree under `build/tests`: the fixed test passes, bypassing `valid_node_ring` in the compiled mount source fails all twelve malformed-ring assertions, and the source restored, touched and rebuilt passes again. The script restores source in a trap after stopping the test; a fallback header with `#error` rejects any build that misses the stub. The full host tier was not run then, and has run in every full set since; this proof runs only the restored test. It tests mount validation and callbacks without a FUSE service, not a live mount. Taken and committed here, in `cd95e596`; the host tier builds and passes it on the testing host, where FUSE is absent. This entry is closed.

137. A password typed on a resume was flushed away before the reader read it (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, found when entry 99's wake preference hung the probe suite; severity P2: the probe hangs, which stops the suite and the sixteen probes after it, and the case it covers passed only on the order the round-robin scheduler happened to give it). Reproduce by running `musl/pwauthmusl` on a kernel that prefers a woken process: `check_prompt_stop_before_read` in `tests/musl/pwauthmusl.c` typed `secret` as soon as its leader reported the continue, while the reader was still inside its resume. That resume re-applies the echo-off modes with `TCSAFLUSH`, which discards what is typed already, and the mode change the stop interrupted does it once more as it returns, so the password went, and the reader waited for a line that would not come, the leader waited for the reader, and the probe waited for the leader. It breaks nothing of the kernel's: `utils/pwauth.c` flushes on a resume deliberately, which the stop-and-continue case beside this one requires. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the case reads the master until the prompt has been printed for the second time, which is after the last flush, and types then; both of the leader's reports are read with `read_verdict`, so a case that does hang kills its leader and fails its own row instead of stopping the suite. Proved on the testing host under KVM: with the preference in the kernel and the old probe, `tests/run default` failed with `musl/pwauthmusl no shell return (hang?)` and 16 probes never ran, three runs over; with the fix the same kernel passes 74 of 74. The kernel's process table, dumped from the timer while the probe hung, showed the probe, its leader and its reader all blocked with nothing runnable, and a wait-queue trace showed the reader parked on the terminal's input queue with no wake outstanding.

138. An open `/proc/interrupts` reported another file's inode (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, found while reading the synthetic file numbers for entry 134's counter; severity P3: a program that tells two files apart by identity is told they are one). Reproduce by opening `/proc/loop` and `/proc/interrupts` and calling `fstat` on both: `SYNTH_PROC_LOOP` and `SYNTH_PROC_INTERRUPTS` in `rach2/sys/fs/synthfs.h` were both 15, and `procfs_fd_ino` in `rach2/sys/fs/procfs.c` tests for the first, so an open `/proc/interrupts` answered with `PROCFS_INO_LOOP`. `stat` on the path was right, because it goes through `procfs_ino_interrupts()` and not through the open file's `origin_file`, which is why `procprobe`'s existing row passed. It breaks the uniqueness an inode number is for. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the two files have their own numbers, and `procprobe` asks both files twice, by path and through an open descriptor. `tests/system/rows_mutate.sh` sets `SYNTH_PROC_INTERRUPTS` back to 15, where `procprobe` must fail, and it did in the script's run on the testing host on 2026-09-25.


139. The debug shell read a program header table the image does not hold (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the process arm; severity P2: a crafted file typed to the `elf` command makes the kernel read outside the buffer it loaded, which faults the machine). Reproduce by putting a file in the image whose header says `e_phoff` is 0xfffffff0 and `e_phnum` is 1, and running `elf FILE` in the kernel debug shell: `elf_check` in `rach2/sys/proc/elf.c` bounded the table with `e_phoff + e_phnum * e_phentsize > size`, which wraps, and it never required `e_phentsize` to be this kernel's entry size, so the debug shell's `elf` command in `rach2/kernel.c` then walked a table far outside the buffer. The exec path is not affected: `elf_valid_exec` already requires the entry size. It breaks `elf_check`'s own statement that the table lies wholly within the first `size` bytes. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the bound is taken by division, `e_phoff` is compared with `size` first, and the entry size must equal `sizeof(Elf32_Phdr)`. `tests/host/elf_hosttest.c` asks the four cases, and `sh tests/host/elfcheck_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails two rows with the wrapping sum compiled into a private copy, and passes again restored, touched and rebuilt.


140. Two kernel range checks wrapped instead of refusing (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the memory manager; severity P3: neither is reachable from this tree's callers, and both promise in their own headers to refuse what they accept). `premap_kernel_window` in `rach2/sys/mm/paging.c` walked to `va_start + size`, so a window at the top of the address space wraps that sum below the start, allocates no page table and returns 0 where the header says -1. `map_mmio` in the same file rejects a wrapping region and then rounds the span up with `+ PAGE_SIZE - 1`, which wraps for a region reaching the top: it asks for no pages, maps nothing, and returns an address into the MMIO window. The callers cannot reach either today: the window starts at `PCWIN_BASE` in `rach2/sys/mm/pagecache.h` and `pc_probe_window_size` caps its span at `PC_WINDOW_CAP`, and the MMIO sizes are constants and a 16-bit SMBIOS length. Both break their headers. They depend on nothing. Fixed here: the window counts its tables after refusing a size past the address space, and the MMIO span is rounded by division. The fix was taken without a row, since nothing host-compiles `mm/paging.c`: its control-register and `invlpg` asm would need the `RACH2_HOSTTEST` guard `sched.c` uses, at six sites, and a stub for the frame allocator. The window's first refusal, `size > 0 - va_start`, refused every window that starts at address zero, above which the whole space lies; `a868ba10` exempts that start. The row is `tests/host/paging_hosttest.c` (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close): it includes `mm/paging.c` whole with `RACH2_HOSTTEST` defined, which leaves out its seven sites of control-register, flags and `invlpg` instructions and takes a fault's address from the test, stubs the heap to count the page tables it gives, and keeps the kernel's 32-bit `uintptr_t` and `size_t`, so the sums wrap where the kernel's do; the kernel's own code for `paging.c` is byte for byte the same with the guards. Its rows require a register page mapped uncached at the MMIO region's start; two spans that begin in the first page and end in the last, whose old rounding by `+ PAGE_SIZE - 1` wrapped to no pages and still returned an address, refused with the region's next page unmoved and the refusal recorded; a wrapping region refused; the next region taking the next page at its offset in the page; a window below the top given its two tables, and given none again; a window past the top of the address space refused with no table made; a window that ends exactly at the top given its table; and a window at address 0 given its table, the case `625cf097` refused and `a868ba10` exempted. `sh tests/host/paging_mutate.sh` compiles the old rounding, the old loop bounded by `va_start + size`, and the refusal without its exception for address 0 into a private copy of the kernel's sources, and requires each to fail its row.

141. A second reader blocked on a pseudo-terminal slept through the hangup (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the terminals; severity P2: a reader waits for ever on a terminal that is gone, and only a signal ends it). Reproduce with two readers blocked on a slave with SIGHUP ignored and blocked, then close the master: the discipline's canonical end-of-file marker is one-shot, `n_tty_read` clears it after the first zero-length read, and `pts_read` sampled `master_open` once before entering `tty_read`, so the second reader found neither input nor the marker and slept on. It breaks the persistent end of input `read(2)` promises after a hangup. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `pts_read` owns its wait, whose condition is the discipline's readiness or the master's closure, so every wake rechecks it; queued input still drains before the end of file. `ptyprobe` has the row, and `sh tests/system/pty_hangup_mutate.sh` passes the fixed kernel, fails with the closure taken out of the wait condition, and passes again restored, touched and rebuilt.

142. A page was written back with nothing holding its slot (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the memory manager; severity P2: a dirty page can be dropped or written from a frame another process has taken). Reproduce by evicting a dirty page while another process reclaims: `pc_evict` and `pc_sync` in `rach2/sys/mm/pagecache.c` cleared `dirty` and called the backing writer without reserving the slot, and that writer reaches the ATA driver, which sleeps, so another process could evict or reclaim the page that now looks clean. It breaks the header's promise that no dirty page is dropped. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: writeback pins the slot and frame across the write, refuses a competing writeback, and keeps a page that failed or was dirtied again; `fsync` reports a competing writeback as `EIO`. `tests/host/pagecache_hosttest.c` models a sleeping writer, and `sh tests/host/pagecache_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails with the pin and with the refusal removed, and passes again restored.

143. A pipe's ring span was held across a copy that can sleep (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the IPC code; severity P2: two readers can take the same bytes, and two writers can overwrite unread ones). Reproduce with two readers on one pipe whose user buffer is not yet resident: `pipe_read` in `rach2/sys/ipc/pipe.c` chose a span, copied it and then advanced `head`, and the copy prefaults with interrupts enabled, where memory reclaim can sleep in disk writeback; the write side held its span the same way. Nothing locks the pipe, and `in_syscall` stops an interrupt's preemption, not a sleep. It breaks the ring's own accounting. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: each user page is resolved before a span is chosen, the ring and the peer's state are rechecked after anything that can sleep, and a partial transfer keeps its count and its wakeup. `tests/host/pipe_copy_hosttest.c` drives a sleeping copy, and `sh tests/host/pipe_copy_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails with the read span and with the write span held across the sleep, and passes again restored.

144. The xHCI driver trusted register offsets and a mixed configuration (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the USB stack; severity P3: a controller or device that reports what these checks were meant to refuse is driven anyway). Eight faults of one shape, in `rach2/sys/usb/xhci.c` and `core.c`: the doorbell and runtime bounds are sums that wrap, a controller reporting no interrupter passed, a timed-out enumeration could leave its slot open, the endpoint context index was not kept at its maximum, the configuration parser could take its two bulk endpoints from different interfaces, and shutdown printed a halt it had not seen. They break the driver's own stated bounds. They depend on nothing. Fixed here, each with a row in `tests/host/xhci_hosttest.c` or `usb_hosttest.c`; `sh tests/host/usb_contract_mutate.sh` passes the fixed sources, fails on nine separately compiled mutations at their named assertions, and passes again restored; two of the nine, a late Enable Slot completion that survives an abort and a cleared CRR that still waits for the stop event, came with the closing review's fixes in `b984b33c`.

145. A detached hub's buffers could be reused before its slot closed (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the USB stack; severity P3: the controller can write into a record another hub has taken). Four faults in `rach2/sys/usb/hub.c`, `msc.c` and `usb.c`: a detached record was available for reuse while the controller could still reach its buffers, simultaneous port change bits were acknowledged with only one acted on, the pending-interrupt test did not cover every controller's vector, and storage bring-up counted its ten seconds per attempt rather than per device. They break the hub's own statement that a record stays allocated until the slot closes. They depend on nothing. Fixed here, with rows in `tests/host/hub_hosttest.c`, `msc_hosttest.c` and the new `usb_interrupt_hosttest.c`; `sh tests/host/usb_lifecycle_mutate.sh` passes the fixed sources, fails on seven compiled mutations, and passes again restored.

146. A send could queue bytes onto a connection that had just closed (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over TCP; severity P2: the bytes are queued and transmitted on a control block a reset has already taken down). Reproduce by resetting a connection from the peer while `tcp_send` is between its state check and its interrupt mask: `rach2/sys/network/tcp/tcp.c` looked the connection up and tested its state, then masked interrupts, and a receive interrupt in between sets `TCP_CLOSED`. It breaks the function's own statement that it sends only in ESTABLISHED or CLOSE_WAIT. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the lookup, the state check, the ring update and the transmit are all inside one masked region, and every return restores the caller's state. `tests/host/tcp_hosttest.c` has the rows, and `sh tests/host/tcp_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails three rows with the old order compiled in, and passes again restored.

147. ARP read a packet's addresses without checking their lengths (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the network arm; severity P2: a crafted 28-byte packet from the network updates the cache and draws a reply). Reproduce by sending an ARP packet whose hardware length is not 6 or whose protocol length is not 4: `arp_receive` in `rach2/sys/network/arp/arp.c` checked the hardware and protocol types and then read the addresses at fixed offsets. The neighbour test also used the primary interface's subnet rather than the receiving interface's, so a second card's network was judged against the first's. IP took a payload above 1480 bytes into its queue before refusing it. They break the receive path's stated contract. They depend on nothing. Fixed here: both lengths are required, the subnet tests take the receiving interface, and IP refuses an oversized payload before it routes or queues. `tests/host/arp_hosttest.c` and `packet_hosttest.c` hold the rows, and `sh tests/host/network_mutate.sh` passes the fixed sources, fails on five compiled mutations, and passes again restored.

148. Four faults in the cards, the interfaces and the boot line's network (2026-09-20, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the drivers and the interface layer; severity P3: each is a wrong answer at bring-up or on a second card, none reached by the images this project boots). A card refused on the boot line had its reason overwritten by the driver's own error, so `/proc/net/dev` named the wrong cause; a second interface's address change was not told to TCP, so a connection on the old address stayed up; a configuration that names a card by its station address left the primary address globals behind; and an option whose value was too long stopped the whole boot line's parse instead of being reported and skipped. They break what `rach2/sys/network/Readme.md` and `ioctl(2)` state. They depend on nothing. Fixed here, with rows in `tests/host/netdev_hosttest.c` and `netconf_hosttest.c`; `sh tests/host/netconfig_mutate.sh` passes the fixed sources, fails on the four compiled faults, and passes again restored, touched and rebuilt.

149. `sudo` authorised a uid written inside a comment, and the account tools left descriptors and state behind (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, phase 8's comment round over the credential library and the account tools; severity P1 for the first, P2 for the rest: a comment in `/etc/sudoers` that happens to hold a uid on a line with other text permitted that user). Nine faults. `permitted` in `utils/sudo.c` read lines with `fgets`, so a line longer than the buffer arrived in pieces and a piece could be a bare uid, and it accepted a line whose tail was anything; `pwauth.c` accepted a lock file whose pid it could not parse whole, reported success after failing to disable echo, and promised a cleanup its error handling did not perform; `login`, `su` and `sudo` closed descriptors only to 63 although this kernel permits 1023; `login`'s end-of-file path skipped the name's termination and its overflow refusal; `init` kept the console when its redirection failed and matched the SSH password setting as a prefix rather than a whole line; and `nc` lost bytes on a partial write in both directions. They break what `sudo(8)`, `login(1)`, `init(8)` and `sshd.conf(5)` state. They depend on nothing. Fixed here, each with a row in `tests/host/accounttools_hosttest.c`, `initpolicy_hosttest.c`, `ncwrite_hosttest.c`, `pwauthfault_hosttest.c` or `pwauth_hosttest.c`; `sh tests/host/account_mutate.sh` passes the fixed sources, fails on fifteen compiled faults, and passes again restored, touched and rebuilt. `tests/run account login ssh authority default` passed after.

150. The descriptor table, the terminal multiplexer and the mounted KRFFS kept promises they did not hold (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, phase 7's comment round over the file systems; severity P2: a process could start without its standard descriptors, and a file relocated by a mounted KRFFS could be written through a stale offset). Thirteen findings. `fd_init_stdio` in `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c` could leave a process without the three descriptors it promises and said nothing; `/dev/ptmx` allocated a pair before refusing an exclusive create; `/proc`'s stat generator wrote its terminator after an allocation failure; and `krffsmount.c` identified an open file by an offset the file system moves, so a relocation during a write or a truncate could reach another file, a mounted `O_TRUNC` open truncated before it registered, and a read-only mount cleared its dirty flag with writes still pending. They break what `open(2)`, `mount(2)` and the file systems' Readmes state. They depend on nothing. Fixed here: the standard descriptors report and unwind, `/dev/ptmx` refuses before it allocates, the stat generator stops at the failure, and a mounted file keeps a stable handle that every path repoints from. Four mutation scripts hold twenty-one faults between them, each failing its rows and passing again restored. The four scripts are `tests/host/fd_stdio_mutate.sh`, `krffsmount_mutate.sh`, `open_mutate.sh` and `synth_open_mutate.sh`; the first could not finish from `216ded22` until entry 186.

151. The flash, DHCP and interface tools lost state on the paths that fail (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, phase 8's comment round over the network tools; severity P2: a DHCP client that cannot remove its address drops its claim, and two clients could write one resolver file at once). Nine findings across `utils/dhcpc.c`, `netcfg.c`, `netup.c`, `ipcheck.c` and `reflash.c`: cleanup dropped the claim before the address was gone, a lease without a netmask was validated with one mask and applied with another, the resolver was rewritten through a shared temporary name, an unreadable claim read as absent, `netcfg` did not check both reads and the seek between them, and `netup` kept the inherited standard descriptors when its redirection failed. `reflash`'s own manual example overwrote the recovery partition. They break what `dhcpc(8)`, `netup(8)` and `reflash(8)` state. They depend on nothing. Fixed here, with `tests/host/dhcpc_hosttest.c`, `netcfg_io_hosttest.c` and `netup_hosttest.c`; `sh tests/host/netutil_mutate.sh` passes the fixed sources, fails on each compiled fault, and passes again restored.

152. The shell reported success for a wait a signal cut short (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, phase 8's comment round over ISH; severity P2: a command's status is reported as zero when the shell's own wait was interrupted, and a typed line longer than the buffer was run in pieces).
