Six findings in `ish/ish.c` and `ish_cstring_utilities.c`: an interrupted wait returned success rather than retrying, the input loop read chunks although its header promised lines, and three comments named the wrong caller, the wrong wrap boundary and the wrong processor. They break what `ish(1)` states. They depend on nothing. Fixed here: the wait retries on `EINTR` and answers 126 when it cannot read a status, a partial line is kept across reads and a line over 255 bytes is refused whole rather than run in pieces. `tests/host/ish_hosttest.c` and `tests/musl/ishmusl.c` hold the rows, and `sh tests/host/ish_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails on six compiled faults, and passes again restored.

153. The SB16 scale row's notes depended on the host's accelerator and its load (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, found in a full set; severity P3: a false failure, no fault in the kernel). Run `tests/run all` on a host whose accelerator is KVM: the row played its five notes and `wavcheck` found three of them wrong, while the same row alone passed. The row was written with `-icount shift=3,sleep=off`, which makes the guest's clock the instruction count and the note lengths fixed; QEMU refuses that option under hardware virtualization, so it was taken out, and what replaced it was the host's own timing under twenty machines. It breaks the rule that a suite asserts on what the kernel counts and not on the clock. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the row names `RACH2_QEMU_ACCEL=tcg` for itself, which the runner's shim reads, and takes its instruction clock back. It passes in 19 s, and the HDA row beside it is unchanged.

154. The boot slot's fallback could be left holding a kernel it had not verified (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, phase 8's comment round over the installer helpers; severity P2: the fallback is what a machine boots when the candidate fails, so a fallback written from an unverified image is a machine with no way back). Fifteen findings in `utils/bootslot.c`, `bootctl.c`, `growroot.c`, `growset.c` and `grubslot.c`. The two that were faults: `bootslot_fallback` restored the fallback's header before it had verified the payload it belongs to, so an interrupted copy left a header over the wrong bytes; and `bootslot_validate_kernel` did not refuse a load range that runs past the 32-bit address space, which its own comment promised. The rest were comments that named the wrong state, the wrong file or a repair that does not happen. They break what `bootctl(8)` and `growset(8)` state. They depend on nothing. Fixed here, with `tests/host/bootslot_hosttest.c`; `sh tests/host/bootslot_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails on the ordering fault and on the address bound, and passes again restored. `tests/run bootorder bootline menu installer grow` passed after.

155. `rm` could empty a symbolic link's target (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, phase 8's comment round over the file utilities; severity P2: `rm -r` on a link to a directory removed what the link pointed at). Reproduce with a symbolic link to a directory whose unlink fails: `remove_recursive` in `utils/rm.c` tried `unlink` first and, on any failure, emptied the path as a directory, which follows the link. Five comments around it also named a walk, a return and a length check the code does not have. It breaks what the coreutils page states. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: a link is identified with `lstat` and unlinked without being followed, and the headers state what the code does. `tests/host/rm_hosttest.c` holds twelve rows, and `sh tests/host/rm_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails two rows with the guard removed, and passes again restored.

156. Two rows failed on a boundary rather than on a fault (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, found in a full set; severity P3: false failures, no fault in the kernel). `procprobe` required the ticks of `/proc/uptime` to equal the idle and busy counts of `/proc/stat` exactly, but those are two files read one after the other, so a tick landing between the reads belongs to one sample and not the other; the row passed alone and failed in a set of twenty machines. The `make` suite's idle control ran a second make immediately after the first build, and make compares whole seconds, so a build that finished inside the same second as its sources left them equal, which is out of date, and the control rebuilt. Both break the rule that a row asserts on what the kernel counts and not on where a boundary fell. They depend on nothing. Fixed here: the identity allows the ticks its two reads can span, four, with the reason beside it, and the make control waits out the second first, as the rows below it already did.

157. The write path's batching serialised every reader of a volume (2026-09-21, milestone 06's second review run, found by the `mount` suite's queue row in the set that followed entry 49's work; severity P2: every description on a mount waited for every other, so sixteen readers of one FAT volume issued one request at a time). Reproduce with `tests/run mount`: its queue row requires sixteen readers to fill a pool of two, and the log showed nobody waiting for a slot. Entry 49's work had generalised `fd_ofd_lock` in `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c` from KRFFS, which moves a file's bytes while it is open, to every mount, and that lock is the mount's namespace. It breaks nothing a document states, and it costs every file system the concurrency KRFFS alone needs. It depends on entry 49. Fixed here: `struct vfs_type` carries `ns_serialised`, which KRFFS and ext2 set, since one moves a file and the other holds a batch of dirty blocks between calls, and the description lock takes the namespace only for those. `tests/host/fd_hosttest.c` has the row for a file system that needs neither, and `tests/run mount` passes with fifteen waiters again, where the unconditional lock left none. The usbdisk fixture's unmount stub was missing with it, which is why that suite stopped before booting; it has the stub now.

158. `bootctl kernel` took a kernel that cannot mount a disk root (2026-09-21, milestone 06's close, found on the Lenovo; severity P2: a machine whose candidate is one boots to a halt and needs a person at its console). The tree builds two disk policies: `build/rach2/kernel` may write a disk, and `build/rach2-metal/kernel`, built with `-DRACH2_DISK_READONLY`, is the live stick's and refuses every write command. Installed as the candidate through `bootctl kernel`, the read-only build boots, finds the controller, the disk and the partition, and is then refused at the mount's first write: `ahci: refused: command 0x35: rach2: disk policy: read-only`, `ext2: root: /dev/disk0p3 refused`, and the boot stops with `boot: refused: no file system mounted`. Reproduce in QEMU by booting `build/rach2-metal/kernel` with `rach2.root=MBRID=...,3 rach2.rootfstype=ext2 rach2.disk=ahci` over a disk whose third partition is an ext2 volume; the same disk with `build/rach2/kernel` mounts in ten milliseconds. It breaks no stated contract; it leaves the one check that would catch the mistake unmade, and the cost is a boot and a trip to the machine. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `bootslot_validate_kernel` judges the policy banner where the whole image is in memory, requiring the read-write banner and refusing the read-only one or an image that names neither; a caller that passes a header prefix is reading back a slot it has already installed and the banner may lie past those bytes, so the policy is not judged there. `tests/host/bootslot_hosttest.c` and `tests/host/bootctl_hosttest.c` hold the rows, `man/man8/bootctl.8` states the refusal, and `sh tests/host/bootslot_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails with the refusal removed, and passes again restored.

159. The boot slot's mutation script did not compile (2026-09-21, milestone 06's close; severity P3: a proof not run, no fault in the shipped code). `bootslot_fallback` in `utils/bootslot.c` declared `present, s, i, n` and used neither `i` nor `n`, which entry 154's fix left behind when it moved the header restore; `tests/host/bootslot_mutate.sh` compiles with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, so every phase of it, the unmutated one included, stopped at two errors. Reproduce on `b984b33c` with `sh tests/host/bootslot_mutate.sh`: it prints two `unused variable` errors and exits before the first phase. The ordinary build of `bootctl` does not use those warnings, so nothing else showed it. It breaks the rule that a test is proved by making it fail, since the proof entry 154 records cannot be repeated on the tree as it stands. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the two dead declarations are gone, and the script passes its fixed, mutated and restored phases for all three faults, the ordering, the address bound and the disk policy.

160. An open could register a description on a node that had moved (2026-09-21, milestone 06's close, the last unit of the review round; severity P2: the description reads whatever took the old place, which on a volume being written is another file's bytes). KRFFS moves a file's bytes while it is open, and `do_open` in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` resolved a path, let the namespace lock go, and only then opened the node and registered the description, so a writer that relocated the file in that window left the new description on an offset another file could already own. Reproduce with `sh tests/host/krffs_open_mutate.sh`: a second thread opens the file at the flush that publishes a copy before the original extent, the writer relocates it again and reuses the first copy's offset, and both descriptions must still read the file. The same unit found `kv_prepare_unmount` in `rach2/sys/fs/krffsmount.c` returning an error after a failed write of the clean flag while leaving the mount writable, so later writes went to a volume the medium may already record as clean. Both break what `vfs.Readme.md` states about a description's node and what `krffs.Readme.md` states about the dirty flag. They depend on nothing. Fixed here: `VFS_WALK_LOCK` makes the resolver hold one namespace lock at a time and return with the final mount's lock held, which `open` and `open_create` keep through registration and release on every exit, and a failed clean-flag write makes the mount read-only and says so. The walk locks only a type that sets `ns_serialised`, which is KRFFS and ext2: locking every type made `tests/run mount` fail its cold-buffer row, where two forked readers of one file on a vfat volume must both reach the cache and one must wait, and a lock held across the lookup let the first finish before the second began. A create still runs under the lock on every type, as it did before, taken in `open_create` where the walk did not take it. `sh tests/host/krffs_open_mutate.sh` passes the fixed source, fails with the open path's lock removed and again with the lock moved into `kv_open` after the lookup, and passes restored; `sh tests/host/krffsmount_mutate.sh` carries the eleventh fault for the flag.

161. A grown ext2 root would not mount read-write once the heap was fragmented (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found on the Lenovo; severity P1: `rach2-install --update` wrote and grew the new root and then stopped with `the new root would not mount`, leaving the machine's accounts off it). Entry 49's write batch took its 288 buffers as one allocation of 289 heap pages at every read-write ext2 mount. The heap keeps runs of 256 pages for large requests, so a longer one has to come from the ordinary region in one piece, and after the recovery system had streamed a gigabyte through the cache that region had no such gap: `kheap: 289 pages: no room or no frame` three times, once per attempt, with 2.6 GB of memory free. A read-only mount skips the batch and worked, which is why the volume looked sound. It breaks what `rach2-install(8)` states for an update. It depends on entry 49. Fixed here: each buffer is one heap page, which is one cache block, so no buffer needs its neighbour's pages. `tests/host/compat/kheap_host.c` can refuse any allocation longer than a limit, and `tests/host/ext2_hosttest.c` mounts read-write under a limit of one reserve run; `sh tests/host/ext2_batch_mutate.sh` fails that row with every buffer asking for 289 pages again and passes restored. On the machine the update then ran to `updated: /dev/disk0p3 carries the new root with this machine's accounts`.

162. An update resumed from the archive drops every SSH key (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found on the Lenovo; severity P2: the machine comes back reachable by password only, with a new host key). When an update stops after the image has overwritten the root, the next run finds `update-in-progress` on the recovery partition and takes the configuration from the archive there, but `write_archive` in `utils/rach2-install` stores only `$CONFIG_FILES`, the password, group and shadow files and `sshd.conf`. The server's host keys, root's `.ssh` and every home's keys were on the overwritten root and nowhere else, so the resumed update writes back the passwords and nothing that SSH authenticates with. Reproduce with entry 161's fault: the second run said `taking the configuration saved on the recovery partition`, and after the trial boot both keys the machine had trusted were refused. It breaks no stated contract: row 17 of `tests/system/suites/installer.sh` requires exactly this, `test ! -e /root/.ssh` after a resumed update, since keys that are not archived can only be dropped or left as the image's, and the image's would let its builder in. The cost is a machine run headless: the Lenovo was left reachable by password alone and needed a power cycle and a hand repair from its recovery system. The archive already holds the password hashes on that partition, so the keys belong there too. It depends on entry 20, which introduced the archive. Fixed when the archive carries the host keys and each account's `.ssh`, and a resumed update restores them with their owners and modes; row 17 then logs in with the key the machine had and finds its host key unchanged, and a mutation that leaves the keys out of the archive fails it. Fixed here, in two rounds. The first put `etc/dropbear`, `root/.ssh` and `home/*/.ssh` in the archive and restored them on a resumed update; the closing review found that FAT32's case folding merged the archived keys of two accounts whose names differ only in case, which BusyBox `adduser` allows after the install, and that a restore looked for `home/NAME` although the archive held the directory of the passwd home. The second keys the archive by UID, `rach2-config/keys/UID/` holding the contents of that account's `$home/.ssh`, restores each into the home its archived passwd names with its owner and modes 700 and 600, and ignores a UID no archived account has. Row 17 of `tests/system/suites/installer.sh` now logs in by the machine's key after a resumed update, and requires the host key and the keys of an account whose home is `/home/work` and of two accounts named `Alice` and `alice` back as they were, and no key for an orphan UID; `sh tests/system/install_keys_mutate.sh` fails it with the keys left out of the archive. The row forwards SSH, so `tests/run installer-repair` has its own port, 2329, where it had shared 2327 with `tests/run installer` and lost the forward when the pool ran the two together.

163. The update archive could be left in no place at all (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the closing review of entry 162; severity P2: a second stop at the wrong instant loses the machine's configuration). `write_archive` in `utils/rach2-install` removed `rach2-config` and then renamed `rach2-config.new` into its place, so a stop between the two left only `.new`; the next run then saw no archive and no update-in-progress mark, took the image's credentials off the root, and began its own archive by removing `.new`, the only copy of the machine's accounts. It predates this milestone: entry 20 introduced the archive. It breaks the installer's contract that a stop at any point leaves the machine's configuration recoverable. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the replacement renames the current archive to `.old`, renames `.new` into place, and only then removes `.old`, and every read or write of the archive first promotes a complete `.new`, or else `.old`, when `rach2-config` is missing; nothing removes `.new` while it is the only archive. The installer suite planted that stop and required the retry to take the machine's configuration from `.new`, and `sh tests/system/install_keys_mutate.sh` failed the row with the old remove-then-rename order; entry 167 replaced both. Superseded by entry 167 (`df8aecdd`): the archive is two slots, `rach2-config-a` and `rach2-config-b`, never renamed, and `rach2-config`, `.new` and `.old` are read only as an old archive, generation 0, and removed once a new slot is complete.

164. Files of one name shared an inode number on a KRFFS root (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by `tests/run installer-repair`; severity P2: `cp -a` copied such files as hard links of one file). KRFFS keeps a regular file's on-disk id as the hash of its name alone, since nothing refers to a file by id, and four places reported that id as the inode number: the root's `getattr` and `readdir` in `rach2/sys/fs/vfs.c`, and both stat fills in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c`. So `/tmp/a/k` and `/tmp/b/k` both read as inode 177614. On the recovery system the update copies the kept homes out of `/tmp` with `cp -a`, which links any two regular files of one inode number, and every account's `authorized_keys` came out on the new root as one inode, `test`'s, owned by 1000 with four links. It breaks the POSIX rule that a device and an inode number identify one file. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `krffs_node_ino` gives a directory its id, which is already `krffs_mix_id(parent, name)`, and a file the same mix of its parent's id and its name, which does not change when KRFFS moves the file's bytes; all four places report it. `tests/host/krffs_hosttest.c` requires two same-named files in two directories to differ and a directory to keep its id, and fails with a file's number set back to its id; `tests/stattest.c` requires the same through `stat` and `stat64` on the running root, and fails with the stat64 fill set back to the id.

165. A probe's boot was held to the step limit (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in a full set; severity P3: false failures, no fault in the kernel). `probe=NAME` in `tests/system/runner/harness.c` expanded to `expect=Type 'help'` and an exec, and that expect, which is the whole boot, was bounded by `--steptmo`, fifteen seconds unless a suite says otherwise. With sixty-four emulators on nested KVM a boot can take longer, and `tests/run authority` and `tests/run persist-ahci` each failed a step in a full set and passed alone in thirty seconds; thirteen probe boots in `tests/system/suites.mk` set no step limit. It breaks the rule that seconds in a suite are completion bounds and not judgements. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `probe=` expands to `boot=Type 'help'`, a wait bounded by the run's `--timeout`, and an explicit `expect=` keeps the step limit. The harness selftest feeds a prompt after the step limit and before the run's timeout and requires the probe expansion to take it and `expect=` to refuse it; with the expansion put back to `expect=` the selftest fails.

166. A make after a reboot rebuilt what nothing had touched (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the first acceptance set at `6b4a46fa`; severity P3: false rebuilds, and one false failure of `tests/run make`). pdpmake decides a target is out of date when its time is less than or equal to a prerequisite's, and when either time has a fraction of exactly zero it takes the file system for one of one-second resolution and compares whole seconds (`vendor/pdpmake/pdpmake-2.0.4/make.c`, `timespec_le`). This kernel's clock stamps times in whole ticks of 10 ms (`rtc_time_from_anchor` in `rach2/sys/rtc.c`), so one stamp in a hundred has a zero fraction, and a target and a prerequisite written in the same second, one of them in tick zero, compare equal. Reproduced by a copy of the suite under load on the testing host: `lib.o` at 06:14:46.000000000 and `prog` at 06:14:46.730000000, and `make` relinked `prog` after the reboot, `TCC=1`. The pair shared a second in six runs of nine, so the rows IDLE1, IDLE2 and REBOOTIDLE each carried a failure rate of about one or two in a hundred. Reading the code for the set's `TCC=3` found a second fault: `sys_sync` in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` ignored `pc_sync`'s answer, and `pc_sync` passes over a page-cache slot that another process is writing, so `sync` could return before that page reached the disk, and the suite stops its machine right after `sync`. Which of the two made the set's run recompile both objects is not shown, since that run kept only the count. It breaks the rule that `make` rebuilds only what changed, and `sync(2)`'s promise that what was written is on the disk. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, in two commits. `rtc_realtime` in `rach2/sys/rtc.c` adds the PIT's position within the tick, read with interrupts off and with the timer's pending bit taken on both sides of the latch so a wrap the handler has not counted still counts, and never returns a time earlier than the last it gave; the clock is anchored once at boot and nothing sets it later, so that clamp cannot hold a clock someone moved back. A zero fraction is now a one-in-a-million event and two stamps in one tick differ. `pc_sync` tells a busy slot from a failed write, and `sys_sync` waits for busy slots with interrupts on, for at most 3000 ticks, while a write failure still leaves its page dirty without a wait; a slot is pinned only for one read or write in flight, so the wait is that long. `tests/stattest.c` requires fifty stamps back to back to be strictly increasing, which entry 213 changed, and explicit fractions to come back as set, and `sh tests/system/rtc_mutate.sh --guest` fails it on the coarse clock and passes restored; `tests/host/pagecache_hosttest.c` requires a sync that meets a busy slot not to return before its page is written, and `sh tests/host/pagecache_mutate.sh` fails nine rows with the single ignored call. The make suite's rows that print the files' times across the reboot stay.

167. An update could keep an image's key, lose a key, or be left with no archive (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the closing review of the machine's fixes; severity P2: after an update an account could be reached with the image's demonstration key or not with its own, and a stop at the wrong moment left the recovery partition with no archive at all). Four faults of `utils/rach2-install`, all from entries 162 and 163. An update resumed from the archive took the image's `.ssh` off root's home only, so an account whose home is outside `/home` and that had no archived key kept the image's key. A normal update kept `/home`, `/root/.ssh` and the host keys and nothing else, so such an account also lost its own keys, on the new root and in the archive. Recovering the archive renamed the only complete copy into place, and `fat_rename` in `rach2/sys/fs/fat32.c` frees and flushes the old name before it writes the new one, so a stop between the two left neither. And FAT32 folds ASCII case, so `authorized_keys` and `AUTHORIZED_KEYS` in one `.ssh` became one file in the archive, silently. The review found these by reading the code; the rows below exercise each case. It breaks the contract of `rach2-install(8)`, that an update keeps the machine's accounts and keys. It depends on nothing. Fixed here. The `.ssh` of every passwd home outside `/home` is kept with its owner and modes, and the image's is removed from each such home whether or not a key comes back. The archive is two slots, `rach2-config-a` and `rach2-config-b`, never renamed: a writer rebuilds the slot it did not choose, syncs, and only then writes that slot's `generation`, one more than the chosen one's, and syncs again; a reader takes the complete slot with the highest generation, and a slot without a valid generation is never taken, so a stop anywhere leaves the chosen slot whole. The earlier names, `rach2-config`, `.new` and `.old`, are read as generation 0 in the old reader's order and removed once a new slot is complete; the Lenovo's archive is one of them. An update marks B, writing A first when the archive it found was an old one or none, and ends by writing A, so a finished update leaves A current. Names in one `.ssh` that differ only in case stop the update before anything is written, and the message names both. The first form of this fix let the name `rach2-config` be both an old archive and slot A, so a half-written first slot counted as an old one; the separate names above correct it. `tests/system/suites/installer.sh` plants a half-written slot A before a normal update and requires the update to ignore it, refuses the case pair with the root's hash unchanged, and requires `/srv/alice`'s keys kept and `/srv/empty`'s image key gone; its stop row plants an old archive beside a half-written A, stops the installer after it clears its destination, and requires the old archive whole and the retry to take it; row 17 requires the same two homes after a resumed update. `sh tests/system/install_keys_mutate.sh` makes the writer rebuild the slot it chose, and the stop row fails; restored, it passes. Each of the four faults has a mutation since 2026-09-24: `install_keys_mutate.sh` also removes the check of names that differ only in case, where the normal update row must fail when the update writes the root with the pair, and that mutation failed its row on the testing host on 2026-09-25; its first mutation, key archiving removed, fails row 17 for the homes outside `/home`. Each of the Lenovo's three updates of 2026-09-25 ran over a recovery partition just rewritten, and so with no archive, and left slot A at generation 3 without the update mark and B at generation 2 with it, the order `rach2-install(8)` gives for an update that finds no archive. After the third, both slots were read on the machine, and each has `keys/0` and `keys/1000` with an `authorized_keys` in each.

168. KRFFS reported unstable and disagreeing inode numbers, and the harness could miss the machine's last output (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the closing review of the machine's fixes; severity P3: wrong answers to programs that compare inode numbers, and a false failure of a test run). Entry 164 made a file's number its directory's id mixed with its name, and `krffs_orphan_at` clears the name of a file unlinked while open, so two such files of one directory reported one number and each changed its number at the unlink. The root reported 0 through `stat` and `stat64` of `/`, 1 through the VFS attribute call, and through `/tmp/..` a number hashed as if the root were a file, since its stored mode has no directory bits. And `expect_pattern` in `tests/system/runner/harness.c` stopped reading once the machine's process had exited and searched only what it had read, so a line written just before the exit could be missed. It breaks the contract of `stat(2)` that one file has one number, and the harness's that every line the machine printed is searched. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `krffs_node_ino` in `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c` mixes a file's directory id with its stored id, the hash of its name, which an unlink leaves, and returns 1 for the root by its node type; every stat call in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` reports 1 for the root; the harness drains the pipe once after the machine has exited. `tests/host/krffs_hosttest.c` requires two orphaned files of one directory to keep distinct, unchanged numbers and the root to be 1 through every walk to it; `tests/stattest.c` requires 1 and a directory from `stat` and `stat64` of `/` and of a directory's `..`, and from `fstat64` of a descriptor on `/` and `fstatat64` of `/`; the harness self-test has a machine that prints the prompt and exits before the harness reads. `sh tests/host/krffs_inode_mutate.sh` compiles five mutations, the orphan's number from its name, the root's node-type case removed, each stat path's root reporting 0, and the drain removed, and each fails its row and passes restored.

169. A stopped proof could not stop its test's process group (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found when a stale key proof was stopped by hand; severity P3: an interrupted proof ran on until its suite's own bounds ended it, and a proof could restore a source while a test process still used it). Nineteen scripts under `tests/` stopped or waited for their child's process group with `kill -TERM -- "-$child"` and `kill -0 -- "-$child"`. dash, the testing host's `/bin/sh`, refuses the `--` in its kill builtin with `Illegal number: -`, and the scripts send that error to `/dev/null`, so the signal was never sent and the wait loop ended at once. A script stopped with TERM then sat in `wait` until its child ended by itself: the key proof stopped on 2026-09-24 waited until its emulator was signalled by hand, and a reproduction of its stop function under dash took 29 seconds to leave a child that slept for 30. It breaks the rule in CLAUDE.md that a proof stops the test processes before it restores a source they share. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the scripts write `kill -TERM "-$child"` and `kill -0 "-$child"`, which dash and bash both read as a process group after the signal option, and the same reproduction leaves in one second with the group gone. `tests/run style` refuses a kill with `--` before a process group; it read every `*.sh` file under `tests`, `tools`, `rach2`, `krffs` and `mk` and `tests/run`, failed with the old form planted in `tests/system/rtc_mutate.sh` and passed restored, and since 2026-09-24 it reads every in-house script `tools/verify/scripts.sh` lists, which none of the 242 fails.

170. A KRFFS directory could not be made where its id or its parent fell across a unit (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while writing entry 15's row; severity P3: a refused `mkdir`, no data lost). `krffs_mkdir_at` in `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c` wrote the new directory's parent and id with `krffs_put_u64`, under the whole-unit rule, where `krffs_create_at` and the allocator's copy have used `krffs_put_new_u64` since entry 15. On a mounted KRFFS volume, whose `grain` is 4096, a directory whose free node put the id field, eight bytes at the node's offset plus 3, or the parent field, at plus 349, across a 4096-byte unit was refused with `krffs: refused: a field at N would reach the medium in two pieces`, `mkdir(2)` answered an error, and the nameless node it had taken was left for the next mount's check. Reproduce in a host image: grow `/spacer` to 3375 bytes, set `grain` to 4096 and make `/across`; its node lands at 4089 and its id, 0x603799db2d6956ab, changes bytes on both sides of 4096. It breaks entry 15's contract that the id and the parent of a node being made are written without the rule. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: both are written through `krffs_put_new_u64`, for the reason that function gives, that nothing reaches the node until its name is written last. A row in `tests/host/krffs_hosttest.c` makes that directory under grain 4096, finds its whole id and makes a child in it; `krffs_id_mutate.sh`'s `mkdir-id-rule` fault writes the id through `krffs_put_u64` again and the row fails.

171. The host image stager let two directories hold one id (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while writing entry 125's row; severity P2: an image whose two directories share their children, a hierarchy the kernel refuses to build). `krffs/stageimage.krffs.c` set a staged directory's id to `krffs_mix_id(parent_id, name)` and never compared it with the directories already staged; `bC` and `cb` have one DJB hash, 0x596ee4, so `dir 0755 /bC` and `dir 0755 /cb` staged two directories with one id, and a file under either appeared under both. The kernel's `krffs_mkdir_at` has refused the second through `krffs_id_taken` since entry 125. It breaks the v2 hierarchy's `parent_id` contract. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `dir_id_free` refuses a directory whose mixed id a named directory already has, in `stage_dir` and for the parents made for a file's path, with `stageimage: directory '/cb' has the id of directory '/bC'; the two would share their children.` Rows `stager-dir-id` and `stager-dir-id-implicit` of `tests/host/krffs_tools_mutate.sh`, whose faults `dir-id` and `dir-id-implicit` each fail their row. None of the twenty manifests has such a pair: every directory they stage, `tree` directories included, was mixed and compared on 2026-09-24 and none collided.

172. `disk write` destroyed the only table of a GPT disk whose primary header was gone (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while writing entry 124's row; severity P2, as entry 124's: the write overwrites the backup header, which is then the disk's only partition table). `disk_write_roundtrip` in `rach2/kernel.c`, the recovery kernel's `disk write`, read LBA 1, refused a disk that had `EFI PART` there, and wrote the last sector of any other. A GPT disk whose LBA 1 was zeroed still has its table in the backup header at the last sector, which the kernel's partition reader takes the table from, so the command wrote over the one copy left. Reproduce with the parts suite's GPT disk with its LBA 1 zeroed, booted on the recovery kernel with `rach2.rescue`, and `disk write`. It breaks entry 124's claim that the command refuses a GPT disk. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: where LBA 1 has no GPT signature the command reads the last sector as well and refuses a disk whose last sector is a GPT header. `tests/system/gpt_write_refusal.sh` has a third disk, the GPT disk with its primary header zeroed, which must be refused and left as it was, and `gpt_write_mutate.sh` takes out the read of the last sector, where the row must fail on that disk.

173. An exFAT set in two cache blocks was written and retired with no barrier between the blocks (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while proving entry 32's barrier sites; severity P2: a crash on a drive that holds writes could publish a set whose secondaries were never written, or retire a secondary under a file entry still in use, and the dirty mount then takes the volume read-only). `set_store` in `rach2/sys/fs/exfat.c` writes the entries past the file entry's sector first and then that sector, and `set_retire` writes the file entry inactive and then the secondaries; `set_retire`'s header called the gap "the barrier", and neither had one. A set that starts at slot 126 of a 4 KiB block, which mixed name lengths allow, or a set of a long name, lies in two blocks, and a drive may put two blocks on the medium in either order. Reproduce under the host device model with a set that crosses a block: created, then unlinked, a stop between the two writes of each leaves the invalid states. It breaks `exfat.Readme.md`'s statement that a stop before the last write leaves secondaries with no primary. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `set_store` flushes before the file entry's sector when the set reaches past that sector, and `set_retire` flushes after the file entry when a secondary lies in another sector. The first form of the fix compared 4 KiB cache blocks; the milestone's closing review showed that a drive may put the sectors of one block on the medium in any order as well, so the comparison is by sector, the unit the driver's guarantee is built on. The crossing sequence of `tests/host/exfat_hosttest.c` unlinks the fixture's `crossing.txt`, whose set crosses from the root's first cluster into its second, and makes it again; `fat_barriers_mutate.sh` counts fifteen exFAT sites now and drops each of the two new ones in turn, and the crossing sequence must fail.

174. A host test's exit status was its failure count, which wraps at 256 (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in `fat32_hosttest.c` while writing entry 32's proof; severity P3: a check that cannot see a fault, at those counts only). `main` returned the count of failed checks, and a process's status keeps the low eight bits of what `main` returns, so 256 failures, or any multiple, read as a pass in the host suite and in every proof that judges the status. Reproduce with any fault that fails exactly 256 checks. It breaks the host Readme's rule that a test returns 0 only after every assertion passes. It depends on nothing. The same form was in the `main` of 33 other host tests. Fixed here: `fat32_hosttest.c` returns 1 and prints the count, and the other 33 clamp the count at 255, which keeps the statuses the proofs that pin a count read, 3 for `tcp_mutate.sh` and 4 for `frame_mutate.sh`; the host Readme states the rule. No mutation is made of it, since putting the count back fails nothing unless a fault fails a multiple of 256 checks.

175. The dhcp suite's `arping` status was read on the host (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while writing the `noentropy` steps; severity P3: a check that cannot fail). `tests/system/suites.mk`'s `dhcp-card` recipe sent `echo ARPING""-STATUS:$$?` inside double quotes; make turns `$$` into `$`, and the host's shell expanded `$?` before the harness typed the line, so the guest always printed `ARPING-STATUS:0` and `expect=ARPING-STATUS:0` passed whatever `arping` returned. The next step, `expect=Received 1 response`, still checked `arping`'s output. It breaks the row's contract that it judges the guest's status. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the recipe sends `\$$?`, which reaches the guest as `$?`. `tests/system/rows_mutate.sh` gives `arping` an interface the guest has not got, and `make dhcp-card` must fail with the guest's log showing `ARPING-STATUS:2`; the first form of that fault, an address nothing answers, left `arping` waiting for good (entry 212).

176. The harness's input pipe was never non-blocking (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while reviewing the earlier session's harness changes; severity P2: a guest that stops reading its serial line holds the run past its deadline). `main` in `tests/system/runner/harness.c` called `fcntl(child_in, F_SETFL, O_NONBLOCK)` one line before `child_in = in_pipe[1]`, so it acted on -1 and the input pipe stayed blocking. `send_bytes` claims to read the machine's output while it waits and to give up at the deadline; that path never ran, a `send=`, `sendraw=` or `sendfile=` to a machine that stopped reading blocked past `--timeout`, and the harness stopped draining QEMU's output while blocked, so a flood could deadlock both. Reproduce with a child that never reads its standard input and a line longer than the pipe holds. It breaks the harness's contract that every step ends by the run's deadline. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `serial_ends` sets both ends of the serial pipes non-blocking, and `sendraw=` writes through `send_bytes`, since a plain write to a full non-blocking pipe fails with `EAGAIN`. The harness selftest's `input_deadline_selftest` requires a write to a machine that has stopped reading to end at the run's deadline, and `tests/system/harness_input_mutate.sh` leaves the pipe blocking in a private copy, where the selftest must fail.

177. `dhcpc` gave up a wait for a reply after one poll (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes; severity P2: a lease's rebinding is sent again every 62 s instead of when RFC 2131 says). `receive` in `utils/dhcpc.c` polled for at most 60 s at a time and returned "no reply" when a poll ended with nothing, although its deadline was further away, so an unanswered rebinding request was sent again after about 62 s rather than after half of what was left of the lease. The idle wait before T1 and the discovery waits were not affected. It breaks `receive`'s own header and the client's claim to follow RFC 2131's retransmission. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the wait polls again until its deadline. `check_receive_wait` and `check_rebinding` in `tests/host/dhcpc_hosttest.c`, on a modeled clock that each poll and sleep advances, and `netutil_mutate.sh`'s faults `receive` and `rebinding`, which put the old condition back, each fail their row.

178. `dhcpc` could take off an address it never set (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while reviewing entry 177; severity P2: a machine configured by its boot line loses its address when the first lease cannot be recorded). `apply_initial` in `utils/dhcpc.c` called `unconfigure` whenever recording the claim for a first lease failed, and that failure comes before any ioctl, so the address it removed was whatever the interface had, such as a static one from the boot line. It breaks the client's rule that it removes only what it configured. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `apply_initial` returns before `unconfigure` when it holds no claim. A row in `tests/host/dhcpc_hosttest.c`'s `check_initial` makes the claim record's creation fail and requires the interface's address kept, and `netutil_mutate.sh`'s `initial-record` fault removes the return and the row fails.

179. `nc` narrowed its port and read past the digits (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes; severity P3: a connection to a port nobody asked for). `utils/nc.c` read a positive decimal prefix and narrowed it to 16 bits, so `nc HOST 65616` connected to port 80, `80x` was taken as 80, and an operand of ten or more digits overflowed a signed `int`; `nc(1)` described the narrowing as the behaviour. It breaks the manual's usage line. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the port must be decimal digits from 1 through 65535, and anything else is refused with `nc: invalid port: PORT: not a number from 1 to 65535`. `tests/host/ncport_hosttest.c` holds the rule, a twenty-digit operand included, and `netutil_mutate.sh`'s `nc-port` fault puts the narrowing back and fails it.

180. `httpd`'s status page named a fixed address, and its port operand had no bound (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes; severity P3: a wrong page, and an overflow on a long operand). `utils/httpd.c` printed `10.0.2.15:80` as the server's endpoint whatever address and port it served on, which `httpd(8)` listed under BUGS, and read the port operand into a `long` with no upper bound. It breaks the page's claim to describe the connection. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the page gives the address and port `getsockname` reports for the connection, or 0.0.0.0 and the bound port when that call fails, and a port operand that is not 1 through 65535 is refused with the usage line and status 2. `tests/host/httpd_hosttest.c` runs the server's own `main` over modeled socket calls; `netutil_mutate.sh`'s `httpd-page` fault prints the fixed endpoint and its `httpd-port` fault removes the bound, which the sanitizer's overflow report catches with status 1 on Linux.

181. ISH ran a `PATH` path cut at 1024 bytes (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes; severity P3: a command could run another program than the one named, on the Linux build and the host test only). `ish/ish.c` joined a `PATH` element and the command name into a buffer of 1025 bytes and cut the result silently at 1024, so a 1019-byte element and `prog-long-name` ran `.../prog`. Rach2 cannot reach it, since the kernel refuses a path of 256 bytes or more. It breaks the shell's rule that it runs the program named or none. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: an element whose joined path would pass 1024 bytes is skipped. `check_join` and `check_long_path` in `tests/host/ish_hosttest.c` test the fix; `ish_mutate.sh`'s fault `join` fails the first and its fault `path` the second.

182. `netup` misreported how `dhcpc` ended (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes; severity P3: a diagnostic that could say the opposite of what happened). `utils/netup.c` printed "dhcpc did not configure the interface" for any nonzero status, but `dhcpc -b` exits 0 whether or not it configured the interface, and one that exits nonzero before the background may have left its address on it. It breaks `netup(8)`'s diagnostics. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `netup` reports the exit status or the signal of a client that stopped before the background. Rows in `tests/host/netup_hosttest.c` and `netutil_mutate.sh`'s `dhcp-status` fault.

183. `k_itoa`'s `force_sign` counted a plus it never wrote (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes; severity P3: a wrong string for a caller that asks for a sign, and no caller does). `rach2/sys/lib/kstd.c` added the plus to the returned count and to the buffer-size check and wrote no plus. It breaks the function's header. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the plus is written. `tests/host/kstd_hosttest.c` requires `+7` with count 3, `0` with count 2 and `-7` with count 3, and `tests/host/kstd_mutate.sh` removes the plus in a private copy and requires exactly one failed check.

184. TCP took a reset at any sequence number, made its initial sequence number from the clock and the ports, and kept a handshake whose listener had gone (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes to `tcp.c`, which this close reviewed and kept; severity P2: a host off the path could close any connection it could name, or predict the number that spoofing one needs, and a late handshake held a control block and a socket for ever). In `rach2/sys/network/tcp/tcp.c` any reset for a connection closed it; the initial sequence number was a linear function of the tick and the ports; and a handshake that completed after its listener closed stayed established with a socket nobody could accept. It breaks RFC 5961's and RFC 6528's rules, which `connect(2)` now states, and `listen(2)`'s queue. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `tcp_rst_verdict` takes a reset only at `rcv_nxt`, answers one elsewhere in the receive window with an acknowledgement and drops one outside it, and during an active open takes only a reset that acknowledges the SYN; `tcp_isn` is the tick count times 2500 plus the low 32 bits of SipHash-2-4 of the two addresses and ports under a key drawn at the first `listen` or `connect`; and closing a listener frees the handshakes still in progress on its port with their sockets, so a peer's final acknowledgement finds no connection and a listener opened on the port later never takes one; the milestone's closing review found the first form of this, a check at the handshake's end that knew the listener only by its port, handing such a connection to a new listener on the same port with the old one's owner. `tests/host/tcp_reset_hosttest.c` holds each rule, and `tests/host/tcp_reset_mutate.sh` compiles six faults, each failing its row; entry 226 split the fault for a reset taken while opening into one for the ACK flag and one for the number, so there are seven. `tcp_hosttest.c` has a stand-in `krandom_u32`, since the key is drawn from the generator.

185. A second card's subnet broadcast left by the first card (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found in the earlier session's changes to `ip.c`; severity P3: a broadcast leaves on the wrong wire with the wrong source address, on a machine with two cards). `ip_tx` in `rach2/sys/network/ip/ip.c` sent every broadcast by the first card. It breaks `send(2)`'s broadcast rules. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `ip_broadcast_iface` sends a subnet's broadcast by the card on that subnet, with that card's address as its source, and the limited broadcast by the first card, which it picks before it compares any card's subnet, since a card in 128.0.0.0/1 has 255.255.255.255 as its subnet's broadcast address; the milestone's closing review found that case, and `ip_mutate.sh`'s `limited-scan` fault is its proof. The two-card rows in `tests/host/packet_hosttest.c` show it, and `tests/host/ip_mutate.sh`'s `first-card` fault fails them. The same review removed `ping_owner` from `icmp.c`, which was written and never read, and found the test's interface initializer two fields short, so an interface began down with a transmit count of one until a reset; no row's result depended on it, and it is filled now.

186. `fd_stdio_mutate.sh` could not finish (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the audit of the close; severity P3: a proof that stopped part way, no fault in the shipped code). Its namespace fault looked for `if (ofd->vmnt) {` in `fd_ofd_lock` and `fd_ofd_unlock`, which `216ded22` (entry 157) changed to `if (ofd->vmnt && ofd->vmnt->type->ns_serialised) {` (`rach2/sys/fs/fd.c`), so the script stopped with `fd-stdio-mutation: lock pattern is not unique` after its first four faults, and entry 157's row, that a file system which needs no serialising takes no namespace lock, had no fault. It breaks the Verification section's rule that a check is proved by a mutation. It depends on entry 157. Fixed here: the namespace fault matches the new condition and takes the namespace for KRFFS alone, and a new fault, `unserialised`, puts back the lock for every mounted file system and must fail entry 157's row. Reproduce the old failure with `sh tests/host/fd_stdio_mutate.sh` at `17274e9e`.

187. `poll_kernels.sh` could not build its second kernel (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while reviewing the comment round; severity P3: a measurement tool that stopped at its preparation). `tools/dev/poll_kernels.sh` matched `rach2/sys/io/poll.c` on a block that began with a three-line comment in the function's body, and the comment round moved that comment into `do_poll`'s header, so the script stopped with `poll-kernels: the poll block no longer matches`. It breaks entry 54's measurement procedure. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the pattern is the code lines alone, which match both the old and the new `poll.c` once. No mutation is made of it; the script's own uniqueness check is what refused.

188. Three probe binaries and an empty file were tracked in git (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while listing the files the text check reads; severity P3: stale binaries in the tree, no effect on a build). `tests/auxvprobe`, `tests/errnoprobe` and `tests/tlsprobe` are i386 executables committed in `3b81b866`, and `tests/.!93966!auxvprobe` is an empty file an interrupted copy left; the build writes its probes to `build/tests/`, and nothing reads these four. It breaks the rule that build output is not committed. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the four are removed from the tree.

189. `rngprobe` took an unopenable device as failing closed (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while wiring its fail-closed branch into `tests/run noentropy`; severity P3: a fault that made the random devices unopenable would have passed). `tests/rngprobe.c`'s fail-closed branch accepted `/dev/urandom` failing to open as a refusal and never looked at `/dev/random`. It breaks the entropy policy's claim that both devices answer `EIO` while the generator is untrusted. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `refuses_read` requires each device to open and its read to answer `EIO`. `tests/run noentropy` runs the branch on `-cpu qemu64`, and `tests/system/noentropy_mutate.sh`'s `random` fault gives `/dev/random` the operations of `/dev/zero`, which the probe's step must fail.

190. The debug recipe stopped at the reset vector (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by phase 11's check of `rach2/.gdbinit`; severity P3: `./debug.sh` left GDB at the first instruction instead of at `k_main`). The file's bare `wh` is GDB's `winheight` with no window named, an error, and an error ends a GDB command file, so its `continue` never ran; and `rach2/debug.sh` started GDB plainly, which reads a `.gdbinit` in the current directory only where its auto-load safe path allows, which by default it does not. It breaks the kernel Readme's debugging recipe. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the line is gone, and `debug.sh` starts GDB with `-iex 'set auto-load local-gdbinit off' -x .gdbinit`, which reads the file once whatever the settings. No test runs GDB; the recipe was read against GDB's documentation.

191. A cppcheck suppression gave a reason that no longer exists (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by phase 10's reading of `tools/verify/cppcheck.suppress`; severity P3: a waiver kept without its cause). The entry for `rach2/kernel.c` named `test_syscall`, which `eb615832` removed on 2026-09-14, and the file had notes written as a diary. It breaks the rule that a waiver states its reason. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the entry is removed and the notes rewritten as documentation; cppcheck 2.22 reports nothing in `kernel.c` without it, and the testing host's version, which `tools/setup/judges` records, decides.

192. The style checks read fewer files than they claimed (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by phase 10's reading of `tools/verify/check.sh`; severity P3: faults in 89 scripts and in the configuration files could pass the style suite). Step 3 ran ShellCheck on the scripts it named, which left out `tests/host`, most of `tests/system`, `tests/fuzz/build.sh`, `tests/musl/build.sh`, the fixtures, the image scripts, `tests/run`'s siblings with no suffix and `vendor/grub/*.sh`, while `check.sh` and `tools/Readme.md` said every project script; and the text check read only Markdown, shell, make, C and Python, so the configuration files were outside it. It breaks `tools/Readme.md`'s statement of what the style suite checks. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `tools/verify/scripts.sh` lists every in-house shell script, 242 of them, step 3 runs ShellCheck over that list and the text check reads it with the configuration files, the assembly, the linker script and the awk checkers; the findings this made visible are fixed, the three `muslgcc` wrappers among them, which rebuilds the vendored projects once. `tests/system/style_scope_test.sh`, run by the style suite, requires each kind of file both lists claim to be read, and its `--mutate` faults each fail a row.

193. Comments and pages stated facts the code does not keep (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found while writing the rows above; severity P3: a reader is told something false, no fault in the code). The comments before `krffs_mix_hash` and on hierarchical resolution in `rach2/sys/fs/krffs.c`, entry 125's text and `stat(2)` called the name's hash 32-bit, where it is the 64-bit DJB hash, and `stat(2)` did not say that the old `stat` call's 32-bit `st_ino` keeps the low bits; `rach2/sys/mm/pressure.h` said `mem_reclaim` works below the low mark, where it works below the high mark; `rach2/sys/block/diskinfo.h` counted ten counters in the widest record, where there are fourteen; `vfs.Readme.md` left the heap's cap out of the buffer cache's size; `exfat.Readme.md` and `set_retire`'s header named a barrier the code did not have (entry 173); `tools/verify/check_sources.sh` named `rach2/Makefile` for `C_SRC`, which is in `rach2/sources.mk`; `tests/waitprobe.c` said a live suite ran the in-house `httpd`, which none does; and `mk/Readme.md` said the compilation database covers four trees, where it has twelve units. It breaks the Writing section's rule that a comment and a page state the current contract. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, each against the code.

194. A file made in an exFAT subdirectory that had to grow was lost, and the directory named itself (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the scan-cost row of entry 27 when it first ran in the closing set; severity P1: a create reported success, the file was not made, and the directory held an entry naming its own cluster, a cycle a walk follows for ever). `dir_grow` in `rach2/sys/fs/exfat.c` publishes the directory's new length in its parent through `node_publish`, and that loads the directory's own set into the mount's shared set buffer; a create builds the new file's set in the same buffer before it asks `free_slots` for room, so after a growth it stored the directory's set in the slots meant for the file. The root has no set and never publishes, which is why the suites, whose many-file rows fill the root, never met it; 220 files in a subdirectory of 4 KiB clusters grow it five times and leave five such entries. Reproduce with the scan-cost row: `mkexfatfixture --check` reports `cross-link: /many/many` and `duplicate name: /many/many` five times. It breaks `exfat.Readme.md`'s contract that a create makes the file it names. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: `dir_grow` copies the caller's set aside before the growth publishes and puts it back after. The row is `scan_cost_rows` in `tests/host/exfat_hosttest.c`, whose volume check after the creates now passes, and `exfat_scan_mutate.sh`'s `grow` fault leaves the copy out, which fails it.

195. `disk write` overwrote the last sector of a partition that reaches the end of the disk (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the milestone's closing review; severity P2: the recovery shell's round-trip writes a pattern over data, on the Lenovo the last sector of its root). `disk_write_roundtrip` in `rach2/kernel.c` took the last sector for scratch space past every MBR partition, but a partition may reach it: the installer grows the root into the whole disk, and the Lenovo's partition 3 ends at sector 488397167, the disk's last. Reproduce with a 32 MiB disk whose one partition runs from sector 2048 to the end, booted on the recovery kernel with `rach2.rescue`, and `disk write`. It breaks the command's claim that the round-trip disturbs no file system. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the command reads the MBR and refuses a sector one of the four primary entries holds, an extended entry covering its logical partitions, with `disk: the last sector belongs to partition N, not a scratch sector`. `tests/system/gpt_write_refusal.sh` has two MBR disks: one whose partition reaches the last sector, which must be refused and left as it was, and one whose partition ends before it, which must take the round-trip and change in that sector alone; `gpt_write_mutate.sh`'s third fault makes the check answer that no partition holds the sector, where the row must fail on the disk whose partition reaches it. The row's first run, in the close's proofs, found its own check of the written pattern wrong, since it did not reduce the pattern's bytes to eight bits as the kernel does; the check is corrected, the corrected row passed in the full sets of 2026-09-25, and the fault failed it in that day's run of `gpt_write_mutate.sh`.

196. exec opened a program on a mounted KRFFS volume without the lock that open holds (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the milestone's closing review of entry 160's fix; severity P2: a description registered on a moved node, which a later relocation does not repoint, reads or writes whatever takes the old place). Entry 160 made `do_open` hold the namespace lock from the lookup to the registration for a type that moves its files. The program and the interpreter lookups of exec in `rach2/sys/syscall_proc.c` resolved without `VFS_WALK_LOCK` and then called `vfs_open_node`, the same window: a writer that relocated the program from A to B and slept at the publication flush, before it repointed the open records, let exec register a second record at B, and the writer's own record then pointed at a place the next relocation freed. It breaks entry 160's contract. It depends on entry 160. Fixed here: both lookups take `VFS_WALK_LOCK` and hold it through `vfs_open_node`, and every exit releases it with `vfs_walk_unlock`. No row drives exec into that window: the relocation proof of entry 160 extracts `do_open` and its helpers, and the exec lookups sit inside a function too large to extract; the change is the one entry 160 proved for open, made in the same form, and the suites that exec from mounted volumes run it.

197. A timeout could hide the machine's last output from the harness's judgement (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the milestone's closing review; severity P2: output written just before a run's deadline, a `--never` text among it, went unread, so a run could pass on an `--ok` token that came earlier). At the end of a run `tests/system/runner/harness.c` stopped the machine and read what was left with `pump`, whose loop checks the run's deadline before it reads, so after a timeout it read nothing. The same review found two more faults of the harness's input, which entry 176 had made non-blocking: a `send=` that did not reach the guest counted as no failure, so a guest that printed the `--ok` token and stopped reading passed, and a long `sendfile=` whose chunks the guest kept taking wrote past the deadline, while `pump`'s 200 ms wait could cross it. It breaks the harness's contract that every step ends by the deadline and that the judgement reads all the machine wrote. It depends on entry 176. Fixed here: `drain_rest` reads the pipe to its end once the machine is stopped, whatever the deadline; `send_text` returns whether the line reached the guest and a `send=` that did not is a failed step; `send_bytes` checks the deadline before every write; and `pump` waits no longer than the time left. The harness's selftest passes with them, and its `input_deadline_selftest` still requires a write to a machine that stopped reading to end at the deadline; of the four new behaviours, the reading after a timeout has had a selftest row since the close's audits, `late_output_selftest`, which `harness_input_mutate.sh`'s `late` fault must fail: in the script's run on the testing host on 2026-09-25 the selftest passed, the fault failed it and the restored source passed (entry 211 puts the selftest into `tests/run runner`); a `send=` counted as a failed step, a `sendfile=` ended at the deadline while the guest keeps taking its chunks, and `pump`'s capped wait have none.

198. `dhcpc` could remove a fixed address that its first lease repeated (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the milestone's closing review of entry 178; severity P3: a machine configured by its boot line loses its address, in a window of two system calls). `apply` in `utils/dhcpc.c` records the lease's address in the claim before it sets it, so that a client killed in between leaves a claim the cleanup compares before it removes; but when the interface already had that address, set by the boot line, the comparison matched, and `dhcpc -k` after such a death took off an address this client never put on. It breaks the claim's contract, stated beside `apply`. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: a first lease whose address the interface already has is recorded as none, until a renewal brings another address. `check_initial` in `tests/host/dhcpc_hosttest.c` has the row, on a model that now answers `SIOCGIFADDR`, and `netutil_mutate.sh`'s `adopted` fault records the address again, which fails it.

199. Two proofs that ran one exFAT host test at once shared its fixtures (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found when the closing proofs ran side by side; severity P3: a proof that fails for a reason of its own, no fault in the drivers). `exfat_hosttest.c` and `fat32_hosttest.c` build their fixture tools and images in `/tmp/rach2_verify`, the host suite's directory, and `fat_barriers_mutate.sh` and `exfat_scan_mutate.sh` both run `exfat_hosttest`; with the two at once, one ran `mkexfatfixture` while the other was writing it, `Permission denied`, and the unmodified driver failed its barrier rows. Reproduce by starting the two proofs together. It breaks the proofs' contract that the unmodified tree passes. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the two tests take `FIXDIR` from the compiler when it is given, and each proof gives its own directory under `/tmp` and removes it at exit. The test that shows it fixed is the two proofs run at once, which they were on the testing host on 2026-09-25, each with a fixture directory of its own: the scan proof passed, and the barrier proof proved all fifteen exFAT sites, its FAT32 half finding entry 209.

200. The USB proofs could not link their private kernels (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the closing run of `usb_latency_mutate.sh`; severity P3: two proofs that cannot run, no fault in the kernel). Since `0432d381` the kernel's link rule runs make in the directory above `rach2/` to build the ELF checker (`rach2/Makefile`), and a private tree that holds only `rach2/` and `mk/` has no Makefile there, so make stops with `No rule to make target 'build/tools/inspect/elfcheck'` before the first build. `usb_latency_mutate.sh`, new at this close and entry 129's proof, and `usbdisk_mutate.sh`, entry 80's, build that way; `gpt_write_mutate.sh` already gave its tree the rule. Reproduce with `sh tests/system/usb_latency_mutate.sh`. It breaks the two entries' proofs. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: both scripts write a Makefile with an empty rule for the checker into the private tree, and `ELFCHECK` names the built one. The test that shows it fixed is each proof's run; both passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25, `usbdisk_mutate.sh` after entry 206's fix.

201. `synth_open_mutate.sh` did not compile on the testing host (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the closing proofs; severity P3: a proof that cannot run where the suites run). The proof compiles `tests/host/devfs_hosttest.c` with `-O1 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` under the sanitizers, and GCC 15.2, the testing host's compiler, read `dev_screenlog_write` in `devfs.c` through the test's `copy_from_user` stub, which failed without writing its destination, and reported `digit` as maybe used uninitialized. The kernel's own copy writes the byte or fails, and the byte is read only after a copy that succeeded. Reproduce with `sh tests/host/synth_open_mutate.sh` on the testing host. It breaks the proof. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the stub clears its destination before it fails; the test compiled with the proof's options passes under GCC 15.2, and the proof passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25.

202. Three mutation scripts could not run, because a text they search for had changed (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the close's second audit, which counted the search texts of the scripts the close took as not run against the current source, two of which had in fact run and exited 1, as the notes record; severity P3: proofs that stop before their first fault, no fault in the shipped code). `tests/system/fdlock_mutate.sh`'s namespace fault looked in `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c` for `if (ofd->vmnt && ofd->vmnt->type == &vfs_krffs_type) {`, which `216ded22` (entry 157) changed to a test of `ns_serialised`, the change that stopped `fd_stdio_mutate.sh` in entry 186; `tests/system/host_tools_mutate.sh`'s two gap faults looked in `krffs_write` of `krffs/krffs_fuse_operations.c` for the gap test that `2f302eb3` moved into `krffs_write_into_node`; and `tests/system/recovery_disk_key_mutate.sh`'s installer fault looked in `utils/rach2-install` for the `cp` of the key file, which the closing review's fix replaced with a `printf` of the checked copy. Reproduce with each script, which stops with its pattern error before its fault. It breaks the Verification section's rule that a check is proved by a mutation. It depends on entries 157 and 186 for the first and on the closing review's fix for the third. Fixed here: the namespace fault matches the current test; each gap fault passes `offset` as the old size at its call of `krffs_write_into_node`, the slack path's or the growth path's, so the gap is not cleared on that path; and the installer fault replaces the `printf`. After entries 186 and 187 this is the same fault a third time: a change to a source that a mutation script searches can leave the script unable to run, and only running the script, or counting its texts, shows it. All three passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25.

203. A background child that was never handed the claim took off an address the interface already had (2026-09-24, milestone 06's close, found by the close's third audit, which read the headers of the functions the closing review's fixes changed; severity P3: a fixed address lost, in a window as narrow as entry 198's). Entry 198 made a first lease whose address the interface already had record none, and `dhcpc -k` leave such an address; but `go_background` in `utils/dhcpc.c`, when its parent dies before it hands the claim over, took off the address in `holding` whenever the interface still had it, without asking whether this client had put it there. Reproduce with a boot line that sets the address the server then offers and a parent killed between the fork and the handshake. It breaks the claim's contract stated beside `apply`. It depends on entry 198. Fixed here: the child leaves an adopted address and removes the claim, as `-k` does. `check_handoff_adopted` in `tests/host/dhcpc_hosttest.c` is the row, the handshake answering with an end of file, and `netutil_mutate.sh`'s `handoff-adopted` fault takes the check out; the host suite passed with the row in the full set of 2026-09-25, and the fault failed it in that day's run of the script.

204. The machine probes' pipe mode read a disk the installed boot does not publish (2026-09-25, milestone 06's close, found while preparing the sitting; severity P3: a measurement that cannot run where it is meant to). `tests/system/manual_probes.sh`'s `pipe` mode, written at this close for entry 26, read `/dev/disk0`, and a boot of the installed system publishes the partitions and not the whole disk: the Lenovo lists `/dev/disk0p1` to `p3` and `/dev/disk1`, and `dd if=/dev/disk0` there finds no such file. Reproduce with `ls /dev/disk0` on the installed machine. It breaks the script's own header, which names the installed machine as its target. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the mode reads 128 MiB from the root's own partition, the device `/proc/mounts` names for `/`, and says which device it read; on the Lenovo on 2026-09-25 it read `/dev/disk0p3`.

205. The kernel reached the size of its boot slot (2026-09-25, milestone 06's close, found when the install stick was built for the sitting; severity P2: a kernel a few kilobytes larger fits no boot slot, so no sealed disk, stick or `bootctl kernel` can take it). `build/rach2/kernel` had its debug sections in it, half of the file, and a boot partition's kernel slot holds 2097152 bytes: the testing host's kernel of `11ac2929` left 4336 bytes of it, and the Mac's compiler made the kernel 2097440 bytes, so `make rach2-install-stick` stopped with `mkdisk_grub: the kernel is 2097440 bytes and the slot has 2097152`. Reproduce with that build on the Mac at `0fb32372`. It breaks the contract that a kernel the build makes goes into a slot. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the link writes the kernel with its debug sections to `kernel.debug`, which GDB reads (`rach2/.gdbinit`), and strips them from `kernel`, 1049112 bytes on the Mac; the rule lists `rach2/Makefile` among its inputs, so a changed recipe makes the kernel again. `tests/system/build_inputs_test.sh` requires the kernel it links to have no debug sections and `kernel.debug` to have them, and `host_tools_mutate.sh`'s `kernel-strip` fault copies the linked file without stripping it, which failed the row on the testing host on 2026-09-25. The Lenovo has run a stripped kernel since that day, and the stick's kernel is stripped too.

206. Four mutation tests could not show their faults (2026-09-25, milestone 06's close, found by the closing run of the mutation tests; severity P3: proofs that fail or prove nothing, no fault in the shipped code). `tests/system/usbdisk_mutate.sh` gave the harness a `wait=1` step, which the harness has refused since `ed1361e0` replaced such steps with tokens, so the test failed in its fixed phase with `unknown step: wait=1`; the `usbdisk` suite's own pull has no such step. `tests/host/exfat_scan_mutate.sh` removed its private fixture directory, entry 199's, in the function it also calls between phases, so its second build found no directory to write into. And `tests/system/gpt_write_mutate.sh`'s two GPT faults were judged on GPT disks with a protective MBR, whose entry covers the whole disk, so entry 195's MBR check refused the write with the GPT refusal taken out, and the fault could not show the disk written. And `tests/host/fat_barriers_mutate.sh`'s `restore` looped over the two drivers in the variable its site loop keeps its driver in, and a shell function shares its caller's variables, so after the first exFAT site every exFAT site was dropped from `fat32.c` by the exFAT index and judged by the FAT32 test; its log labelled exFAT's functions `fat32.c`. Reproduce with each script. It breaks the Verification section's rule that a check is proved by a mutation. It depends on entries 195 and 199. Fixed here: the pull follows the holding token as the suite's does; the fixture directory is removed only at exit; and `gpt_write_refusal.sh` has two GPT disks with no protective MBR, the second with its primary header zeroed too, which the GPT checks alone keep and on which the two GPT faults are judged. `restore` has a loop variable of its own. The first run of the fixed GPT test failed the same way on another disk: the row stops at its first failure, and with the refusal taken out disk b, which the MBR check refuses in that check's words, came before g. The two disks with no protective MBR now run first, so each GPT fault stops the row on a disk it wrote, and `gpt_write_mutate.sh` passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25. The other three tests of this entry passed there the same day.

207. Entry 120's row could not fail (2026-09-25, milestone 06's close, found by the closing run of `tests/system/rows_mutate.sh`; severity P3: a row that proves nothing, no fault in the shipped code). With the operations check taken out of `disk_node_of` in `rach2/sys/fs/devfs.c`, `devprobe` still passed. A device table entry read as a disk node has, where the node keeps its partition number, the major and minor of the entry two further on, and no entry has both zero, so `BLKRRPART` on /dev/null answers `ENOTTY` with or without the check. The same boot's `procprobe` failed its `/proc/net/route` row, which it passes on the unmodified kernel: without the check, each open of a table device also adds one to a word of kernel memory through the misread node's `disk` field, which is the address of another entry's name, so what the fault breaks depends on how the kernel was linked. Reproduce with `sh tests/system/rows_mutate.sh`, which stops with `devprobe did not fail with its fault compiled in`. It breaks the Verification section's rule that a check is proved by a mutation. It depends on entry 120. Fixed here: `devprobe` requires fstat and the `/dev` listing to give /dev/null one inode. The listing numbers a leaf by its place in the table, and fstat by the descriptor's record, which without the check is numbered as a disk node, so the row fails on any build. The ioctl row stays, for the answer the request must give. With the row, `rows_mutate.sh`'s devprobe phase failed as it must on the testing host on 2026-09-25. Entry 226 moved the fault into `entry_index` alone, since the check taken out of `disk_node_of` also let each open of a device table entry add one to a word of kernel memory through the misread node, and `rows_mutate.sh` now requires this row's own failure line.

208. The machine probes stopped at their survey on the Mac and reported success (2026-09-25, milestone 06's close, found by the sitting's first run of `manual_probes.sh probes`; severity P2: a measurement script reported a pass for probes it never ran). The survey's script is a here-document inside `$( )`, and it contained a `case` pattern. Bash 3.2, which is `/bin/sh` on the Mac, takes the pattern's `)` for the end of the substitution and runs the rest of the document as the script's own code, where `$tty` is unset, so `set -u` stopped the script with `tty: unbound variable`. The EXIT trap then gave status 0: bash 3.2 hands an EXIT trap a status of 0 when `set -u` stops a script that also has `set -e`, as this one does (entry 222). The rehearsal on the testing host passed because its `sh` is dash. Reproduce with `sh tests/system/manual_probes.sh KEY ADDRESS probes DIR` on the Mac before this fix. It breaks the script's contract that status 0 means every probe passed. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: the survey's loop tests the terminal's owner with `if`, and the EXIT trap fails a run that reached no success exit. A scan of the tree's 232 other `.sh` files found no other here-document inside a command substitution with a parenthesis left open. It also said that no other script the Mac runs combines `set -u` with an EXIT trap, which was wrong; entry 222 corrects it. The fixed script's run on the Lenovo passed nine of nine.

209. The FAT32 truncate's flush before a cut failed no row (2026-09-25, milestone 06's close, found by the first run of `tests/host/fat_barriers_mutate.sh` after entry 206's fix; severity P3: a barrier no row proves, no fault in the driver). With the flush in `fat_truncate` that puts a shorter size on the medium before the chain is cut (`rach2/sys/fs/fat32.c:1826`) dropped, the matrix passed. The truncate sequence shrinks a file right after writing it, and that write's size and first cluster are still pending in the directory block the truncate rewrites, so no state the model leaves has the old size over the cut chain; the unlink row had the same blind spot, which entry 32 answered with an unlink after an `fsync`. Reproduce by dropping that site and running the driver's host test with `--barriers`. It breaks the Verification section's rule that a check is proved by a mutation. It depends on entry 32. Fixed here: a truncate after an `fsync`, a file of 2010 bytes cut to 700, which with the flush dropped fails `'truncate after a sync' stopped after write 10 of 13, the newest 1 of the pending writes kept, leaves a consistent volume`; over the unmodified driver the barrier rows pass. `fat_barriers_mutate.sh` passed with it on the testing host on 2026-09-25: every site but `fat_rmdir`'s flush fails a row when dropped. The two files of the sequences after an `fsync` were not read back once the sequences ran, which `tests/host/Readme.md` said of every sequence; the scan of entry 215 found that, and `write_rows` now requires the file unlinked after its `fsync` to be gone and the one truncated after its `fsync` to read its first 700 bytes, and `tests/host/fat_synced_mutate.sh` compiles the test against a private copy of `rach2/sys`, where `fat_unlink` leaving the name fails the first row and `fat_truncate`'s shrink leaving the old size fails the second. It passed on the testing host on 2026-09-25, on the build from clean of 14:34 by that host's clock, and neither the driver nor its test changed before the day's last build, of 23:32.

210. The rows mutation test took a probe killed by a signal for one that passed (2026-09-25, milestone 06's close, found by the second closing run of `tests/system/rows_mutate.sh`; severity P3: a proof that stops on the fault it should see, no fault in the kernel). With entry 121's fault compiled in, the kernel enters a one-shot handler at address zero and `sigprobe` dies of signal 11, which the probe runner reports as `sigprobe killed by signal 11 FAIL`; the script's pattern asked for `status=` before `FAIL`, so it stopped with `sigprobe did not fail with its fault compiled in`. Reproduce with that script. It breaks the Verification section's rule that a check is proved by a mutation. It depends on nothing. Fixed here: a probe fails by either form of the runner's line.
