The parent took the child to be in `recvfrom` 50 ms after the fork. It breaks the same rule as entry 250 (`AGENTS.md:53`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, e5d00569: the parent signals the child once `/proc/PID/stat` shows it asleep, up to a bound whose end fails a row of its own.

252. Seven rows of five freestanding probes count a child that a signal killed as one that exited with 0 (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the review of area 8; severity P3: a kernel fault that kills the child a row judges passes the row). Each reads only the exit byte of the status `waitpid` returns, which is 0 for a child a signal ended: the getppid row of `tests/miscprobe.c`, the two reader rows of `tests/pipeprobe.c`, `run_child` in `tests/jobprobe.c`, the row `the child that held sockets held some` of `tests/rawprobe.c`, and the row of `tests/mountprobe.c` that runs a program from the mount. Reproduce with the getppid row by making `sys_getppid` post `SIGSEGV` to its caller: the row passes. It breaks the rule that a row fails when its fault is present (`AGENTS.md:52`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, da586827: each row requires `waitpid` to return the child and a status with no signal beside the exit code it wants. The faults getppid-kills-caller, pipe-reader-exit-killed, pipe-refill-exit-killed, job-setpgid-exit-killed and job-setsid-exit-killed fail the rows of the default suite; the rawprobe and mountprobe rows run in the raw and mount suites, and their faults were run there by hand, each failing its row.

253. The memory suite's fail mode reported `rach2.framefail` missing from a boot line that had it (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the memory suite of the full set; severity P3: the suite failed with a false message at 64 MiB). `arm` in `tests/memprobe.c` opened `/dev/framefail` for each count; a count that a fork or an exec left unspent failed the next frame allocation in a system call, which the disarm's own open could make for its description, and the open answered `EMFILE`, which the probe reported as the switch being off. Reproduce by running `memprobe fail` at 64 MiB on this close's kernel, whose heap then needs a new page at that open. It breaks the rule that a row fails only when its fault is present (`AGENTS.md:52`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, c90c464e: the probe opens the device once before the first count and writes every count through that descriptor, and the device's write allocates nothing; the memory suite passed.

254. Every failure of `fd_alloc` reads as `EMFILE`, a failed allocation among them (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found while fixing entry 253; severity P3: a caller told its table is full when the heap had no room for a description, which Linux reports as `ENOMEM`). `fd_alloc_from` in `rach2/sys/fs/fd.c` returns -1 for a full table and for a failed `ofd_alloc` or `fd_table_grow`, and its callers answer -1 with `EMFILE`, but for `put_rights` in `rach2/sys/ipc/unix.c`, which stops installing a message's descriptors there, closes the rest and sets `MSG_CTRUNC`. Reproduce by running `memprobe fail` as it stood before c90c464e. It breaks Linux's open(2) and the other calls that make a descriptor, whose `EMFILE` is the descriptor limit and `ENOMEM` a failed allocation; this kernel's page gives `EMFILE` for both (`man/man2/open.2:546`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 1ce4c627 and 4a70f1ef: `fd_alloc_from` returns `-EMFILE` at the descriptor limit and `-ENOMEM` for a failed allocation, each call that makes a descriptor returns what it gets, `put_rights` truncates for both, and the pages give `ENOMEM`. `fd_hosttest.c`, `open_hosttest.c` and `netif_hosttest.c` fail each allocation, and `fd_stdio_mutate.sh`, `open_mutate.sh` and `netif_mutate.sh` prove them.

255. A GNU program saw no terminal anywhere, since GNU libc asks with requests the kernel did not answer (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the gnu suite's password login; severity P2: OpenSSH's server ended every session that asked for a terminal, with `openpty returns device for which ttyname fails`, and every GNU program that asked whether a descriptor was a terminal was told it was not). GNU libc 2.42 and later make `tcgetattr` and `isatty` with `TCGETS2` and `tcsetattr` with `TCSETS2`, `TCSETSW2` and `TCSETSF2`, the termios2 requests, and `tty_common_ioctl` in `rach2/sys/syscall.c` answered only the older four, so every one was `ENOTTY`. Reproduce by calling `tcgetattr` on a pty slave from a program linked with the GNU libc. It breaks ioctl(2)'s terminal requests as Linux answers them (`man/man2/ioctl.2:62`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 375afb4a and 24e221ff: the console and pty slaves answer the four with the 44-byte termios2, whose speeds are those the baud codes of `c_cflag` name, and a set takes the settings and does not keep the speeds; ptyprobe's slave rows require both, and the faults tcgets2-unknown, tcsetsw2-unknown and tcgets2-no-speed fail them.

256. The demonstration accounts' password had expired by the rule of shadow(5), which OpenSSH enforces (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the gnu suite's password login; severity P2: a password login over SSH to the GNU system was sent to a password change, which it could not make, and ended). `krffs/gen_demo_password.sh` wrote each line with a last change of day 0, which shadow(5) reads as a change root forced at the next login; BusyBox's login and the project's credential library ignore the aging fields, and OpenSSH's server does not. Reproduce by logging in by password as `test` to the GNU system. It breaks the demonstration accounts' promise that their password opens a login (`Readme.md:37`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 26c7efe7, 11fbc3a0 and a9177cd3: the lines have their seven aging fields empty, a shadow minted with day 0 keeps its hashes and has those fields emptied, and the script is a prerequisite of `krffs/shadow`, so a tree that has one takes the change; passwd(5) says so, and the gnu suite's password login passed.

257. OpenSSL's test server could not start on a TCP socket, since `FIONBIO` was answered on pipes, ptys and AF_UNIX sockets alone (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the gnu suite's curl row; severity P2: every program that sets a socket non-blocking with `ioctl(FIONBIO)`, as OpenSSL's `BIO_socket_nbio` does, failed on AF_INET). `sys_ioctl` in `rach2/sys/syscall.c` passed the request to a descriptor's own operations, which a TCP or UDP socket does not have, and answered `ENOTTY`. Reproduce by `openssl s_server -accept 127.0.0.1:4433`. It breaks ioctl(2), whose `FIONBIO` Linux answers in its common layer for every descriptor not opened with `O_PATH` (`man/man2/ioctl.2:24`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 2d04e6c2 and 665e11c1: `sys_ioctl` answers `FIONBIO` for every descriptor not opened with `O_PATH`, before its own operations, as `F_SETFL` sets `O_NONBLOCK`, and the four copies it made unreachable are gone; sockflagmusl sets and clears it on a TCP connection, the fault fionbio-per-type fails that row, and the gnu suite's curl row passed.

258. Chromium's zygote stopped at its check that it runs one thread, since `/proc/PID/task` gave one link (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the gnu suite's chromium row; severity P2: no process the zygote forked could start, and the browser ended with `GPU process isn't usable`). Chromium's `ThreadHelpers::IsSingleThreaded` reads the link count of `/proc/self/task/` and requires 3 for one thread, as Linux gives 2 and one for each thread, and `synth_stat` in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` gave every synthetic directory 1. Reproduce by `stat` of `/proc/self/task/`, which gave a count of 1. It breaks proc(5)'s agreement with Linux (`man/man5/proc.5:486`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, f2cce45c: the task directory's count is 2 and one for each live thread (`procfs_task_links`); threadmusl requires 4 with two threads and 3 in a child with one, and the fault task-dir-one-link fails the first.

259. A thread could not give up its capabilities, since the kernel did not answer `capget` and `capset` (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the gnu suite's chromium row once entry 258 was fixed; severity P2: every process Chromium's zygote forked ended at its first step, which drops all capabilities, and no page loaded). The kernel's privilege was the effective user ID alone, and calls 184 and 185 were not in its table. Reproduce by running `chromium-headless-shell --no-sandbox --headless --dump-dom` on a page. It breaks the Linux i386 system call interface the kernel implements (`Readme.md:9`) and the GNU system is built against. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 44893094: a thread has every capability or none; `capset` with every set empty gives them up, which makes the thread unprivileged whatever its user IDs, and every privilege decision of the kernel reads `proc_privileged`; a partial set or an inheritable capability is refused with `EINVAL`, where Linux keeps it (`capget(2)`). Four review rounds of the change found what it had missed, fixed in b346b1ce, 18554d8b, 2b4dd1e7, be12b1c2, 5a4e0bb8 and 04233ab1: an exec gives the capabilities back only where Linux would, holding off `CLONE_FS` meanwhile, and leaves that image not dumpable; the header's version is read before its pid; a thread without them may not attach to, open the memory of or read the robust list of a thread of another process that has them, nor may a privileged thread make its tracer, with `PTRACE_TRACEME`, a parent process with a live thread that is not privileged, a process being judged as a tracer by every live thread it has; a thread still reaches its own process's `/proc` files; and the owner checks of metadata root owns stay owner checks. Capmusl's rows require each part, and its eighteen faults fail them. With entries 258 and this one, Chromium loads a page through its zygote.

260. The ext2 suite's lchown and dirent rows could never pass, since their patterns had parentheses the harness refuses, and the harness left its machine running (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the ext2 suite of the full set; severity P3: two rows that failed with nothing in their logs but the shell's prompt, and a machine left behind by each run outside the runner). `boot_root`'s steps in `tests/system/suites/ext2.sh` gave `expect=LCHOWNMUSL OK (links)`, and `regex_compile` in `tests/system/runner/harness.c` ends a run at a pattern with a group, with status 2, when the step comes, after QEMU has started. Reproduce by running the ext2 suite as it stood. It breaks the rule that a row fails when its fault is present, and only then (`AGENTS.md:52`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, bcbb04a6 and c10b9dac: the rows escape their parentheses, and the harness compiles every step's pattern before it starts the machine.

261. Chromium runs with no disk cache, no inotify and no netlink, and cannot adjust a renderer's OOM score (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found by the gnu suite's chromium row; severity P3: each is logged as an error and none stops a page from loading). The disk cache maps its index file shared and writable, which the kernel refuses with `ENODEV` for a file on a disk file system, since it has no write-back of mapped pages (mm Readme); `inotify_init` is not a call the kernel has; an `AF_NETLINK` socket is `EAFNOSUPPORT`; and `/proc/PID/oom_score_adj` is not a file of `/proc`. Reproduce by reading the browser's log of the gnu suite's row. It breaks nothing the manual promises. It depends on nothing. Fixed here for the OOM score, 31c602fe and de1909f6: `/proc/PID/oom_score_adj` reads and writes a process's adjustment, which a fork passes on, with Linux's range, the floor a privileged write sets, the owner's right to write it, and Linux's `kstrtoint` reading of the text; schedmusl's rows and `procfs_hosttest.c` prove it, with the faults of schedmusl's group and `synth_open_mutate.sh`. The rest followed on 2026-10-01, from the owner's survey of the machine as a user: the inotify calls, 446f4833; a netlink route socket, b358eb30; the write-back of shared writable mappings of ext2 files, 627059d7 and 2a4236e0; and D-Bus's system bus, which the browser asked for next, a1cb4dde. The gnu suite's chromium row requires a browser that prints nothing of D-Bus, and its rows and inotifymusl, netlinkmusl and mapmusl each fail under their faults.

262. The AHCI stress probe failed once, and why is not known (2026-09-28, milestone 54's close, found by the stress-ahci suite of the close's first full set; severity P3 until the cause is known: a probe that writes 33 MiB through the page cache of a disk-backed root reported a fault 27 seconds into its run). `tests/stressprobe.c` printed `STRESSPROBE FAIL` on the AHCI disk, where the same image on the IDE disk passed in the same set; the suite passed in the two sets after it, and its log, `build/images/stress-test-ahci.disk.log`, was overwritten by the next run before anyone read it. Reproduce by running `tests/run stress-ahci` until it fails. It breaks the stress suites' proof that a file system larger than the page cache round-trips through it (`tests/stressprobe.c:9`). What it depends on is not known. Since db3ce7d7 the probe prints the step, the file and the failing call's result, or the offset that differs. Fixed here for the logs, d766296b and e8e054fc: the runner keeps a failing suite's serial logs and its machines' console in a directory of their own, which no later run writes over, and its selftest requires that. The failure has not come back: the suite passed in ten runs on 2026-09-28 on the leftovers' tree. The cause is likely entry 277's: the probe writes through the page cache, whose faults take the AHCI driver's polled path, which gave a command one second where IDE's gives five, and the IDE disk passed in the same set. Left: to confirm it, from the kept logs of a failure, which now name the command and why.

263. The stat calls by path can read a node of a mounted KRFFS volume that a write has moved (2026-09-27, milestone 54's close, found with entry 239; severity P3: a stat or an access check can report another node's attributes, or bytes of another file read as a node). `do_stat64`, `fstatat_path`, `do_old_stat` and `do_access` in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` resolve the path without `VFS_WALK_LOCK` and read the node without the lock, so a growing write that holds the lock and moves the file after the walk leaves them the old offset, as entry 239 left `chmod` and `chown`. Reproduce with `open_hosttest.c`'s model, in which a read of the node sleeps when the mount's lock does not cover it and a growing write moves the file in that sleep; a probe cannot place the write there. It breaks `stat(2)`, which reports the file the path names (`man/man2/stat.2:28`). It depends on the volume's numeric handles. Fixed here, 2df9a036: each call walks with `VFS_WALK_LOCK` and keeps the lock until the node is read, as `do_chmod` does since fb6f952c. `open_hosttest.c` runs a stat64, an old stat, an fstatat and an access of a file a write moves, and `open_mutate.sh` compiles each walk without the lock, which fails its row.

264. `execve` checked the file its path named and then loaded the program by the path again, so a file put at the path between the two ran with the first's checks and set-id bits (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found while fixing entry 236; severity P2: a caller who can replace a name another program's path passes through could run a file of its own with a set-id program's IDs). `exec_open` in `rach2/sys/syscall_proc.c` resolved the path for the permission and set-id checks, and the load opened the path once more. Reproduce with `open_hosttest.c`'s model, which replaces the file at the path while the exec sleeps between its checks and its load. It breaks execve(2)'s rule that the checks are those of the file that runs. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, d854fe94: the address space keeps the file the checks were made on, and the load reads that file; `mm_hosttest.c` and `open_hosttest.c` have the rows, and `open_mutate.sh` compiles a load by path, which fails them.

265. `kstack_free` gave the boot stack to the heap when a record whose stack it was went (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found while fixing entry 248; severity P2: the heap could hand out the pages of the stack process 0 and the debug shell's programs run on). It freed every stack whose top was not 0, and a thread of a program on the boot stack that calls `execve` takes its leader's place, whose record then goes (`exec_take_leader`). Reproduce with `fixtures/kstack.c`, whose heap refuses a free of what it did not give. It breaks the heap's contract that only what it gave is given back (`rach2/sys/mm/Readme.md`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 8a20802e: the boot stack's top is left alone, and `kstack_mutate.sh` removes the test, which fails the fixture's row.

266. A FAT32 or exFAT file's record locks were keyed by a number a rename changes, so a lock taken before a rename did not meet one taken after it (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found while fixing entry 238; severity P2: two processes could hold write locks over the same bytes of a file on a FAT volume). `fd_lock_key` in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` keyed them by the inode number an open records, which the FAT types derive from the directory entry's place. Reproduce by locking a file, renaming it to another directory and locking it again from a second process. It breaks `fcntl(2)`'s locks, which are on a file whatever its name. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 94d6baa7: they are keyed by the file's node, which a rename updates in place, with rows in `fat32_hosttest.c` and `exfat_hosttest.c`, each failing when the key is the number.

267. An orphan's description locks stayed in the table after the KRFFS root freed the orphan, so a node made at its place met them (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found while fixing entry 238; severity P2: a new file could be refused a lock that no open file held). The last close of an unlinked file freed its node before the locks of its description went, and 21ab7a2b's keys are the node's place. Reproduce by locking an unlinked file, closing it, making a file at the freed place and locking that. It breaks `fcntl(2)`, whose locks go with the last close. It depends on entry 238's keys. Fixed here, 370262cc: `fd_free_orphan` drops the locks before the node is freed, with a row in `lock_hosttest.c` that fails without it.

268. A process's POSIX record locks belonged to the process, so a process sharing its descriptor table could not drop them by a close, and the exit of one sharer took them from the others (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the review of entry 238's fix; severity P3: a lock held by threads made with `CLONE_FILES` and not `CLONE_THREAD` behaved unlike Linux's). Linux's owner of a POSIX lock is the `files_struct`, the descriptor table. Reproduce with lockmusl's rows of a table shared by two processes. It breaks `fcntl(2)`'s statement of the owner. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, fe6f9bb6 and 7b57b537: the table owns them; lockmusl's rows fail under the faults posix-owner-is-the-process, getlk-names-a-sharer-of-the-owner and sharer-exit-drops-table-locks, and `lock_hosttest.c` has a row of each.

269. A `mkdir` whose parent another process filled meanwhile answered `ENOSPC` or `EIO` where the name had been taken (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found while fixing entry 243; severity P3: a race of two `mkdir` of one name gave the loser an error that names a full or failing volume). `do_mkdir` in `rach2/sys/syscall_fs.c` looked the name up, let go of the parent, and made the directory under a new lock, where KRFFS refused a taken name as a taken identifier. Reproduce with `open_hosttest.c`'s model, in which another mkdir runs between the lookup and the creation. It breaks `mkdir(2)`, whose answer for a taken name is `EEXIST`. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 46931fae: the walk ends holding the parent's lock and the creation runs under it, with a row that fails without it.

270. `rmdir` on the KRFFS root and on a KRFFS volume removed a directory a descriptor or a working directory referred to, so the reference was left on a place a freed directory had (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found while fixing entry 231; severity P2: a later directory made at that place was reached through the old descriptor or working directory). A KRFFS directory's node is freed at its removal; Linux keeps an unlinked directory for as long as it is referred to, and this kernel cannot. Reproduce with fdstale's rows, which remove a directory held open, held by `O_PATH`, and entered by a process. It breaks the rule that a descriptor names the directory it was opened on. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, cafcc085, 86640bb1 and 4ffe2842: `rmdir` refuses such a directory with `EBUSY`, which `rmdir(2)` states, and `chdir` keeps a reference to the directory from its lookup until the process's record has it; fdstale's rows fail under the faults of its group.

271. The host tests wrote their fixtures under fixed names in a shared `/tmp`, so two runs at once, by two trees or two users, overwrote each other's files (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the host suite run beside a proof; severity P3: a failure with no fault in the code, or a pass on another run's files). `ext2_hosttest.c`, `pwauthatexit_hosttest.c` and the others named their files in `/tmp` itself, and the host suite, the analysers and coverage kept theirs in fixed `/tmp` directories. Reproduce by running the host suite in two trees at once. It breaks the rule that a test judges only what it made. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 33def1bd, 3e350ee1, 35f09bc4, e5860f31, 8b620642, 6c3eeaf0 and 0175124d: each test makes a directory of its own under `$TMPDIR`, or `/tmp` when that is no plain absolute path a shell passes through, keeps it when the test fails and formats every command and path through `fix_format`; the suites keep their files under `build/`. `fixdir_hosttest.c` and `verify_temp_test.sh` check them, and `fixture_readers_mutate.sh` and `ext2_batch_mutate.sh` run with them.

272. A pty master's registration for output room was not told of room its own input made, when a signal character or an erase took bytes out of the slave's line (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the review of entry 246's fix; severity P3: an edge-triggered writer of a pty master waited for room it had). `tty_input_char` in `rach2/sys/tty/tty.c` woke no one for room when a flush or an erase emptied part of the line. Reproduce with `tty_hosttest.c`'s row of input that frees room. It breaks `epoll(7)`'s edge for `EPOLLOUT`. It depends on entry 246. Fixed here, cd8f458a: the input wakes the master's queue for room, with the host row, two faults in `tty_mutate.sh`, and epollmusl's row, which the fault tty-input-room-unnamed fails.

273. A TCP segment that a full receive ring took none of woke the connection's readers for data (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the review of entry 246's fix; severity P3: an edge-triggered reader was told of data that did not come, and found `EAGAIN`). `rach2/sys/network/socket/socket.c` woke for data at each segment. Reproduce with `socket_wake_hosttest.c`'s row of a full ring. It breaks `epoll(7)`'s edge for `EPOLLIN`. It depends on entry 246. Fixed here, ed4d97e3: the wake comes only when the ring took bytes, with the host row, which fails without it.

274. A nameless file of the KRFFS root was reclaimed without the root's lock when its last holder was not a descriptor closed by `close`, so an allocation could take its extent while the reclaim slept (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the review of entry 236's fix, and on the other paths by the review of that fix; severity P1: a file made in that sleep had its bytes freed under it). The program's file an address space keeps, a `dup2` over a descriptor, the release at the end of another thread's call, the close-on-exec sweep, a table's release at exit, the core file's descriptor, a FIFO's last close and a failed FIFO open, a file mapping and a descriptor in flight each let the last holder go without `vfs_ns_lock`. Reproduce with `open_hosttest.c`'s rows, in which another process's allocation runs inside the reclaim's sleep. It breaks the KRFFS root's rule that its nodes change under its lock (`rach2/sys/fs/Readme.md`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 8b8bb7eb and 6b12744c: every such release counts the holders and reclaims under the lock through `fd_release_orphan`, and a mapping and a descriptor in flight take the lock before they leave their lists; `open_mutate.sh` and `unix_mutate.sh` compile the faults program-reclaim-unlocked, orphan-release-unlocked, mapping-left-unlocked and flight-left-unlocked, each failing its row.

275. A seek through a description that a read of `/proc`, `/dev/bootlog` or `/dev/screenlog` shared was lost when the read slept in its copy, and the boot log's 32-bit `lseek` wrapped (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the review of entry 241's fix; severity P3: a seek made during a read took no effect, and from an offset near 4 GiB a forward seek landed near 0). Each read set the offset from the value it read before its copy, and `sys_lseek` added the boot log's offset in signed 32-bit arithmetic. Reproduce with `open_hosttest.c`'s rows, in which the copy sleeps while a sharer seeks. It breaks Linux's position lock, which read, write and lseek of such a file hold. It depends on entry 241. Fixed here, 2b0c66a5, b2d1d7bb, 0e977015 and 203c5396: read and write hold the description for these files (`ops_offset_held`, `devfs_fd_moves_offset`), the boot log's seek adds in 64 bits with `EINVAL` past 4294967295, a sum past the largest signed offset among them, and `EOVERFLOW` from the 32-bit call past 2147483647; `open_mutate.sh` and `synth_open_mutate.sh` compile the faults, each failing its row.

276. Three guest rows could pass with their defect present or waited on the clock (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the review of the leftovers' rows; severity P3: a row that proved nothing, or failed on a slow host). Lockmusl judged a lock kept by a parent's own view of its table, and a child it waited on could hold its locks past a failed row; rawprobe held its socket pressure for a fixed time; `sb16_mutate.sh` scaled its notes by the host's speed. Reproduce by reading the rows as they stood. It breaks the rule that a suite judges what the kernel counts, never the clock (`AGENTS.md:53`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, dfc3f5ed, f0e02631, a2012bec and 33a529d6: lockmusl requires a child that saw `EAGAIN` or `EACCES` and releases and reaps every child a failed row leaves, rawprobe starts its checks once both children report their tables full through pipes, and `sb16_mutate.sh` runs under the instruction clock the audio suite has.

277. A polled AHCI command was given one second, and its failure was not logged, so a flush a loaded host took longer over failed the root's mount with EIO (2026-09-28, milestone 54's leftovers, found by the gnu suite of the leftovers' last full set; severity P2: a boot whose first write outlasted a second came up with no root, and a write through the page cache with interrupts off failed the same way). `ahci_run_command` in `rach2/sys/block/ahci.c` bounded its wait by `AHCI_CMD_STEPS`, one hundred thousand polls of ten microseconds, where the queued path waits four thirty-second periods, IDE's polled wait at least five seconds, and Linux thirty; a write here ends with a flush, which QEMU answers by syncing the image to the host's disk, which the full set's other machines were writing to at the same time; and `ahci_polled_failed` returned `EIO` with nothing in the log. Reproduce by running the gnu suite in a full set on a loaded host: its log ends at `ext2: root: /dev/disk0p1 refused` after `bcache: write: sector 0 failed (-5)`, and alone the suite passes. It breaks the block layer's contract that a request waits out its device's deadline (`rach2/sys/block/block.h`), and the Messages rule that a failure says why. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 8e296c77 and 8814a034: a polled command has thirty seconds with interrupts on, where the timer keeps time, and five with them off, the IDE driver's bound, since there each tick of the wait is lost to the clock and to every timer; a command that fails, polled or judged at its completion, is logged with why. `ahci_hosttest.c` has a command that answers after ten seconds of polls, which must complete with interrupts on and be refused with them off, one that answers after a second and a half with them off, which must complete, one that never answers and one that moves too few bytes, each of which must be logged, and each row fails under its fault.

278. `rach2-install` replaced an image's account files with the machine's, so an update to the GNU root would have lost its `sshd` account and left the machine with no SSH server (2026-09-30, milestone 54's machine phase, found while preparing the update of the Lenovo; severity P2: OpenSSH's server does not start without its privilege-separation user, and the machine is then reached only at its console or from recovery). The update copied the old root's `/etc/passwd`, `/etc/group` and `/etc/shadow` over the image's, and an install wrote them afresh with root and the first account, so an account the image has and the machine does not, `sshd` with id 74 on the GNU root, was gone. Reproduce by updating a machine whose root was the musl system to the GNU root and reading its `/etc/passwd`. It breaks `rach2-install(8)`'s promise of a root that runs, and the GNU system's OpenSSH server (`vendor/gnu/Readme.md`). It depends on nothing. Fixed here, fdea51ce and 7892dd24: both paths append the image's system accounts, id below 1000, whose name and id the machine's files lack, with their groups and shadow lines, after a newline where a file's last line has none, comparing ids as numbers and taking each once; `tests/system/install_accounts_test.sh` in the host suite has a row for each case and nine faults, each failing its row. The Lenovo's update kept `sshd:x:74` and its group, and its OpenSSH server started.

279. A GPT disk whose primary header is damaged does not boot by BIOS, though the kernel and the tools read its backup (2026-10-03, milestone 57, found by its GPT work in QEMU; severity P3: the disk still boots by UEFI, from the recovery stick, and from a repaired table). GRUB 2.12's BIOS core, `vendor/grub/gpt-core.img`, reads the partition table through the `part_gpt` module, which refuses a disk whose primary header fails its checksum and does not look for the backup at the disk's last sector, where `mkgpt --list`, `growroot` and the kernel's GPT reader take the backup. Reproduce with `mkgpt --damage-primary` on a copy of an installed disk and a boot under SeaBIOS: GRUB stops at its rescue prompt, and the same disk boots under OVMF, whose firmware reads the backup. It breaks the GPT layout's promise that one disk boots by either firmware (`dev-log/57-arch/plan.md`, `rach2-install(8)`). It depends on the pinned GRUB, which `vendor/grub/regen_grub.sh` makes from a snapshot and does not patch. Fixed here, within that limit, in 06550a24 and ab04a93f: the recovery system names at its boot each disk whose table it read from the backup and the command that writes the primary again, `diskctl relocate`, which now says it did so; UEFI firmware built on EDK2, OVMF among it, writes such a primary again itself when it reads the disk, which `recovery(7)` and `diskctl(8)` say; and the uefi suite's seventh part damages a GPT disk's primary header, boots the stick beside it by SeaBIOS, requires the recovery system's line and the repair, and boots the disk by SeaBIOS after it.

280. `block_physical` took a loop device for a partition view and followed its `used` flag as the device under it (2026-10-03, milestone 57's x86-64 boot, found by mounting ext2 on a loop device under qemu-system-x86_64; severity P1 on x86-64, where the mount panicked, and P3 on i686, where the read landed in the identity-mapped first page and the request was claimed against a device that does not exist). `rach2/sys/block/block.c`'s `block_physical` told a view by `dev == &((block_partition_t *)dev->drvdata)->device`, which holds for any record that begins with its device and is that device's `drvdata`, as `struct loop_device` is, and then read the record's next word, a loop's `used`, as the view's backing. Reproduce with `loopctl attach 0 FILE` and `mount -t ext2 /dev/loop0 DIR` on the x86-64 kernel. It breaks `block.h`'s contract of `block_physical`, the disk under a view, which the request layer claims ranges on. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, c9b8e751: a view is told by its `submit`, `block_partition_submit`, as `block_submitter` tells it; `block_hosttest.c` has a row with a record of a loop device's shape, which the old test fails reading the pointer 1. On x86-64, ext2 and FAT volumes on loop devices mount, are written and read back.

281. The whole-disk node of a disk past 2 TiB stopped at 2^32 sectors, so a GUID table's backup at the disk's end could not be written through it (2026-10-03, milestone 57's 4 TiB install row, found while designing it; severity P2: an install onto a disk past 2 TiB left the backup where the image ended and the root ungrown, `growroot` and `diskctl relocate` failing with `the whole-disk node does not reach the disk's last sector`). `rach2/sys/block/diskinfo.c`'s `diskinfo_window` clamped every ATA, AHCI and NVMe disk's window to `DISKINFO_WINDOW_MAX_SECTORS`, a ceiling chosen when file offsets were 32 bits and kept after they became 64. Reproduce with a sparse 4 TiB NVMe disk and `rach2-install` from the stick. It breaks the plan's promise that a disk of any size takes the install and its root fills it, to ext2's 16 TiB. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, b02adec9: the window is the disk's whole size, `devfs_hosttest` requires a 2^33-sector disk's node to cover every sector, and the uefi suite's sixth part installs onto a 4 TiB disk and boots a root past 2 TiB.

282. An NVMe controller whose I/O completion queue was made before MSI-X was enabled sent none of its messages under QEMU 10.2, so every interrupt-driven transfer stalled until the request layer quarantined the disk (2026-10-03, milestone 57's 4 TiB install row on the testing host; severity P1: the disk was unusable once the boot moved it to its messages). QEMU 10.2's `nvme_init_cq` marks a vector in use only when MSI-X is on as the queue is created, and `msix_notify` drops a message for a vector not in use; QEMU 11.1 on the Mac delivered it, which is why the Mac's runs passed. `rach2/sys/block/nvme.c` made its queues polled at boot and enabled MSI-X in `nvme_start`. Reproduce with the recovery kernel, an NVMe disk and a `dd` to it on QEMU 10.2. It breaks `nvme(4)`'s interrupt-driven transfers. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, 73f225ee: MSI-X is held, enabled with every vector masked, before the queues are made, as Linux orders it (`pci_msix_hold`), `nvme_hosttest` and `pcimsi_hosttest` have rows for the order and the hold, and the 4 TiB row passes on the testing host.

283. The boot log printed a disk's sector count through a 32-bit cast, `disk0: nvme 0: 0 sectors` for a 4 TiB namespace and a negative count past 1 TiB (2026-10-03, milestone 57, the same row; severity P3). `disk.c` and `diskinfo.c` passed `(uint32_t)sectors` to `%d`. It breaks the log's account of the disk. Fixed here, 18ade346: the count is printed through `diskinfo_u64_dec`.

284. The harness kernel, `HARNESS=1`, built on neither architecture: `harness_exit` in `sys/arch/x86/portio.c` had no prototype in scope, an error under `-Werror=missing-prototypes` (2026-10-03, milestone 57, found by the first x86-64 run of the uefi suite on the testing host; severity P2: every suite that boots the harness kernel could not build, since milestone 57's platform split moved the function out of the file that included `kio.h`). Reproduce with `make -C rach2 COMPONENT=rach2-harness HARNESS=1`. Fixed here, 0a508bb1: the file includes `kio.h`, and every kernel variant of both architectures was compiled.

285. The install stick's builder left its two copies of GRUB's EFI image beside the stick when it succeeded (2026-10-03, milestone 57, found preparing the machine's stick; severity P3: a suite that builds a stick then made files no suite owns, which the runner fails). `tools/image/mkinstallstick.sh` removed the volume and the manifest on success and cancelled its trap without removing `$EFI` and `$EFISUM`. Fixed here, b58a9fe8.

286. Neither architecture registered `time` (i386 13, x86-64 201), and glibc on x86-64, which has no vDSO here, makes the call for `time()`, which then failed with `ENOSYS` (2026-10-03, milestone 57's first x86-64 run of the gnu suite; severity P2: OpenSSL could not date a certificate, curl's and openssl's rows failed, GnuPG stopped with `time() failed`, cron ran no job and `uptime -p` printed nothing). Reproduce with `syscall(SYS_time, &t)` from a program. It breaks `syscalls(2)`'s promise of Linux's interface for the architecture. Fixed here, e85cc543: the call returns real time's seconds and stores them, callsmusl's clocks row requires it to agree with `clock_gettime`, and the gnu suite's rows pass on x86-64.

287. `gettimeofday` cast the seconds to 32 bits on x86-64 and refused a null `tv` with `EFAULT`, where Linux answers 0 (2026-10-03, milestone 57, read beside entry 286; severity P3: wrong after 2038 on x86-64, and a program's `gettimeofday(NULL, &tz)` failed). Fixed here in e85cc543.

288. `BLKRAGET` answered an int where Linux answers a long, so x86-64's util-linux `blockdev --getra` read garbage in the upper half (2026-10-03, milestone 57's x86-64 gnu suite, the losetup row: `-4294967040`; severity P3). Fixed here, ca0dce46.

289. `PTRACE_LISTEN` did nothing, and a seized tracee was never told that its process's stop had ended, so strace, which answers its child's group stop with `PTRACE_LISTEN` after sending it `SIGCONT`, waited for good whenever the `SIGCONT` ended the stop first, which two processors make the usual order (2026-10-03, milestone 57's x86-64 gnu suite on two processors, the strace row; severity P2: strace hung on every SMP machine; one processor gave the other order and passed). Reproduce with `strace /bin/true` under `-smp 2`. It breaks `ptrace(2)`'s Linux semantics of `PTRACE_LISTEN` and of `PTRACE_EVENT_STOP` for a seized tracee. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, f9fd79c1: `ptrace_group_change` asks each seized tracee for a new `PTRACE_EVENT_STOP` when its process's stop begins or ends, one held by `PTRACE_LISTEN` leaves its stop at once, a listen made after such a change re-traps at once, and `PTRACE_LISTEN` of any stop but an event stop is `EIO`; ptracemusl's new row forces both orders and passes on both kernels with one and with two processors, and strace's row passes.

290. The gnu suite's dig row asked its Python responder before the responder had bound its port, and dig's five tries, each refused at once, could all be spent first (2026-10-03, milestone 57's x86-64 gnu suite; severity P3: a test's race, which the slower emulation made the usual outcome). Fixed here, 2510b2da: the row waits until the port is in `/proc/net/udp`.

291. Pages of the manual still said what the kernel did not have: `nsenter`, `unshare`, `renice`, `nice`, `rdate`, `rtcwake`, `setarch`, `date`, `mkfifo`, `mknod`, `readahead` and `setfattr` failing for calls the kernel registers, `rach2(7)` that the system does not implement SMP and is a 32-bit system, `rtc(4)` referring to `rtcwake(8)` where the page is `rtcwake(1)`, and the system call pages and `syscalls(2)` describing i386 alone (2026-10-03, milestone 57's documentation phase; severity P3: a reader is told a working program fails). Fixed here in c908667b, 416a2c7b and 20156a64: each entry says what the program does now, `syscalls(2)` has both architectures' interfaces and an x86-64 number table generated from the kernel's, the pages of calls that differ on x86-64 say how, and `mancheck` reads both architectures' tables.

292. `grubembed` refused a core longer than the BIOS boot partition but not one longer than a tail of it, so a GPT disk could boot with a core `bootctl grub` could not later replace (2026-10-03, milestone 57, a helper's note on the GPT core; severity P3). Fixed here, 0dc1bc7f: both tables' rooms are laid out by `grubslot_window`, as the machine lays out its window, and a core whose sectors after the first do not fit in one tail is refused.

293. The initial stack had no `AT_MINSIGSTKSZ`, so a program could not learn that a processor's XSAVE state makes a signal frame larger than `MINSIGSTKSZ`, about 2.7 KB of state with AVX-512 (2026-10-03, milestone 57, a helper's note on XSAVE; severity P3: a frame that does not fit an alternate stack ends the process with `SIGSEGV`, as on Linux, and nothing told the program the size it needed). Fixed here, 1f799f70: `sigframe_stack_bytes` gives the most bytes a frame takes on each architecture, the vector carries it, musl's `sysconf(_SC_MINSIGSTKSZ)` reads it, and `uimage_hosttest` and `auxvprobe` have rows for it.

294. The runner chose the emulator without a word when `/dev/kvm` existed but its user could not open it, and every bound of the longest suites, set under KVM, was then missed (2026-10-04, milestone 57's gnu runs on the testing host; severity P3: a test's environment, no fault of the kernel). The testing host's runs from the evening of 2026-10-03 printed `accelerator tcg` in their first line where milestone 56's had `kvm`; under the emulator GNU sed's `configure` took about 1000 s on both architectures where it takes about 50 s under KVM, so the gnu suite's autotools row (900 s) and chromium row (1200 s) failed on both, which was first read as a slowdown of the SMP kernel. After a restart of WSL, `/dev/kvm` was `root:kvm 0660` and the testing user in `kvm`, and KVM ran again. Reproduce with a `/dev/kvm` of mode 0600 and `tests/run gnu`. It breaks the reading of every time-bound result. Fixed here, 1f3a4ffe: the runner says on stderr that it fell back beside a `/dev/kvm` it cannot open, and that the bounds were set under KVM.

295. Every temporary mapping on i686 changed the kernel's mappings twice, once to map its slot and once to unmap it, so on two processors each page zeroed, copied or filled made the other processor drop all its translations when it next took the big lock (2026-10-04, milestone 57's SMP, found measuring entry 294; severity P3: a slow result). `kmap_temp` and `kunmap_temp` in `rach2/sys/mm/paging.c` each called `paging_flush_tlb` on a kernel address, which counts a change of kernel mappings (`smp_kernel_mapping_changed`), and x86-64, whose direct map reaches every frame, used the same slots. Reproduce by counting `kernel_gen` across a `fork`. It breaks no contract. Fixed here: on x86-64 a temporary mapping is the frame's address in the direct map (5eb95e37); on i686 a released slot stays mapped until no slot is free, and then every released slot is unmapped with one change, once for up to 1024 releases, as Linux's `kmap` pool does (3c34faf2), which `paging_hosttest` proves with five rows and `paging_mutate.sh` with four faults. Measured under KVM with two processors, GNU sed's `configure` three times each: i686 53, 55 and 55 s before, 52, 54 and 54 s after; x86-64 56, 58 and 55 s before, 51, 52 and 52 s after. With them, an idle task or a kernel thread now keeps the last address space loaded, as Linux's lazy TLB mode does (733d7dec): i686 52, 53 and 53 s. Its first form interrupted an idle processor for every shootdown of the directory it kept and cost 58 to 63 s; a shootdown now marks the directory stale there, which `smp_hosttest` and `smp_mutate.sh` prove.

296. After a large write, no pipe could be made and no socket given its ring: the buffer cache took the heap's pages one at a time between everyone else's and gave them back in any order, so the heap's free half lay in holes of a page or two and a 64 KiB pipe buffer, 17 pages, found no run (2026-10-04, milestone 57's rehearsal of the sitting in QEMU; severity P1 at the machine: after a root image was copied onto the stick, every pipeline of the recovery system and of the installer, which is a shell script, failed with ENOMEM, and the log filled with `kheap: 17 pages: no room or no frame`). `rach2/sys/mm/kheap.c` searched the region next fit from where the last run ended for every allocation, the cache's pages among them, and milestone 50's reserve of large runs serves only runs of 64 pages or more. Milestone 56's kernel had the same search and the same cache. Reproduce with the recovery system, a 1 GiB file written onto the stick's exFAT volume, and `sha256sum FILE | cut -c1-8`, which prints nothing. It breaks pipe(2)'s and socket(2)'s promise of memory a machine has. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, f86a5993: the cache's pages come from the top of the region down and every other allocation from the bottom up, the lowest run that fits, as Linux keeps reclaimable pages apart from the rest; `kheap_hosttest` fills the region with cache pages and single pages in turn and requires a run of 17 once the single pages are given back, which the old search fails, and `kheap_mutate.sh` proves it.

297. `rach2-install` asked its question again forever when its standard input ended, printing each refusal, so a run given too few answers never stopped (2026-10-04, milestone 57's rehearsal of the sitting; severity P3: a scripted install hung and filled its log, though nothing was written). Each `read` of the questions went on whatever it read, and at the end of the input it reads nothing, which every question refuses and asks again. Reproduce with `printf '/dev/disk1\n' | rach2-install`. It breaks `rach2-install(8)`'s promise that all questions precede the first write and end in an answer. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, efc9a4d9: the end of the answers before the last question stops the run with `the answers ended before the questions did; nothing was written`, and the installer suite's third row requires it.

298. A large write on the x86-64 Lenovo ran at 25 MB/s where the disk reads 266 MB/s, the time spent in the kernel ordering the blocks it wrote back (2026-10-05, milestone 57's machine; severity P2: a 512 MiB `dd` took 21 s, 10 s of it in one write-back of 65536 dirty blocks, while the disk had requests in flight for 1.3 s). A sync gathered the lowest 128 dirty blocks of the device in each walk of the dirty list by insertion sort; the list has its newest buffer first, so a file written in order lists its blocks highest first, and each one shifted the whole batch; and a run of 256 blocks spent a batch of 128, so the sync walked the list once a run. The cost was quadratic in the dirty blocks, whose number the x86-64 cache, sized from all of memory, made eight times i686's. The shifts were not counted in `cache-visits`, so `bcache_cost_hosttest.c` could not see them. Reproduce on a machine of 16 GiB with `dd if=/dev/zero of=/root/big bs=1M count=512` and `/proc/disk`'s `disk-wait-ticks` before and after. It breaks no stated contract; it made the write-back the slowest step of a large write. It depends on nothing. Fixed here, fed30fc0: a sync gathers the device's dirty blocks in one walk into a batch the heap gives it, up to 256 pages, sorted in a heap, with 128 a walk on its stack when the heap has no room; the heap's steps count as visits, and the cost test bounds the one walk and the batched path, which the insertion sort, a batch of 128 with room for all and a batch of one each fail in `bcache_mutate.sh`. On the machine a write-back of 256 MiB then took 1.3 s, and 1 GiB with fsync ran at 107 MB/s.

299. `ext2_batch_mutate.sh`'s fault, a drain of the write batch after every write, passed: `ext2probe cost` counted no command more under it (2026-10-05, milestone 57's checks; severity P3: a proof that could not fail). Since milestone 56's write-back, `228b1047`, a mount without `sync` writes a drained batch into the cache and issues no command before fsync, so the batch saves nothing the probe counted; a run at the milestone's base, 5349d3df, passed the fault too. Reproduce with `sh tests/system/ext2_batch_mutate.sh` at 5349d3df. It breaks the testing rule that a test is trusted only after it has failed. It depends on milestone 56's write-back. Fixed here, 08661f63 and 9bf728f2: the probe mounts its fixture with `MS_SYNCHRONOUS`, whose ordered write-through is what the batch saves commands on, and the script builds for the architecture `ARCH` names; the fault costs 64 commands and 32 flushes for the rewrites where the batch costs 4 and 2, on both architectures.

## Decisions

### The code is reviewed before the machine is written

The installer, the kernel and the recovery image this milestone's fixes changed are what the sitting writes with, and `CLAUDE.md` puts a destructive write after the review of the code that performs it. So the review of phases 1 and 2 runs in full before the sitting: build from clean, the full set, the Codex review, every finding fixed, the full set again. The fixes made after the sitting are reviewed once, as one batch, at the close.

### The owner is called once, with everything ready

Before the owner is called, the following are done and recorded in `notes.md`:

* The reviewed kernel and images built, their hashes recorded, and the stick image booted in QEMU.
* The save-set tool run against the Lenovo's recorded geometry: its ranges, their total bytes, and the new stick's free space recorded; milestone 49's rehearsal run again against that manifest.
* The restore commands written out twice, for `rach2-recover` on the new stick and for `reflash` on milestone 43's stick.
* The third stick's layout decided, one exFAT volume labelled `RACH2TEST` over the whole stick, written in the sitting's first step; the pull test's read command, a `dd` of the whole stick to nowhere over SSH; its readiness signal, the first 64 MiB reported by the host; and its deadline, the removal reported by the kernel within one minute of the pull.
* Every answer the installer asks for prepared: the account name and the key in `notes.md`, the passwords in the credential file on the host that the acceptance reads.
* The sitting's commands in order, with what each shows, what the owner is asked, the observable condition for each physical action, and which boot switch answers which symptom.

Nothing in the sitting is discovered on the day. A step that turns out to need something not prepared stops the sitting with the disk untouched or restored, and the owner is called again only after the gap is closed.

### The install replaces the grow

Milestone 47 proved `growroot` in QEMU. The Lenovo does not get its root grown in place; the installer writes the sealed image and grows the ext2 root into the free space of the disk, as milestone 49 decided. The saved set is what the installer writes: the image's extent and the growth ranges from the tool. The rest of the disk is not written and is not saved.

### A known-working recovery path stays outside the change

Milestone 43's stick is kept as it is. If the new stick fails to boot, or fails after the disk has been written, the older stick boots and its `reflash` puts the saved set back. Its boot-time repair may write into a damaged root before the restore runs; the restore rewrites every sector of the set, and the sectors outside the set were unused before the install, so the repair changes nothing the restore does not overwrite. Milestone 49's rehearsal proves this path with a recovery ISO built at the commit milestone 43 installed. The restore through milestone 43's own stick was never rehearsed; the sandbox rule of 2026-09-24 retired the saves and restores this path served. The new stick is a second stick, and the acceptance and the pull test use a third stick that carries nothing; milestone 43's stick is not written at all, and the new stick's boot image is not written after its preparation; the save goes to the new stick's data volume.

### The pull test is the removal half of entry 1

This test setup cannot make a physical stick stop answering on demand. On the Lenovo the third stick is read over SSH from the host, and while the read runs the owner pulls it when the host says so; the kernel must report the removal, keep running, and take the stick back when it is plugged in again. The never-answering half is proved by entry 1 in the storage driver's host model, since QEMU cannot make a stick stop answering its class reset. The owner ran the pull on 2026-09-25 on kernel `4ae77083`: with `dd` reading the development stick, the kernel logged the port gone, failed the request in flight with its slot given back and reclaimed `disk1`, and the read ended with an I/O error; the stick, put back, was found again four seconds after the pull and was `disk1` again with its two partitions a second later, and its exFAT volume mounted read-only with its three files. A first pull came after the first read of the whole stick had finished and before the second was started, and showed the removal and the return with no read in flight.

### What the owner does

At the desk, at the start: approves the write of the new stick and the formatting of the third, naming each as the host reports it, and plugs both into the notebook. At the notebook, at the start: powers it on, plugs in the keyboard, mouse, hub, card and the third stick as milestone 43 had them, and chooses the new stick as the boot device when asked. At the desk: approves each later write by naming the device the installer or the host reports, including the change of the saved boot default. At the notebook, once: types the target disk's name into the installer and the prepared answers. At the notebook, once more: pulls the third stick when the host says so and plugs it back. At the notebook, last: moves the keyboard and then the mouse through each of the three sockets and the hub, typing a line and moving the pointer at each, with the host reading what arrived. The owner's part ends before the load runs.

## Phases

### Phase 1: the fixes

The entries recorded so far, in order of severity, each with the smallest suite that covers it and a mutation for any new check.

### Phase 2: the review before the machine

Once and in this order: build from clean; the full set; everything that comes up fixed; the whole milestone's diff so far to Codex in units of six files, most serious first; every finding fixed; the full set again. Then the preparation the decision above lists, ending with the rehearsal in QEMU and the sitting's commands in `notes.md`. Done on 2026-09-10, as `notes.md` records; the sets it produced passed 84 and 81 of 88 in the pool with the rest alone, which the owner judged, with the record of the week, not the reliable runner this milestone owes, and widened it on 2026-09-11 with the phases below, deliberated with Codex.

### How phases 2b to 2h run

Each phase in this order: the facts and the code are gathered here and the change is designed; Codex reads the design and the files it touches before anything is run and says what is wrong with it, as the Agents section allows, one unit of at most six files and ten minutes; the code is written with what Codex found; the smallest suite that covers the change runs once, and the phase's new check is proved by its mutation once; a failure is read before anything runs again, and only the failed suite runs again, never a set; at the phase's end Codex reviews the phase's diff once, the findings are fixed, and the phase is committed. Every run over five minutes has its stages and their expected times written down before it starts, the first stage's rate read as it runs, and a stage at half its expected rate stopped and explained. Nothing edits or builds the tree while a run reads it. The full set runs once, at the end of phase 2f, on fixed artifacts, and once more at the end of 2g; a set is never rerun to get a better number, and a suite that fails in a set is read, then run alone once with its cause named. Six files and ten minutes bound a review unit, not the work: every contract a change touches is covered, and a fix gets the proof it needs whatever the unit's size. In phase 2h a run alone after a failure is diagnosis and counts for nothing; acceptance is phase 2h's item 4.

### Phase 2b: the runner's ownership

Entry 28: private paths, artifacts prepared once, process groups tracked and reaped, abandoned resources recovered, results bound to their inputs' hashes, each suite's result printed as it ends. First, since every measurement after it depends on a run that owns what it uses. Proved by the runner's own rows with a mutation each.

### Phase 2c: the console under load

Entry 29: the receive path measured, then made independent of the shell's scheduling, proved by checksummed input under load with the service disabled as the mutation; `musl/pwauthmusl`'s pseudo-terminal failure and the monitor's silence reproduced apart.

### Phase 2d: waits, not the clock

Entry 30: the harness's `wait=` ended by its token, the suites' sleeps replaced by bounded waits on fresh output where a condition is what they wait for, kept where an interval is the assertion, one type-ahead row kept; each changed stage timed before and after.

### Phase 2e: the storage proofs

Entry 32, before any further storage change: the pending-and-durable device model, the cache's two interleavings, the ext2 publication check, the operation-count rows, the queue's deadline cases, the isolated propagation row.

### Phase 2f: the set's shape

Entry 31: the network scenarios run once where the driver does not matter, the installer suites sharing what is immutable, the lone group tried again under measured load, the pool's width chosen by measurement. Then the full set, once, on fixed artifacts, and its result is the milestone's, not a set plus reruns.

### Phase 2g: the kernel's remaining costs

Entries 33, 34 and 35, and the dispositions the record owes: entry 10 superseded by 25; entry 16's cause proved by its mutation or written as inferred; entry 26 reopened as an experiment with controlled buffer placement, the emulator's page identified, every timed result kept, the pipe row's outliers visible; entry 11 kept as a restriction, called one, with its refusal proved through controlled IDENTIFY data. The three experiments of 34 kept only where a row shows the gain.

Run on 2026-09-10 and 2026-09-11: entries 35, 36, 11, 10 and 16 done, 34's second and third costs done, and 33, 34's first cost and 26's experiment left undone by the session's own decision, against this milestone's rule that nothing is deferred. The two full sets of those days, at twenty and at twelve, each failed four suites. Phase 2h takes what was left.

### Phase 2h: the leftovers, and a set that passes

Nothing in this phase is deferred, narrowed or dropped without the owner's word, asked for in the message where the question comes up with the item's cost and its value stated; "if it fits" appears nowhere in it. The work is ordered by its value to a fast and stable set, each item designed, read by Codex, written with the findings, proved by its rows and their mutations, and reviewed by Codex at its end, as the section above says.

1. Entry 33, the USB request size, first: the path under the three longest suites and under the sitting's save and restore, and the largest measured cost, though not the only one, since the records also show the guest builds, the scheduling and the pipe. The requirements are Codex's review of 2026-09-11 and its reading of this plan: the controller's ownership of a request's pages is held until the controller has acknowledged the endpoint stopped or the slot disabled, so that no descriptor can name them on a restart; a command issued, a software timeout or a class reset alone does not release them, and a disable that fails keeps the pages with the tombstone. The data stage is a chain of Normal TRBs, one per page, with the TD size field as the specification gives it, the chain bit kept across a Link TRB, ISP on the intermediate IN TRBs and IOC on the last, and the event's TRB pointer kept; a short transfer and a halted endpoint retire the TD differently, the short by skipping the TRBs left and the halt by the recovery that moves the dequeue pointer, the first short or failed outcome is kept whatever events follow, the byte count is exact, and one completion is delivered. `max_sectors` is `BLOCK_REQ_MAX_SECTORS`. The rows, in the host test's controller model where a QEMU device cannot drive them: patterned reads and writes through pages whose physical order is permuted inside one contiguous virtual buffer, checked page by page, with a first and a last partial page; a chain across the ring's Link TRB; an intermediate short and an intermediate failure with the exact bytes and status expected, and a following request that succeeds; a stop or a disable that is delayed and one that fails, a completion that arrives late, and the reuse of the slot and the request, requiring the pages held while unsafe and released when safe; the stick pulled during a 32 KiB request in the usb suite; and in `diskrate` a bound of one data command per full 32 KiB request with the stick's read and write rates recorded, in QEMU and at the sitting. Each row has its own mutation: the contiguous address for the mapping, the pages released at the timeout for the ownership, the last IOC waited for on a halt for the retirement, the chain bit dropped at the Link for the ring.

2. Entry 2's rule: a deadline that bounds a suite's completion is set from a measured rate of the emulator under the load the set makes, not from an idle host. The runner calibrates once at a set's start with fixed work of both kinds, computing and storage, timed by the host's clock at the width the pool will run at, and records the reference, the loaded measurement, the width and the factor; one factor is used only if it is a conservative bound for both kinds, else two. The runner passes the mode and the factor to every suite in named variables, pooled or alone, the suites alone inside `all` included; a suite told nothing runs its numbers as written. Only the deadlines that bound completion are scaled, outer and nested alike; a guest protocol deadline, a latency assertion, a sustained-load interval and a deliberate timeout row keep their numbers, and the inventory of which is which is written before the change. A rate outside the envelope the calibration allows is a named failure of the set, not a longer deadline. The mutations: a producer delayed by a known amount passes under the scaling and fails at the named bound with the scaling bypassed or a faster rate claimed; a completion suppressed shows the scaled deadline finite. The owner replaced this rule on 2026-09-15: `tests/run` no longer calibrates, and entry 2 records what took its place.

3. Entry 26's experiment, then entry 34's first cost. A probe reads a pipe into a buffer verified to share a page with its own code and into one verified to lie entirely outside every code page, the mappings identified and printed, the same work for both, each run timed and every time kept; `diskrate` holds every far-buffer run to the rate bound and prints the pipe row's three times. If the placement does not reproduce the slow runs, entry 26's cause is open again and its explanation is not used in its place. After that the pipe's copies: the reader and the writer copy straight from and into the ring's spans, the reader woken on every empty-to-nonempty transition and the writer once per read, a partial copy reporting the bytes moved, with wrap, EOF, non-blocking, signals and the TinyCC-built kernel proved as entry 34 lists them; an implemented and proved change may be taken back when its row shows no gain, and the experiment may not be skipped.

4. Acceptance. Two consecutive complete `tests/run all` invocations on one tree, the same source and the same prepared artifacts by hash, at a width and a lone group frozen and written down before the first, each passing every suite in its group with no suite skipped, no run alone in a set's place, no retry and no result assembled across invocations; the suite inventory, the width, the groups, the deadline policy and the calibration's numbers are recorded with them. A failure is read and its cause fixed as this plan says, with the regression rows of entries 37 and 38 and of the menu and mount rows kept; a run alone after a failure is diagnosis and counts for nothing; after the fix, both sets start again, and any change to the tree between the two voids the pair. Then the rehearsal at the machine's size, since entry 33 changes the path it uses, and a fix the rehearsal produces sends the pair of sets again before the sitting. Two passing sets show the set repeats under that configuration, which is what the sitting needs. The machine follows.

### Phase 3: the sitting

One sitting, in this order, each step with a durable state before the next, each timed as it runs, the boot state recorded after each transition:

1. The new stick written on the host from the proved image and the third stick formatted, the owner naming each, the new stick hashed back and both identities recorded.
2. Power on, the devices and the sticks plugged in, the netconsole capture started on the host, the machine on the network.
3. The new stick booted on the Lenovo, its recovery menu shown to work, `rach2-recover` listing the disks with the Lenovo's disk shown as the rule describes, and the memory map from the netconsole capture saved for milestone 46's fixture.
4. The save: the owner names the disk; every sector of the save set copied to the host and to the new stick; the stick's copy hashed back against the host's. A failed hash ends the sitting here, with the disk untouched.
5. Recovery made the saved boot default, with the owner's approval, and the change read back.
6. The install: `rach2-install` on the Lenovo's disk, the owner naming it and giving the prepared answers; the readback against the manifest hash; the trial boot of the candidate with recovery still the saved default. An install that fails is restored from the new stick with the written commands, and the sitting ends.
7. From the running candidate: the root's identity, its writable mount, its grown capacity and the installed credentials checked; the acceptance with those credentials and the third stick; only then `bootctl commit`.
8. The devices: the metal acceptance on the installed system; the reboot from SSH; the pull test on the third stick; the keyboard and the mouse at each socket and through the hub, the host reading each line typed and each pointer movement from the console over SSH.

The owner's part ends here. Then, unattended: twenty sessions running milestone 46's load row for an hour; the numbers into `hardware.md` and the Readme's Limits section; the sitting's times, including the stick's sustained read during the save and the install against milestone 49's ten-minute budget. What the machine shows becomes entries. The notebook may be turned off after the load; nothing later in this milestone needs it.

### Phase 4: the machine's entries

The entries phase 3 added, worked as phase 1 worked its own.

### Phase 5: the close

Once and in this order: build from clean; the full set; the diff since phase 2 to Codex in units of six files, most serious first; every finding fixed; the full set again. Record the results, the sitting's log with its times and boot states, and the numbers in `notes.md` and the milestone's Readme. Add the index entry.

### Phase 6: the second sitting's entries

Entries 48 to 54, each with the solution it is to be given and what remains of it. The machine is installed and answers SSH, so each is proved on it as well as in the emulator. The work below is what milestone 50 still owes.

* **48, the chown rule. Done.** Both chowns on a mounted file system go through `vfs_chown_plan` (`db580d84`, `40b6f436`). The proof the entry promised is in `modeprobe`: it now runs over both dispatch paths, and the descriptor path had no row at all before. The rows cover a directory keeping its bits, a setgid file with and without group-execute, a privileged change, a permitted group change by an unprivileged owner, a refused change with its mode and times unmoved, and both spellings of a request that changes nothing, with the times checked on the no-op as well as the mode. The probe empties its own supplementary group list when it starts as root, since a login shell leaves the primary group in that list and a child that keeps root's group 0 is in the group of a file owned 1000:0, which is the hole the escalation row exists to catch; that is how the probe passed under the test runner and failed on a root login. It takes the directory to work in as an argument, so the same rows run where they are pointed. `tests/run default` runs it on the KRFFS boot root, where the KRFFS paths answer, and `tests/run ext2` now runs it on an ext2 root, where the shared plan answers; the probe and the helper it execs are put into that volume with `debugfs`, since neither is in the shipped image. Proved by mutation, both against `tests/run ext2`: the descriptor path with the plan's mode result masked off fails `an fchown kept setuid (mode 04755 not 0755)`, and the plan without the rule that only a privileged caller changes the owner fails `give a file away as its unprivileged owner (got 0)`; the source restored, both pass.

* **52, the frame copied a byte at a time. Done.** Both card drivers move the frame with `k_memcpy` and pad with `k_memset` (`ac48f68b`), and TCP's four ring copies do the same through helpers that split at the ring's wrap (`1d9c384e`). Measured on the machine: TCP out 117 MB/s and in 116, UDP out 119, against 57 and 69 before. The checksum's word-at-a-time form (`f55cae53`) is in and worth 4 percent.

* **54, a poller with a deadline. The change is kept and its claim is corrected.** `poll.c` halts while still on its wait queues (`7d94137d`), which is the order a reader expects: a waker must be able to find the process it is waking. The row the entry asked for could not be built, and the reason is in the code. On one processor no waker can run while the poller is halted except an interrupt handler, and the interrupt that carries the wake is the one that ends the halt, after which the loop rescans and finds the data; a process waker cannot run at all until the halt ends. So the old order loses no wake that the following scan does not find. Measured: with the old order compiled back in, `pollprobe` ran two hundred poll round trips over a pair of pipes in under 600 ms, where one tick a round trip would be two seconds. The figure this entry carried, a receiver whose `select` has a timeout reading 111.8 MB/s where it read 6.4, is withdrawn: 6.4 MB/s is one 64 KiB receive window a tick, which is entry 55's card fault, and that fault was present on the machine that night. The third close built distinct artifacts and collected transfer logs, but those logs omit running-kernel identity. The two reported groups took 2.26 to 2.29 s and 2.27 to 2.30 s inbound, and 1.33 to 1.37 s and 1.33 to 1.36 s on loopback, all in delivered guest ticks that can be lost with interrupts masked. Program hashes and successful byte counts do not identify a booted kernel. The claimed completion of the poll comparison is withdrawn in unit D; no throughput advantage or equivalence is concluded. The source analysis above supports retaining the clearer order. No new comparison is added to the sitting.

* **49, the cost of a small write. The taken half is proved and the batch has its design record.** Storing the inode once a call (`7d94137d`) is proved by two rows in `tests/run ext2`: fifty rewrites of 4 KiB must cost no more than five disk commands a write and take 204, and fifty appends no more than eleven and take 494. Proved by mutation: with the second store compiled back in the rewrites take 304 and fail their row, and with that row's bound lifted so the append row is reached the appends take 594 and fail theirs; the source restored, both pass at 204 and 494. The interruption rows that replay pointer publication run against this change in `tests/run host`, which the close runs. The batch that spans calls was not taken in this milestone, as this entry recorded; it was taken on 2026-09-21 in `aecb6f1b`, which entry 49 records. What phase 6 owed for it is in the tree: `dev-log/50-shortcomings/ext2-write-batch.md` settles the shape, the ownership of a pending inode, the difference between a visible and a durable size, the scope and locking, the bounds and what happens when admission fails, which close is meant, how an error survives close and reopen, and the seven obligations with the host rows that would discharge each. It records four places where the code contradicts what the plan assumed: the cache contract lives in `vfs.Readme.md` and its line about a new pointer block's zeros is stale, ext2 stores no access time on a read and an executable mapping keeps no file backing, `O_APPEND` picks its end-of-file before the mount lock is taken, and the host write helper releases after every call so the existing stubs cannot prove concurrency or media order.

* **50, the loopback ring. The rows are in, in the form the code allows, and so is the machine's measurement.** The ring holds 48 frames and counts its refusals with one log line (`07ae85c0`), and it is drained at a system call's exit as well as on the tick, once it holds more than a pair, a pair being left to the timer because a simultaneous open is two crossing SYNs and is only a pair while neither has been delivered. A bare acknowledgement the ring refuses is owed on the connection and sent fresh by the tick, while a SYN or a FIN keeps its own retransmission. The four rows the entry asked for, forcing the ring full for an acknowledgement, a window reopening, the handshake and the close, cannot be written from user space, and the reason is the design: one system call can put at most a window's worth into the ring, 65535 bytes in segments of 1460, which is 45 of the 48 slots, and every call's exit drains the ring before the next call runs, so no second call can add to a full one; a delivery that makes a reply during a drain enqueues one frame for each it retires, so the occupancy does not climb. What the entry can have instead is the headroom measured rather than assumed: `/proc/stat` now publishes `loopback_peak`, the most the ring has ever held at once, and `loopprobe`'s bulk row moves 256 KiB in window-sized writes, checks every byte, and requires the ring to refuse nothing, to stay under its last slot and to have been pressed at all. Measured: the peak is 37 of 48, which is the room the ring has over a window. Proved by mutation: the ring back to the eight slots it had before this entry fails the row with `the loopback ring refused a datagram during the transfer`; restored, it passes at 37. The probe's own stream accounting was wrong and was fixed on the way: after a short write it began again at the start of its buffer, which sends bytes the reader is not expecting and would have called the kernel wrong for it. What is left is the machine: a transfer large enough to be worth timing, with the boot log still holding its first line afterwards. The machine's measurement is in: 256 MiB through the loopback in one direction, timed on the installed system, and the boot log still holds its first line afterwards, `boot: Rach2 Booting`, with no loopback line in it at all. That transfer also found entry 57.

* **51, the probes and the root they judge. Done, and the hang was two other things.** Each probe that encoded the boot root's answers now asks the kernel instead. `fdstale` does not hang: on an ext2 root it stopped at its scope case, where it required `rmdir` to take a directory out from under an open descriptor. ext2 refuses that with `EBUSY`, which its Readme states and POSIX permits, so the probe now reads the root's type and asserts the refusal, that the descriptor still reads, and that the close is what releases the name; on the boot root it asserts the removal and the `EBADF` that follows, as before. What looked like a hang on the machine was the probe's leak loops: 256 rounds of a file filled with sixteen writes of 4096 bytes, unlinked and closed, which is 16 MiB in 4 KiB calls and tens of seconds on a disk where such a call is four commands and two flushes. Running it a second time took the machine off the network, which is entry 56. `tests/run default` passes with the change, 71 of 71 probes. The whole probe set was run on the machine at the close, which is what the entry still owed, and it found four more of the same kind. `loopprobe` carried the emulator's address, 10.0.2.15, as the one every loopback peer must report; it now asks the kernel for the machine's own through `getsockname` on a socket connected to 127.0.0.1, which is the rule the rows judge, and it passes on the machine. `jobprobe`, `procprobe`, `suidprobe` and `sudoprobe` cannot pass anywhere but the test image and now say so in a line at the top: job control needs a shell that gives each command its own process group, the `/proc/net/tcp` and pool rows need the machine to itself, and the two set-id probes need fixtures the shipped image does not carry. Of the twenty probes run there, `modeprobe` passes on the ext2 root, which is entry 48's machine proof, and `vfsprobe` was run against its own stated need for a KRFFS root, which was my error and not the probe's.

* **53, the port's alignment test. The patch and its proof are in; what the patch is worth is not what the entry claimed.** `tests/host/e2fsprogs_alignment_hosttest.c` and its 64-bit twin compile the port's own `unix_io.c` with every data call and seek intercepted, and judge a misaligned buffer over reads and writes, a large aligned request, a partial sector preserved, a short transfer retried and then reported, and an error reported rather than swallowed. Proved by mutation through `tests/host/e2fsprogs_alignment_mutate.sh`, which restores the port's source from a trap: patched, 64 checks pass; with the guard put back to the address test, 28 misaligned checks fail; restored, 64 pass and the source matches byte for byte. The controlled grow is named and reproduced: the recovery system on a 16 GiB disk holding a 1 GiB ext2 volume at sector 2048, booted by `build/tests/system/harness` on a qcow2 overlay, running `growroot MBRID=47470001,1`, which rewrites the table entry, runs `e2fsck -f` and then `resize2fs` over `/dev/disk0p1`, a raw window. Measured with `/proc/disk` read either side of that command: 9595 disk commands and 8 seconds with the port as it stands, and 9595 commands and 9 seconds with the alignment test put back. They are the same. A grow on the machine tells the same story: a file-backed volume grown there costs 32 commands at 16 MiB and under a second, and the two binaries differ by nothing that is not noise. So the patch is right about what `unix_io.c` does, which the host test proves, and the figure this entry carried, a full-size grow's 477659 write calls turned into 9346, is not reproduced by either measurement and is withdrawn. Entry 58 required the separate physical phase measurements. Those are now recorded from the recovery sitting on 2026-09-16: the grow totals 14.75 guest-tick seconds, including 14.54 for resize.
